# Rust SSH ownership: S0 qualification ledger

**Status: S0 complete for contracts and local baselines by explicit approval. Release-artifact and runner rows are deferred to Pre-cutover qualification, not passed.** Companion to the [implementation plan](2026-11-22-rust-ssh-ownership-plan.md), not a replacement for its decisions. Source baseline: `dev`, `9102a636119b933bd318e7961de3f503254ba9fb`. No production behavior or downstream transfer scope changes.

**Evidence key:** **Static** means read from the named source or followed through its callers; a checked-in test name is a fixture seed, not a result. **Measured** requires a run with artifact/profile identity, OS/arch, resolved OpenSSH image and version, and a redacted result. **Open** means the stated implementation or parity evidence is still missing. **Deferred — unverified** identifies required release evidence moved from S0 to Pre-cutover qualification. Previously recorded local runs retain only their stated scope; no new runtime runs were performed in this documentation pass. Owner names below are accountable areas for follow-up, not sign-offs.

## S3 selected-development exit checklist

S1 and S2 are closed only for the approved macOS/Linux development scope. **S3 is not accepted.** The table maps each plan row to the selected app as inspected; isolated evidence does not mean the adapter is selected by product callers. A temporary fail-closed refusal is not authorization to remove existing support at cutover. New evidence below supersedes older entries only for the exact case tested.

Cross-cutting exit remains open: classify all launch edges (including delegated Git), verify end-to-end byte bounds and partial outcomes, prove independent cancellation under load, and exercise owner-wide budgets with reserved interactive capacity and a distinct quota outcome. Release/deployed parity, signed rollback, Windows, port 22 and owner-death retirement remain named separate gates; automatic retirement remains disabled.

- **C04 / authenticated watch:** The [live WebSocket proof](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.websocketWorkspaceWatch.test.ts) used stock macOS OpenSSH and a real authenticated router. Two independent clients received the initial watch event, one was interrupted, and the other received a file-change invalidation, a no-dial rescan on Disconnect, and a fresh invalidation after explicit Connect. One owner started; the legacy launch recorder stayed empty. The watch now passes cancellation to owner queue admission; a [focused test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevPicker.workspaceCancellation.test.ts) verifies an aborted queued snapshot cannot dispatch or close an active request. The focused watcher run passed 24 tests across five files; explicit formatting/lint/length checks passed. Rust snapshot cancellation after dispatch is still open.
- **C05 / native bounded write outcome:** [Native write acknowledgements](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/workspace/write.rs) now require an exact bounded marker and matching exit status for a definitive failure. A failure after attempted mutation without confirmation is unknown and not retried. The scoped run passed 44 Rust workspace tests plus one live disposable macOS lifecycle test, strict Clippy, format and lengths. The recorder proved zero launches over 48 KiB and exactly one launch per accepted request when commit acknowledgement was withheld. This remains an isolated overwrite, not selected app write parity; the editor supports up to 512 KiB UTF-8 and an expected SHA-256.

- **G1 / selected-development launch fence:** The [initiating Git classifier](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitCore.initiatingFence.ts) refuses unported transport verbs and unknown argv in the selected dev domain. Explicit fetch/pull/push, both PR fetch paths and live `gh` are refused before launch; initiating local status skips background remote refresh. Ordinary local Git and the explicit typed push proof retain their paths. The [Git regression](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitCore.initiatingFence.test.ts) and [gh regression](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitHubCli.initiatingFence.test.ts) are part of a 24-test, seven-file focused run. Explicit formatting, lint, length checks and server typecheck passed. This fences known product commands; it is not an OS sandbox for arbitrary repository hooks, filters, or tools, and does not port general G1 transport or close release parity.

- **C12 / fresh selected reservation admission:** The [selected development gate](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-backend/workspaceMode.gate.ts) skips legacy positive cache and lazy-error bypass for SSH, and requires fresh owner admission for the exact fixture root even when its reservation is already held. Unsupported selected profiles fail closed. The [layer tests](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-backend/workspaceMode.gate.dev.test.ts) used the real picker, a controlled native port, and disposable local records; Disconnect blocked the next lazy turn and Connect permitted fresh admission without changing the record. Wrong scope, owner loss, unsupported stores/profiles and zero legacy calls were checked. The six-file focused run passed 54 tests plus format, lint, lengths and server typecheck. This is not live SSH or native remote publication; production/deployment parity remains open.

- **C05 / selected Files search:** The [selected workspace router](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerDev.workspace.ts) now routes name and literal content searches for the exact fixture target/root through existing typed native verbs. The selected name index never branches to legacy SSH; name ranking, ignore filtering and truncation reuse the existing workspace policies. The [authenticated live WebSocket case](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.websocketWorkspaceWatch.test.ts) found an actual file by name and content over stock macOS OpenSSH, then refused a search after Disconnect without another dial or legacy launch. The [controlled router cases](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerDev.workspace.test.ts) cover wrong-target/root refusal, index filtering, and bounded search responses. Focused Vitest passed 27 tests in four files; explicit formatting, lint, length and server typecheck passed. This does not port writes or the other C05 bridges, or qualify in-flight native search cancellation or other platform profiles.

- **C07 / real manager-driven Restart:** The [disposable macOS manager proof](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevTerminal.managerRestart.test.ts) runs the real manager, the same selected adapter across replacement, the Rust owner, and stock OpenSSH. The [adapter](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.terminalAdapter.ts) and [dispatch helper](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.terminalAdapter.dispatch.ts) now admit explicit replacement after confirmed exit without allowing delayed old Open to consume the new grant. The test positively observed the old exit receipt, reaped PID, and `terminal` row from an independent SQLite connection before replacement preparation; the new lease/PID/generation returned interactive output and stale old Close did not reach it. An injected pre-dispatch close failure retained the live reservation and prevented a new spawn, prepare or open. Ordinary Open and attach-only recovery could not resurrect the closed pane. The eight-file focused run passed 26 tests, formatting, lint, lengths, and server typecheck. This is not Electron, other platform, detached idle or backend-loss survival evidence.

- **C04 / native in-flight snapshot cancellation fixture:** A macOS-only opt-in now uses the private FD3 inbox for the exact active 16-byte snapshot request ID. The [process test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/workspace/snapshot.tests.rs) verified that cancellation after child start discards partial output and reaps the exact local child. The [owner isolation test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/rebind_tests.rs) verified that a late ID does not cancel a different request and leaves owner epoch/generation unchanged. Focused Rust snapshot, cancellation, FD3 and rebind tests, formatting, length, type checking and scoped Clippy passed. The selected WebSocket watch does not yet wire this opt-in or the native in-flight request signal; no shared-owner close or remote command termination is claimed.

- **C04 / WebSocket unsubscribe to in-flight native cancellation:** The watch now passes its abort signal through the [selected workspace router](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerDev.workspace.ts) and [workspace client](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.client.workspace.ts). When the private FD3 capability exists, the client sends the exact dispatched snapshot request ID and retains the queue until its correlated response. The [live authenticated macOS test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.websocketWorkspaceCancel.test.ts) held one stock OpenSSH authentication, unsubscribed, saw the exact local child disappear before gate release and received `listCancelled`. Another subscriber completed on the same native owner, with two dials, one authentication and no legacy launch. Four focused files passed 12 tests; formatting, lint, lengths and server typecheck passed. This is the dedicated snapshot-only native fixture installed behind the real WebSocket router; the normal dev runtime does not opt into FD3 for snapshots, and no remote command termination is inferred.

- **C07 / exact terminal hard lifetime:** The [native expiry worker](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_terminal/expiry.rs) now receives the monotonic deadline set before spawn rather than starting a new 75-second proof or 30-minute dev timer whenever its thread is scheduled. A [two-PTY regression](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_terminal/tests.rs) passed: a late watchdog reaped only its exact retained child and another PTY remained interactive. Fourteen focused native tests, binary check, explicit formatting/length and strict scoped Clippy passed. This is a hard lifetime cap, not detached idle expiry, stock OpenSSH or Electron evidence. For idle expiry, the SSH owner still needs fresh visibility and remote foreground activity evidence tied to the exact lease; unknown must remain busy. Existing [remote inspection](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal/Layers/Manager.remote-inspection.ts) and [typed view state](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/remote-agent/pty_lifecycle.proto) are references, not authority to open a new unmanaged probe after backend loss.

- **C05 / selected-development readiness and session-FS fence:** [Readiness](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-workspace-bridge/remoteWorkspaceReadiness.ts) and the [session-FS bridge](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-workspace-bridge/remoteWorkspaceSessionFsBridge.ts) now fail closed with a typed unsupported error for selected remote targets, including direct SSH and managed-agent targets. The bridge refuses before a provider-supplied probe or allocation, so Copilot and ACP cannot take this path to legacy transport. Local and non-selected behavior remain unchanged. Twenty tests across four focused files passed, along with formatting, lint, lengths, whitespace and server typecheck. This is a development fence, not permanent support removal or native readiness/session-FS parity; the typed OS/architecture probe and full contract remain open.

- **C05/C12 / typed selected workspace readiness:** The selected [workspace reservation gate](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-backend/workspaceMode.gate.ts) now calls a [dedicated native readiness verb](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/workspace/readiness.rs) rather than the terminal cwd probe. It accepts only the scoped root and returns allowlisted OS and architecture values from a fixed, 10-second, 4 KiB probe; neither shell text nor deployment authority crosses the wire. The [live macOS test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.workspaceReadiness.test.ts) used stock OpenSSH and SQLite, observed `darwin`/`arm64`, and verified no new dial on wrong root or Disconnect. The [controlled selected gate tests](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-backend/workspaceMode.gate.dev.test.ts) cover current admission and failed native readiness without reservation publication. Thirteen focused server tests, 58 focused native tests, four wire/grant checks, strict scoped Clippy, formatting, lint, lengths and server typecheck passed; Linux and Windows were cross-checked, not run for this verb. Generic provider readiness without a bound capability and the Copilot/ACP session-FS bridge remain fenced: that bridge still needs typed filesystem operations and must not regain a generic shell path.

- **C05 / selected bounded overwrite:** The [selected workspace router](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerDev.workspace.ts) now dispatches an unguarded overwrite for the exact development fixture target/root through the typed Rust write verb. The shared wire limit is 48 KiB of UTF-8; oversize, guarded, create-only, and wrong-scope requests refuse before owner acquisition. Confirmed native failures return a terminal disposition; unconfirmed post-admission outcomes are unknown and never retried. The [authenticated macOS WebSocket case](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.websocketWorkspaceWatch.test.ts) now creates fixture contents through the write RPC, observes their exact bytes and watch invalidation, and confirms that a write after Disconnect is not dispatched and neither changes bytes nor dials. The [router tests](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerDev.workspace.test.ts) check the exact UTF-8 boundary, settlement and zero legacy fallback. Eighteen focused tests, formatting, lint, lengths and server typecheck passed. This does not enable editor hash-guarded or larger writes, create-only semantics, session-FS mutations or other configuration profiles.

- **C07 / exact native terminal owner loss:** The [selected lifecycle adapter](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal-host/localTerminalHost.directSsh.owner.lifecycle.ts) retains the exact durable reservation as `outcome-unknown` and reports `host-lost` when the bound native owner is unavailable. The [macOS live crash proof](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevTerminal.ownerCrash.test.ts) confirmed a live OpenSSH lease, killed only the fixture-owned Rust owner, and waited for it to exit before checking a warm and a fresh manager on the same SQLite DB. The target, epoch, generation, lease and reservation ID stayed fixed; no replacement owner, SSH dial, native prepare/open or legacy launch occurred. The [policy regression](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal-host/localTerminalHost.directSsh.owner.policy.test.ts) verifies a delayed loss report cannot mark a replacement reservation. The 12-file run passed 56 tests; the tightened crash/policy files passed all seven again. Scoped format, lint, lengths and server typecheck passed. No full backend-process restart or Electron was exercised, and no inference is made about remote shell exit. Automatic retirement remains disabled.

- **C07 / user-facing host-loss safeguard:** The [host-loss notice](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/web/src/components/terminal/terminalLifecycleNotices.copy.ts) now identifies the terminal and thread, says termination could not be confirmed, and explains that available output stays visible and no automatic restart will occur. It offers no restart action. Fifteen focused web tests verified the copy, lack of action, and retained pane behavior; formatting, lint, lengths and web typecheck passed. The exact durable reservation and owner-death fence are unchanged; this is not crash recovery or a new manual release path.

- **C07 / live manager quit and no-hint detach:** The [real macOS terminal manager cases](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevTerminal.managerQuit.test.ts) use the selected native PTY adapter, stock OpenSSH 10.5p1, the actual backend quit-intent reader, and disposable SQLite. With a quit hint, keep mode off and on both closed the original exact lease, reaped its SSH PID, and marked the same reservation `terminal` without any additional launch or fallback. With no hint, the same child and lease remained live after manager shutdown and the durable reservation stayed unchanged. Nineteen tests across five focused files passed, alongside server typecheck, formatting, lint, and lengths. No runtime change was needed. This establishes manager shutdown, not an Electron quit, whole-backend shutdown or post-restart reattachment.

- **C07 / real dev backend restart fails closed:** The [macOS Bun backend regression](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevTerminal.backendRestart.test.ts) opened a native OpenSSH PTY through authenticated WebSocket, verified shell output, then sent SIGTERM to the owned backend without a desktop Quit hint. The owner, SSH child and fixture listener exited; a different backend process opened the same SQLite inode. Attach-only returned `TerminalNotRunningError`, and ordinary Open against the old directory was refused before owner acquisition. The exact reservation and writer epoch did not change; neither request launched an owner, dialed SSH or invoked legacy. Seven focused tests across four files passed, including related live Restart, Quit and dispatcher attachment, with formatting, lint, lengths and server typecheck. This is verified fail-closed shutdown/restart, not same-PTY survival, SIGKILL or Electron auto-respawn. Recovery still requires an independently retained owner and fixture plus verified private attachment and exact-lease admission; deliberate Quit behavior and the owner-death fence are unchanged.

- **C07 / cold manager exact native reattachment:** The [manager open path](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal/Layers/Manager.session.open.ts) now recognizes an exact durable native lease at the startup-bound workspace root without treating renderer history as a prerequisite. It pins the existing lease ID and skips the otherwise dialing cwd probe; the adapter still performs the actual lease reattach. The [live macOS manager case](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevTerminal.managerRecovery.test.ts) used an empty logs directory for the fresh manager, refused a wrong root, recovered the same OpenSSH PID and unchanged durable row, and received new shell output without an additional dial, prepare or open. Seven focused live manager/quit/restart/crash cases passed, and the expanded wrong-root/resumed-output case passed separately; scoped formatting, lint, length and server typecheck passed. This provisions safe manager recovery when an owner survives; independent supervisor and actual two-backend-process survival evidence are separate.

- **C07 / same terminal across real backend replacement:** The opt-in macOS Bun source-dev supervisor (`BIGBUD_SSH_OWNER=1 BIGBUD_SSH_OWNER_RETAIN=1 bun run dev`) keeps one disposable SSH fixture and owner outside the backend lifetime. The [retained restart proof](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevTerminal.retainedRestart.test.ts) killed the first Bun backend and authenticated a different backend to the same owner across the private Unix attachment. The exact OpenSSH PID, native birth generation, lease and reservation were retained; verified from-zero history appeared once and new terminal input worked, with unchanged dial and authentication counts. Intentional Quit closed the exact lease and settled SQLite; without a hint it stayed alive until launcher shutdown retired the PTY, owner and fixture listener. Hard-linked handoff and tampered capability refused without a second owner or writer change. The focused live run passed 19 tests; a separate real [dev supervisor CLI smoke](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/scripts/dev-supervise.ts) replaced a crashed Bun process under the unchanged handoff and shut down cleanly. Final validation passed 61 focused Vitest tests, 16 focused Rust tests, scripts/shared/server typechecks, scoped Clippy, formatting, lint and file-length checks. History is verified byte replay, not an exact renderer-screen model; without a complete replay the manager still refuses to invent a baseline. This does not qualify Electron, other OSes, normal dev without the opt-in, detached idle or automatic owner-death recovery.

- **C03 / selected connected-monitor reachability:** The connected list now uses native typed health for the exact generated, non-retained direct workspace after successful verification establishes the caller lease. Confirmed loss or successful Disconnect removes the lease; unavailable owner or native denial does not masquerade as transport loss. Connect alone cannot recreate a lease. The [macOS handler proof](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshMonitor.healthOwner.native.test.ts) used real SQLite, the native owner and stock OpenSSH. Twenty-nine focused tests across eight files and explicit quality checks passed; the integrated server package typecheck passed after Git composition settled. This is not a browser or full WebSocket monitor stream; stats, subscriptions, other profiles and selected unsupported native lanes remain closed.
- **C06 / selected direct Git reads:** The [Git layer](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitCore.ssh.selected.ts) now takes the same existing selected dev runtime as the rest of the server; it does not construct a second owner. Only the exact selected Unix target and fixture root can dispatch the existing bounded worktree, status, staged/unstaged numstat and origin metadata verbs. Unsupported Git, other profiles/roots and Disconnect fail without legacy fallback. The [live macOS test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.git.test.ts) now uses the actual configured remote Git service with a disposable repository, native owner and stock OpenSSH; it observed no additional dial for invalid requests or Disconnect. Four focused files passed 65 tests, and a separate real backend startup/monitor regression run passed two tests. Explicit formatting, lint, lengths, whitespace and server typecheck passed. This is server composition evidence, not browser status, initiating G1 transport or mutation parity.

- **C10 / Pi explicit source-dev selection:** The [Pi capability guard](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/Pi/RpcProcess.selection.ts) now refuses remote startup, remote version probes, and the remote-workspace bridge in selected mode before SSH readiness, bridge setup or process launch. The [session-start regression](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/Pi/RpcProcess.selection.test.ts) checks zero denied-path side effects and a local recorder `get_state`. - **C05 / E2 guarded-save foundations:** The native preview now returns an optional SHA-256 of the complete raw payload (never a range). The [preview decoder](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.codec.workspaceFile.ts) validates the private field and accepts old replies without it. The [live macOS case](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.workspace.test.ts) verified BOM, CRLF and multibyte text plus its exact-byte digest over stock OpenSSH. Thirteen focused TS tests, eight native workspace tests, nine compatibility/grant checks, strict owner **binary** Clippy and server typecheck passed; test-target Clippy is blocked by existing test lints in the retained attachment lifetime module. The [standalone guarded recipe](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/workspace-write-guarded.py) and [fixtures](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/workspace/guarded_write.tests.rs) verified a 512 KiB guarded save, conflict, cooperative locking, exact bytes and supported metadata-preserving in-place writes in disposable substrate tests (11 native fixture cases plus Linux checks). No live SSH launch of that recipe, host tool qualification, guarded chunk/lease admission or editor Save enablement is claimed. E2 remains open and the direct SSH editor remains read-only. E0/E1 shared editor, RPC, contracts and agent-write files were not edited.

- **C05 / E2 bounded guarded-write staging:** The [isolated native state machine](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/guarded_write_stage.rs) permits one draft per owner, up to 512 KiB in contiguous chunks of at most 48 KiB. It binds lease, generation, exact scope, operation ID, base hash, attempted hash and byte count; a matching commit consumes the lease and only returns bytes after full SHA-256 and NUL-free UTF-8 verification. Expiry and attachment/generation changes discard the draft. The [focused target](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/tests/guarded_write_stage.rs) passed eight tests, targeted Clippy, formatter and length checks. ## W1 owner-core slice (25 November 2026)

Unit-only evidence on macOS arm64; no live sshd fixture was run for these entries, and no Windows-native run was attempted.

- **W1.1 / frozen production verb names.** Every `proof_*` operation now has one recorded production name in [`v1.golden.verbs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/v1.golden.verbs), mirrored in the [`v1.proto`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/v1.proto) comment and checked against the schema by `v1.golden.verbs.test.ts`. Field numbers and the five existing product verbs are asserted unchanged; reserved future names stay unallocated. No operation is promoted in product code (W7 owns promotion). The protocol golden suite passed 7 tests, including the new files, after adding syntactic frames and grant digests for operations 54–60 and outcomes 35–38 that the coverage checker already required.
- **W1.2 / owner budget and session quota.** The [budget](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_grants.budget.rs) admits one grant per settled operation, keeps a reserved interactive share, and returns the additive `ERROR_CODE_SESSION_QUOTA = 28` instead of `OWNER_UNAVAILABLE` or auth. The key and password transports map `MaxSessions`, "Too many sessions" and `open failed: administratively prohibited` to quota before any auth check. Seven focused `budget` unit tests and the `max_sessions` regression passed; the owner bin suite passed 323 tests and the library 65. The live `sshOwnerPickerProof.sessionBudget.test.ts` (`MaxSessions 2`) is written but was not run: this host is macOS and no disposable Linux fixture was provided.
- **W1.4 / Windows key-only denial.** `ERROR_CODE_WINDOWS_PASSWORD_UNSUPPORTED = 29` crosses the proto, Rust (Windows picker and dispatcher, method classifier, transport), TypeScript codec and goldens. The dispatcher answers a password-class request locally before forwarding, and a password-only `Permission denied` maps to the typed denial, not auth. Web copy is "Use an SSH key for this host." and never opens a prompt. `windows_password_denial`, the Windows client test and the web copy test passed; no native Windows run was performed.
- **W1.5 / keyboard-interactive refusal.** `ERROR_CODE_KEYBOARD_INTERACTIVE_UNSUPPORTED = 30` and the [`auth_methods`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/auth_methods.rs) guard decide from the OpenSSH method list, before any askpass listener, master or release. Key and password lanes pass and their fixed methods never offer keyboard-interactive; a keyboard-interactive-only method list refuses in the fixture. The typed wire error is fixture-proven, not derived from real destination capability. Password bytes remain under the existing wipe guard. `sshOwnerPickerProof.keyboardInteractive.linux.test.ts` is written but not run (no disposable Linux fixture); its OTP negative asserts zero connections, no master and no cached release. A CHANGELOG entry was added under **Unreleased**.
- **W1.6 / policy v3 for inert GSSAPI.** `GSSAPIAuthentication yes` is admitted only because the private key and password launches now share [`GSSAPI_INERT_OVERRIDE`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/config/fixed_policy.rs) (`GSSAPIAuthentication=no`). `GSSAPIDelegateCredentials`, `GSSAPIKeyExchange` and `GSSAPITrustDNS` gained named, redacted diagnostics and stay refused. The recorded stock Debian 13 active lines (comments omitted) are admitted unedited in `default_policy_v3_tests`, and the launch options are asserted to offer only publickey. The unit suite passed; the live Debian stock-config rerun was not performed because no disposable Debian copy was used.

## W1 owner-core review fixes (25 November 2026)

Read-only review follow-up for the W1 owner core. macOS arm64, focused checks only; no repository-wide scan, full suite or workspace-wide check; no commit or push. Automatic retirement remains disabled.

- **B1 / per-instance journal.** [retirement.rs](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/retirement.rs) now uses `owner.retirement.<kind>.<16-hex>` records, with `Instance` handles for `bind`/`clear`; `begin` generates a fresh slot. `reconcile` verifies every record and clears each only on verified reap. The regression `same_kind_instances_are_independent_and_each_is_cleared_on_reap` reproduces the old leg-2 refusal and passes, and `four_jump_legs_are_each_reconciled_and_cleared` covers the 4-leg bound. The live jump test now constructs a real `Retirement` instead of `None` and asserts no leg record remains after `retire`. **The live jump test is not run here** (requires the approved Linux two-daemon namespace).
- **S1 / typed, recoverable refusal.** Added additive `ERROR_CODE_OWNER_RETIREMENT_UNVERIFIED = 31` across proto, Rust, TS codec and goldens. The owner emits one redacted `ssh_owner_retirement_refused` stderr record; the picker surfaces `ownerRetirementUnverified` and the web copy names the remedy. `bigbud-ssh-owner --retirement-recover --lock <stateDir>/backend.lock --record <name>` re-verifies read-only and clears only a provably absent or PID-reused record; exact live, unreadable, unknown and namespace-mismatch records refuse. Tests cover gone, PID reuse, exact live, unreadable and unknown, and no signalling.
- **S2 / auto-retire gate.** `auto_retire_enabled(requested, disposable_fixture)` requires both the reviewed switch and the same validated disposable root used by the lease fence; otherwise the switch is ignored and a redacted reason is written to stderr. The normal-profile test sets the predicate false with a live exact record: refusal unchanged, process alive.
- **S3 / unknown names.** `parse_child_name` accepts only `owner.retirement.<master|connector>.<16 lowercase hex>`. Forged, future-kind and extra-suffix names refuse; an interrupted-write temp is recognized and removed (it cannot precede a spawn). Tests cover both.
- **S4 / mirror truthfulness.** `PickerProofOwner` now exposes `targetGeneration`; the dev runtime writes the real hex generation instead of a random UUID, and acts on `recordSshOwnerRetirementIntent`/`Binding` results (`unresolved`/`conflict` raise the typed refusal after closing a started owner). The layer test covers intent conflict and binding conflict; `markSshOwnerRetirementRetired` now reports `retired` or `missing` honestly.
- **S6 / quota test.** The Linux session-budget test no longer relies on a burst that may all succeed. It issues three interactive grants against a two-slot budget through the proof-only grant path and asserts exactly two successes, at least one wire-28 `sessionQuota`, and no wire-9 `authRequired`. **Written but not run** (no disposable Linux fixture in this pass).
- **S9 / status ordering.** The broker assigns the sequence before the probe starts and drops an older completion, and the monitor does not publish after the probe signal aborts. Deterministic tests cover late-older completion and abort.
- **S5 / keyboard-interactive wording.** The typed error is fixture-proven; the product guarantee is that the fixed key/password method configuration never offers keyboard-interactive. Deriving a real destination-capability error without prompt heuristics is a tracked follow-up. The earlier stronger ledger claim is corrected below.
- **S7 / pidfd.** Linux `retire_exact` opens a pidfd, re-verifies the exact `(start, namespace)` against the pinned instance and sends SIGTERM through the pidfd; a mismatch refuses before signalling. macOS keeps the re-verified `kill` path with the residual check-to-signal window documented. The switch remains off.
- **S8 / budget semantics.** The budget is a **grant-issuance bound**, not a live-session count: one slot per admitted-but-unsettled grant, released on consume/expiry/revoke. Serialized clients settle under the cap; the plan and ledger now say so.
- **S10 / Windows gap.** Windows still has no owner retirement journal (the one-shot picker binds no child identity and is POSIX-only). A faithful image-plus-creation-time/ACL implementation and native evidence are now an explicit tracked W6 gap rather than a silent omission.
- **Nits.** Unique per-write temp names, macOS `pbi_pid == pid` cross-check, connector record cleared when the connector is already `None`, `Owner::drop` teardown ordering comment, migration 184 comment/constraint alignment, honest retired return, real generation, quote-copy, and the vacuous key-only/own-options assertions removed.
- **Checks.** `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner -- --check`, scoped strict Clippy, **339** owner bin tests (6 ignored), **65** library tests, Linux and Windows `cargo check`; live macOS `posixOwnerDeathRetirement`, `posixOwnerCrash` and both health tests passed; focused Vitest 62 server tests (subscription, persistence, picker, startup diagnostic) and 8 web tests; server typecheck passed with the documented 8 GiB heap. Protocol goldens passed 7 TS and 9 verify-rust. Explicit-path format/lint and the 400-line limit were checked on changed files.
- **Written but not run:** Linux `posixOwnerDeathRetirement`, Linux `sessionBudget`, and the live jump test. No pass is claimed for them.

It reuses the cutover `token`/`digest` report helpers, reads only explicit paths (missing input is an error; 5,000-file and 2 MiB bounds), and emits `structuralOnly: true` / `s4Accepted: false`. Twenty-five fixture tests passed, including every deleted row 1–8, `sh -c "ssh …"`, node-pty `file: "ssh"`, `spawn(process.env.SHELL, ["-c", …])`, renamed `sshBinary`, concatenated executables and D1–D5. The lock uses the existing bounded 8-attempt contention path and fails closed with the command, read/write mode and owner details. ## W1.3/W1.7 owner retirement and status slice (25 November 2026)

Bounded second slice of the [finish-line plan](2026-11-25-rust-ssh-ownership-finish-line-plan.md): W1.3 owner-death retirement and W1.7 bounded status. macOS arm64, source checkout, focused checks only. No full-suite, workspace-wide or repo-wide run; no commit or push. Automatic retirement remains **disabled**.

- **W1.3 / owner-death retirement journal.** [owner/retirement.rs](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/retirement.rs) writes an owner intent (PID, start time, Linux PID-namespace inode, owner epoch) before any owned child may spawn, and a per-instance record (`owner.retirement.<kind>.<slot>`) before `spawn`, completed with the exact child identity after it exists. Startup after the profile lock reconciles every record read-only: unresolved intent, PID reuse (different start time), namespace mismatch, PID-only binding, unknown names and unreadable records refuse admission; a verifiably absent record is removed; a live exact instance is refused without signalling. The owner Drop clears the intent only on a graceful return, so a SIGKILL leaves the evidence. `PosixMasterSlot`/`Master`, jump legs and the connector helper write and clear their records around checked spawn/reap. **Automatic retirement** (SIGTERM to an exact live instance after re-checking the identity) is behind `BIGBUD_SSH_OWNER_AUTO_RETIRE=approved-disposable`, is ignored unless the validated disposable fixture root matches, and is set by no product path. Per-instance binding covers the persistent master lanes (direct key/password, jump legs) and the connector helper; other ephemeral SSH children remain covered by the owner-level intent until their consumer tracks adopt `Retirement::begin`/`bind`. **Windows has no owner retirement journal yet**; that is a tracked W6 gap (see the review-fix section above).
- **W1.3 / Build A mirror, migration 184.** [184_SshOwnerRetirement.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Migrations/184_SshOwnerRetirement.ts) adds `ssh_owner_retirement(profile_scope, target_id, owner_epoch, generation, state in intent|bound|retired, pid, start_time, namespace, detail, updated_at, PRIMARY KEY(profile_scope,target_id))` plus `(profile_scope,state)`. Intent rows carry no identity; bound rows require epoch and generation (the owner journal is authoritative for PID/start/namespace and may leave them empty in the mirror); retired rows are terminal until a new intent replaces them. The row is registered in `Migrations.ts` (`latestMigrationId` 184) and is part of the Build A schema ceiling. [SshOwnerRetirement.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Layers/SshOwnerRetirement.ts) records intent once, completes it once, marks verified retirement, lists unresolved rows, and never overwrites unresolved evidence. The selected dev runtime writes intent before owner start and bound after `HelloAccepted`, then marks retired when the picker close returns (the owner journal still decides admission). Migration and layer tests passed (3 focused tests).
- **W1.3 / live macOS evidence.** [sshOwnerPickerProof.posixOwnerDeathRetirement.test.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.posixOwnerDeathRetirement.test.ts) ran with `BIGBUD_TEST_SSH_OWNER_PICKER_PROOF=1` on stock OpenSSH 10.5p1. It established a live master, recorded `owner.retirement` and a per-instance `owner.retirement.master.<slot>` (bound, exact PID/start), SIGKILLed the owner, and observed the replacement refuse while the socket inode, the exact master PID and the journal stayed unchanged and the SQLite writer row stayed byte-identical. After the lease was moved aside, the replacement still refused with the typed `ownerRetirementUnverified`, proving the journal is independently binding and recoverable only by the explicit remedy. **The Linux variant is written but not run** (no disposable Linux fixture in this pass); it asserts the same refusal from a fresh private PID namespace and checks the recorded namespace is present.
- **W1.7 / bounded typed status.** [sshMonitor.healthSubscription.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshMonitor.healthSubscription.ts) fans one sample to independent subscribers with a per-subscriber queue (capacity 2 by default). Overflow coalesces to one `resync` plus the newest event, so a stalled reader cannot grow memory or block the sampler, the owner or another subscriber. A generation change queues a `resync`; cancellation removes only that subscriber. `unavailable` (could not sample) stays distinct from `unreachable` (observed failure). [sshMonitor.healthOwner.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshMonitor.healthOwner.ts) owns the broker and publishes after each typed `sampleHealth`, preserving the existing owner/lease denial path. Nine focused unit tests passed.
- **W1.7 / live macOS evidence.** The extended [sshOwnerPickerProof.health.test.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.health.test.ts) passed both tests with the disposable macOS fixture. Two subscribers received the same typed status; a stalled reader stayed bounded to `[resync, newest]`; draining added no destination connection; cancelling one subscriber did not disturb the other; the legacy launch recorder stayed empty. The live test used the owner's non-cancellable health sample because the fixture does not opt into the private FD3 cancellation lane; the selected runtime composition is unchanged and keeps `sampleCancellableProofHealth`.
- **Checks.** `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner -- --check`, `cargo clippy --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner --all-targets -- -D warnings`, 333 owner bin tests (6 ignored), 65 library tests, and Linux + Windows cross-target `cargo check` passed. Focused Vitest: 12 persistence/subscription tests, 2 live health tests and 1 live retirement test passed; 1 Linux retirement test skipped. Server typecheck passed. Explicit-path format/lint and the 400-line limit were checked on changed files only.
- **Written but not run:** `sshOwnerPickerProof.posixOwnerDeathRetirement.linux.test.ts`. No pass is claimed for it.

## S2 transition faults and closure (24 November 2026)

- **Lost committed Disconnect reply, with the native owner and PTY surviving (macOS arm64, stock OpenSSH 10.5p1, real SQLite).** [The live test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.transitionFaults.test.ts) started the attachable native owner, listed, opened a ready PTY and detached. Over the private attachment it then committed Disconnect through the real storage adapter and never sent the storage reply. The flag (with its timestamp) and receipt sequence 1 committed while the PTY child was still alive. A backend restart followed: a fresh SQLite scope and a fresh authenticated client, which rotated the writer and hydrated the committed flag. The PTY was retired once, before acknowledgement, and the rows were byte-identical. Listing and opening a terminal returned `targetDisconnected`, and closing the old lease was refused rather than repeated. A second restart found nothing left to tear down and the rows still unchanged. Neither restart made a destination connection. Only explicit Connect cleared the flag (sequence 2) without dialing; the next read made exactly one connection, and the retired lease then returned `operationDenied` as unknown. A test-owned outside master stayed alive and answered mux checks throughout.
- **Delayed old-epoch Connect after a newer Disconnect, against a live owner.** The test formed a Connect under the original writer epoch and owner generation, then committed a newer Disconnect (sequence 1) from a reattached client. The delayed storage transition returned `epochMismatch`. The delayed wire Connect, carrying the stale generation, was refused at grant before any storage transition was requested. The SQLite rows were unchanged, a fresh client still got `targetDisconnected`, there were zero new destination connections, and the outside master was untouched.
- **Supporting change.** The attachment proof helpers moved to [a shared fixture module](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.attachment.fixture.ts), which adds durable-row and stale-request helpers. No owner defect was found, and no Rust or product code changed for these cases.
- **Limits.** The backend restart is a fresh in-process storage scope and client against the surviving native owner process; the real separate-process backend restart remains the earlier `backendRestart` evidence. The attachment lane is macOS-only, so no Linux run was attempted.
- **Checks.** With `BIGBUD_TEST_SSH_OWNER_PICKER_PROOF=1`, the new file plus the existing attachment suite passed 5 tests in each of 2 consecutive runs. Explicit-path oxfmt and oxlint passed, the three touched files are at most 248 lines, and the server typecheck passed.
- **S2 exit review (re-done).** The two missing transition cases now have live evidence. Every remaining S2 criterion in the macOS and Linux development scope has evidence:
  - The durable-disconnect schema, writer rotation, receipts, stale and conflict handling, and failed COMMIT.
  - Lost acknowledgements in both directions, and delayed old-epoch requests in both directions (Disconnect after Connect earlier; Connect after Disconnect now).
  - Backend and owner restart without admission.
  - The reconnect controller.
  - The POSIX password master.
  - Cancellation and Disconnect during native startup on macOS and Linux, with outside masters untouched.
  - Checked child retirement and storage rebind.

  **S2 is closed for the macOS and Linux development scope. S1 was closed above.** The following named, non-skippable gates remain open:
  - Signed pre-A/A/B rollback (Pre-cutover qualification).
  - Owner-death identity retirement (hard pre-S4).
  - The Debian stock GSSAPI default, which blocks Debian profile S4.
  - Windows password, where the typed `windowsPasswordUnsupported` denial must be implemented before credential collection, and keyboard-interactive, which stays unqualified. Both block S4 unless an explicit compatibility decision is made.
  - Live port 22 (pre-cutover environment matrix).
  - Release-artifact and runner rows (Pre-cutover).

  S3 and S4 acceptance are not affected.

## S1–S2 closure review (24 November 2026)

- **Linux connector, live (user-approved install in the disposable VM only).** `netcat-openbsd 1.229-1` was installed with `apt-get` in `bb-ssh-owner-linux-dev`; nothing was installed on the macOS host. The image is `/usr/bin/nc.openbsd`, SHA-256 `1bd30fad66f1ad6d0cd563354b50756b4a644b70beb4993493d1939591c7e998`, and `/usr/bin/nc` is an alternatives symlink. The connector picker mode and connector-only route facts now also bind to the Linux fixture: `/tmp/bigbud-picker-linux-*`, user `picker`, port 22222, with no account or route overrides. [The Linux test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.connector.linux.test.ts) ran with real SQLite in the fresh private scratch `/home/bbdev/workspace-mode-proof.1MZti8/s1s2-closure.kKGXPF`, inside rootless bwrap, and passed 4 runs. In each positive case, bare `nc %h %p` on the approved custom-directory PATH (a private copy of the packaged image) and the absolute `<custom>/nc %h %p` listed twice over exactly one destination connection and one accepted key. The only helper process was `<custom>/nc 127.0.0.1 22222`, and none remained after close. These returned `routeUnavailable`: a marker earlier on PATH, the marker named absolutely, and the packaged `/usr/bin/nc` symlink on PATH `/usr/bin`. Shell syntax (`;`, `|`), extra flags (`-w 3`, trailing `-v`) and wrappers (`/usr/bin/env`, `/bin/sh -c`) returned startup `configUnsupported`. Every negative recorded zero destination connections and zero accepted keys, and the marker, which was executable inside the namespace, never ran.
- **Linux outside master.** The fixture can start a test-owned `ControlMaster` beside sshd in the same private namespace. The entry script removes any stale socket from preflight and waits for a fresh one. In both [Linux authentication-in-flight cases](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.authInFlight.linux.test.ts), its PID, `lstart` and argv were unchanged after cancellation, Disconnect, the late signature and reconnect, and its mux still answered `-O check`. Owner counts were taken relative to it, so it carried no owner traffic. Three consecutive runs passed together with the connector test.
- **User-accepted positions, recorded as accepted.** (1) The Linux host-key-proof hold point (sshd `HostKeyAgent` holding one signature with a real handshake in flight) satisfies "authentication in flight" for S2. (2) Custom-directory `nc` on macOS is a known macOS fixture limit: a copy of Apple's signed `/usr/bin/nc` never connects. Custom-PATH coverage comes from the live Linux test and the native Rust unit tests.
- **Reclassified named gates; these are not skippable and remain open.** Signed pre-A/A/B rollback moves to **Pre-cutover qualification**; the plan's S2 text says the actual release-pair rollback requirement "must pass at Pre-cutover qualification before S4". Owner-death identity retirement is a **hard pre-S4 gate** by the prior user decision; automatic retirement stays disabled. The Debian stock `GSSAPIAuthentication yes` default **blocks Debian profile S4**, quoting "If a supported OS's default configuration is rejected, block that profile's S4 until its benign directives are explicitly covered". Unqualified Windows password **must be an explicit typed denial** (`windowsPasswordUnsupported`, returned "before credential collection"), and keyboard-interactive stays unqualified. Both block S4 unless an explicit compatibility decision is made. Today the typed denial exists only as the documented contract in `windows_password_gate.rs`; the Windows dev picker refuses passwords without that code. Live port 22 moves to the **pre-cutover environment matrix**.
- **Independent review of S1 exit** (“trace the entire slice, including child identity, RPC result, durable disconnect and restart, and pass the S1 effective-trust fixtures”). Each element has evidence in this ledger:
  - The control channel, lock, epoch and attachment.
  - Unknown-host refusal, key swap before approval, approval, listing, outage and recovery, and durable Disconnect across owner, DB and backend restart.
  - Explicit Connect, and a changed host key.
  - Owner death, version mismatch and an absent binary returning `sshOwnerUnavailable`.
  - Before-Connect syscall audits.
  - Alias and `HostKeyAlias` on a non-default port, user/system precedence and includes, identity ordering, two and quoted known-hosts files, and hashed, revoked and CA records.
  - Inert unrelated stanzas, refused active `Match exec`, and a config change before approval.
  - An injected outside master on macOS and Linux.
  - Two-hop key-only ProxyJump with per-hop counts and a changed hop key.
  - The restricted connector with custom-PATH `nc` and substitute and syntax negatives.
  - Shipped defaults for macOS (admitted) and Debian (refused at GSSAPI, which is a reclassified gate).
  - The saved-alias retargeting rule.

  "Two-hop" is read as the qualified bastion→destination route; multi-bastion routes remain unverified. Port 22 is reclassified. **No S1 criterion inside the development scope lacks evidence.**

- **Independent review of S2 exit** (“native and integration tests prove the auth/lifecycle matrix; no previously supported combination is silently dropped”). Evidence exists for:
  - Schema, migration and writer rotation; transition receipts, conflicts and stale requests; the lost Connect acknowledgement live; storage-level withheld acknowledgement and writer-race fencing.
  - Failed COMMIT and a failed Disconnect with a live master.
  - Reconnect backoff and typed health retry; owner restart wiping passwords and refusing residual leases.
  - The POSIX password master (unlock, relock and refusal); cancellation and Disconnect during native startup on macOS and Linux; checked child retirement; storage rebind.

  **Missing evidence (S2 cannot close):**
  1. The plan's transition-fault case "crash after commit before reply" for **Disconnect** with a surviving native owner and PTY, then backend restart, asserting actual SQLite state, post-restart admission and zero stale transport teardown. Only the Connect direction is live, and the Disconnect direction is storage-only; the carry-forward reviewer case is still open.
  2. "delayed old-epoch Connect after a newer Disconnect" asserted against a live owner, with admission and teardown checks. Only SQLite race tests cover it.

  Windows and keyboard-interactive rows are reclassified gates, not development-scope S2 evidence.

- **Verdict.** S1 is closed for the macOS and Linux development scope, with the reclassified gates above still open. S2 was not closed at that review because items 1 and 2 lacked evidence; superseded by the S2 transition-fault entry above.

## S1 policy v2 for Debian defaults and S2 Linux authentication in flight (24 November 2026)

- **S1 / policy v2, per the user's decision.** (a) Host patterns may contain `/`, which is valid only in Host patterns; Match and the other pattern syntax are unchanged. An alias or loopback HostName can never contain `/`, so the systemd `Host unix/* vsock/* machine/*` and `.host machine/.host` stanzas stay inert. Their `ProxyCommand`, `ProxyUseFdpass yes`, `StrictHostKeyChecking no` and `/dev/null` trust lines are never applied. [The tests](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/config/default_policy_v2_tests.rs) cover the packaged stanzas verbatim for aliases `picker`, `unix`, `machine`, `vsock`, `host` and `localhost`. `picker/*`, `*/picker` and `picker/` do not match `picker`. Bracket, brace, backslash and `%` forms are still refused, and an active `Host */* *` stanza is still policed. (b) `SendEnv` admits exact `COLORTERM` and `NO_COLOR` (and their removal) alongside `LANG`/`LC_*`. `COLORTERM_X`, `NO_COLORS`, `COLOR*`, `TERM` and lowercase names are refused. Only the selected names present in the environment are forwarded, and clean probes and pinned peers still omit them (existing SendEnv tests unchanged, plus the live locale test). (c) `GSSAPIAuthentication yes` stays `sshConfigUnsupported`. The remedy now reads “GSSAPI authentication is not supported. Add GSSAPIAuthentication no for this host.”, with directive, source class and line, and no value. The Rust and TypeScript remedy tables match. The ProxyCommand, Host and SendEnv remedies were updated for the connector and v2 policy, and their four expectation sites were updated.
- **Stock Debian 13 re-run (disposable copy, Include prefix only).** `/etc/ssh/ssh_config` with the packaged `20-systemd-ssh-proxy.conf` is refused only at `GSSAPIAuthentication` (system_config line 53) with the new remedy. With that line set to `no`, it is **admitted**. Adding the guest's non-stock OrbStack include is refused at its `IdentityAgent` (system_include line 3), as expected for an ambient agent. The macOS stock config remains admitted. A Debian profile still requires the user to add `GSSAPIAuthentication no`; the shipped file is not admitted unedited.
- **routeFacts failure: outdated expectations, no regression.** Both stale cases returned the typed located startup diagnostic introduced by the earlier redacted-Match and trust-path work, which this change did not touch. Without a trusted account, `Match ... localuser` is refused at Match (user_config line 6), which matches the existing redacted-Match ledger entry. Without the port override, `%p` selects an absent trust file, which is refused at UserKnownHostsFile (line 8). The uid-mismatch case still returns `ownerUnavailable` from the trusted-facts binder, and the positive case still lists. [The test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.routeFacts.test.ts) now asserts the located diagnostics and passes.
- **S2 / Linux port of the cancellable owned-mux lane.** `CancellableMuxRead`, the private FD 3 cancellation inbox, its admission limits and `ListFailure` now build on Linux. They are bound to the `/tmp/bigbud-picker-linux-*` fixture with user `picker` on port 22222, and the Linux master uses the default trust reader. The direct cancellable read and cancellable health remain macOS-only. The owned master lane freezes `/tmp` read-only, as the password lane does, because the residual-lease fence checks its parent. A rootless sshd cannot use `AuthorizedKeysCommand`, because bwrap maps real root to the overflow UID and sshd rejects `/bin/sh` as unsafe (measured). Instead, the [fixture gate](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshPicker.loopback.fixture.linux.hostKeyGate.ts) serves the in-memory Ed25519 host key through sshd `HostKeyAgent` and holds one armed signature. That keeps a real OpenSSH handshake in flight: the client has connected and is waiting on host-key proof before user authentication. This differs from the macOS gate, which holds the user public-key lookup.
- **Debian 13 arm64, stock OpenSSH 10.0p2, Bubblewrap, fresh private scratch `/home/bbdev/workspace-mode-proof.1MZti8/s1s2-linux-auth.6jQylr`.** The private scratch is a copy of the current sources; `node_modules` was copied from the shared scratch with workspace links resolving inside the copy, and nothing was installed. [The Linux test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.authInFlight.linux.test.ts) passed three consecutive runs (2 tests each). In the waiter case, the held master (identified by PID, `lstart` and argv) was gone after its waiter was cancelled. The queued waiter then succeeded on a different master, with two connections, one accepted key and one owner start. In the Disconnect case, both the in-flight and queued reads were fenced, the exact held child was retired with no `bbcm-` lease left, and the SQLite disconnect row was committed. Releasing the held signature produced no new accepted key, no new connection and no admission. Connect did not dial, and the next read authenticated once. No outside master was used on Linux, because the fixture sshd lives in the owner's private network namespace and is unreachable from outside; untouched-outside-master evidence remains macOS-only.

## S1 restricted connector lane (24 November 2026)

- **Policy (versioned change).** `config.rs` was split by concern: [fixed policy and route family](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/config/fixed_policy.rs) moved out, and the resolver is now 349 lines. [The connector grammar](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/config/connector.rs) reads the raw `ProxyCommand` value and admits only `nc %h %p` or an absolute literal path named `nc` with exactly those operands. Shell syntax, quotes, wrappers, extra flags, other programs and other expansions remain `sshConfigUnsupported`. The first value wins, `none` stays direct, any active ProxyCommand with a jump is refused, and the connector is part of the saved-alias descriptor. The previous config test that expected `nc %h %p` to be refused now uses flag and wrapper negatives.
- **Resolution, pin and launch.** [The transport connector](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/connector.rs) resolves a bare `nc` to the first existing entry on the trusted startup PATH (absolute, at most four entries) and never searches past a substitute. It qualifies the image by SHA-256 against the trusted approved digest. It requires a regular file, no symlink, no group or world write access on the file or its directory, and root or current-user ownership. It pins dev, inode, size, mtime, mode and owner, then re-hashes and re-stats before every launch. The owner launches `nc <host> <port>` with structured argv, a cleared environment and two private pipes, and gives those pipes to an owned OpenSSH master as `ProxyCommand=-`. No shell, credential or askpass grant reaches the helper. The lease retains the helper process and reaps it after the master. When a connector policy is present, every direct SSH child and host-key scan returns the new `routeUnavailable` (wire code 27, folder RPC `sshRouteUnavailable`), and so does any missing, substituted or unqualified helper. Startup selection is the macOS fixture `--posix-connector-picker` with `--fixture-connector-image` and `--fixture-connector-path`.
- **Live macOS arm64, stock OpenSSH 10.5p1, `/usr/bin/nc` (`com.apple.nc`, SHA-256 `0ee35bac…1442`).** [The live test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.connector.test.ts) ran with real SQLite. The owner sandbox allowed exec of the marker helper, `/bin/sh` and `/usr/bin/touch`, so only owner policy could keep the marker from running. Bare `nc %h %p` on approved PATH `/usr/bin` listed the folder twice over one destination connection and one accepted key. The only connector process was `/usr/bin/nc 127.0.0.1 <port>` while the owner lived, and none remained after close. Absolute `/usr/bin/nc %h %p` listed over one new connection. A marker `nc` earlier on the approved PATH, or named absolutely, returned `routeUnavailable`. `nc %h %p; <marker>`, `| <marker>`, `-w 3`, a trailing `-v`, `/usr/bin/env <marker>` and `/bin/sh -c` all returned startup `configUnsupported`. Across all negatives the marker never ran, and the reachable destination recorded zero further connection attempts and no legacy launch.
- **Limit: custom-directory nc on macOS.** A private copy of Apple's signed `/usr/bin/nc` qualifies by digest but never connected when run directly (it was terminated after a 3 s accept timeout), so the custom-directory positive is not qualified on macOS. The custom-PATH mechanism itself is covered by native unit tests with a private helper image. Debian 13 has no `nc`, `ncat`, `netcat`, `nc.openbsd`, `nc.traditional` or netcat package; nothing was installed, so the connector is unqualified on Linux. Revalidation still leaves a check-to-exec window, since macOS has no `fexecve`; the approved directory permissions bound that risk.
- **Debian auth-in-flight cases: not run.** The cancellable mux key-master picker used by the authentication-in-flight cases (`CancellableMuxRead`, its private cancellation inbox and the `PlainFixture` trust read) and the key-mode master are macOS-only. The Debian owner exposes only the synthetic-password master and the pre-pinned jump lane. Neither has cancellation, and the bwrap fixture has no authentication gate. Running these cases on Linux first requires porting that lane and adding a gate to the Linux fixture; no partial result is claimed.
- **Checks.** macOS: 279 owner unit tests, 6 verify-rust goldens, 3 TypeScript goldens, strict owner Clippy and `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner --check` passed, as did `cargo check` for the Windows x64 and Linux targets. The focused unit run passed 62 (1 skipped); explicit-path oxfmt and oxlint, file lengths, and the server, contracts and web typechecks passed. The live run with `BIGBUD_TEST_SSH_OWNER_PICKER_PROOF=1` covered the connector, configStartup, matchStartup, authInFlight, aliasRetarget, profileConfig, alias, posixMaster, cancel, websocketCancel, websocketLifecycle and routeFacts files: 15 passed, 1 skipped and 1 failed. The failure is `routeFacts` expecting `ownerUnavailable` where the owner now returns the typed Match `configUnsupported` startup diagnostic, which matches the earlier redacted-Match entry; it was not rerun on a pre-change build. Debian, in the fresh private scratch `/home/bbdev/workspace-mode-proof.1MZti8/s1s2-connector.CKCa1X`: 287 owner unit tests passed, including connector, grammar and descriptor. Two git-fixture tests failed (`git_receive` advertisement and workspace git nonzero exit), outside this change. Owner Clippy `--no-deps -D warnings` passed. The Debian shipped-defaults policy was not changed.

## S1–S2 shared-core continuation (23 November 2026)

- **S2 / native startup during authentication (macOS arm64, stock OpenSSH 10.5p1).** The loopback fixture can now hold one armed public-key lookup inside the disposable sshd ([gate](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshPicker.loopback.fixture.authGate.ts)) with the root-owned `/bin/sh` as `AuthorizedKeysCommand`, so the native owner's master is genuinely mid-authentication. (1) A waiter cancelled while its master was authenticating: that exact child, identified by PID, `lstart` and argv, was gone; the queued waiter then authenticated once on a new master and listed the folder. Totals: one owner start, two connections, one accepted key. (2) A test-created outside master to the same endpoint was running. Disconnect, requested while the native startup was held, fenced both the in-flight read and a queued read. The owner retired only the held child, committed the SQLite disconnect row and left no `bbcm-` lease. Releasing the held lookup produced no admission, accepted key or new connection. The outside master stayed alive with the same identity and answered `-O check`. Explicit Connect did not dial; the next read authenticated once. Startup is not coalesced across a cancellation; the remaining waiter pays a second connection.
- **Product change for that result.** [Disconnect](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevPicker.ts) now aborts folder reads admitted before the request, cancels the native read where the private cancellation channel exists, and discards a late result otherwise. Reads after the request are unaffected. The existing serialization unit test was updated for the new rule. Automatic retirement after owner death remains disabled; no crash test changed.
- **S1 / saved-alias retargeting rule.** The native owner now reports a SHA-256 [alias descriptor](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/config/descriptor.rs) in `HelloAccepted.proof_alias_descriptor` (field 5, empty without an alias policy; the frozen goldens still match). It covers the destination, port, login, host-key name and algorithm, ordered trust sources and ProxyJump operands, but not identities or timers. Migration [183](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Migrations/183_SshAliasDescriptorBindings.ts) adds `ssh_alias_descriptor_bindings(profile_scope, target_id)`; [the store](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Layers/SshAliasDescriptorBindings.ts) never infers a binding. In the [picker guard](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevPicker.aliasBinding.ts), saving a workspace (folder verification) records the first approval. A later save cannot overwrite it. A changed descriptor returns typed `sshAliasChanged` before any owner request that could dial; a missing descriptor fails closed. Only an explicit Connect re-approves, and only for the exact descriptor that was already presented. The selected macOS profile-config lane scopes this to the validated profile root. The [live test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevRuntime.aliasRetarget.test.ts) ran three real runtimes on one SQLite file. Because the dev runtime creates a new fixture port on every start, a saved profile alias needs Connect re-approval after each dev restart. Migration 183 must be part of the build A/B schema ceiling.

The approved Debian guest has no `nc`, `ncat`, `netcat`, `nc.openbsd` or `nc.traditional` and no netcat package; none was installed. Required work: split `config.rs` (now exactly 400 lines) and parse only `nc %h %p` or an absolute qualified `nc`; resolve it on the approved absolute PATH; pin and revalidate the image; add a structured-argv connector feeding `ProxyCommand=-` with no shell; add a typed route error across proto/TS. With all three removed it is admitted. The guest also has a non-stock OrbStack include with an`IdentityAgent`socket; it did not change the first rejection. No policy was weakened. ## S3 explicit terminal restart follow-up

The manager now marks an explicit restart separately from attach-only recovery. The native SSH terminal adapter permits replacement only after the retained route is confirmed terminal; it refuses a live or uncertain route. The SQLite reservation also compares the previously observed resource identity inside its transaction, so a changed or missing route cannot be replaced by a stale request. The terminal settings explain the direct SSH quit exception and suggest remote tmux or screen for work that must survive.

On macOS arm64, focused Vitest runs passed for [the SQLite-backed native adapter](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal-host/localTerminalHost.directSsh.owner.test.ts), [reservation races](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-agent/remoteAgentOwners.race.test.ts), [manager restart refusal](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal/Layers/Manager.restart.refusal.test.ts), [owned manager lifecycle](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal/Layers/Manager.owned-local.test.ts), and [settings](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/web/src/components/settings/TerminalLifecycleSettings.test.tsx). These use controlled owner ports or fake PTYs; they do not establish a real native PTY restart, full backend-loss reattachment, owner-crash host-lost handling, native idle expiry, or actual app quit. S1–S3 remain unaccepted; platform and release qualification remain separate.

The existing [native dispatcher recovery proof](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.terminalRestart.test.ts) also passed on this checkout with its disposable macOS fixture. It exercises reattachment to the same retained PTY after dispatcher death; it does not exercise the new explicit replacement path.

The separate [native terminal replacement proof](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.terminalReplacement.test.ts) passed on disposable macOS arm64 with stock OpenSSH 10.5p1 and real SQLite. A live route refused explicit replacement; after exact-lease close was confirmed, a distinct native lease opened and the old route could not close it. This uses the adapter directly, not the full manager or app restart.

## S3 selected development workspace watch

The selected development workspace now routes directory watches through the typed native snapshot operation for its exact fixture target and root. Other SSH targets and invalid paths fail before owner acquisition. The [polling stream](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/workspace/Layers/WorkspaceFileSystem.remoteWatch.ts) applies bounded backpressure instead of silently dropping invalidations when its queue is full; focused tests cover ordered generations under a slow reader and cancellation while full.

The [development composition proof](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.devWorkspaceWatch.test.ts) passed on disposable macOS arm64 with stock OpenSSH 10.5p1: an actual file change produced an invalidation, Disconnect produced a rescan request without another dial, and explicit Connect permitted a fresh snapshot and invalidation. Two subscribers then observed the same owner; after one ended, the other received another file change. The default workspace backend was not called. This is the same composition function used by the WebSocket layer, exercised directly rather than through a WebSocket client; an in-flight snapshot is not proven cancelled remotely. Writes, other Files parity, full product composition, and other platforms remain open.

The selected development directory route also refuses an owner-reported truncated listing, because its current product result has no partial-result field. The [composition test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerDev.workspace.test.ts) exercises that refusal with a controlled owner; it does not establish behavior for all directory-listing callers or a native oversized-directory fixture.

- **S2:** A folder caller can cancel while waiting for the shared owner bootstrap without aborting it. A real macOS native owner fixture verified that a second call and a subsequent read used one owner, one OpenSSH master, and one authentication. The cancellation barrier was at publication of the bootstrapped owner, before SSH authentication. A separate controlled agent-pool test verified that Disconnect discards a late successful setup and blocks work until Connect. This does not qualify concurrent waiters during native SSH authentication or a native Disconnect race. [Native bootstrap waiter proof](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.posixMaster.test.ts) · [Late startup regression](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-agent/remoteAgentConnectionPool.disconnect.test.ts)
- **S3:** A reconnect through the same agent lifecycle now rejects a changed instance ID even if the peer presents the retained epoch. The controlled test verified closure before the replacement session became usable. Durable replay verification across a full backend restart and a real replacement agent remain open. [Agent reconnect regression](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-agent/remoteAgentLifecycle.test.ts)

## S1–S2 live key-only forwarding checkpoint

- **Selected native owner, disposable Debian 13 arm64, OpenSSH 10.0p2.** The [native picker dispatcher test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/jump_picker_tests.rs) bootstrapped the existing authenticated v1 owner with a private ProxyJump alias and independent Ed25519 pins. It admitted the normal ListFolders grant, browsed twice using one authentication per endpoint, and refused jump trust inspection through the direct lane. A committed Disconnect acknowledgement fenced the old generation and retired both owned masters before the reply. Reads stayed blocked until explicit Connect, which made no dial until the next read. The storage peer in this Rust test supplied framed acknowledgements. A live SQLite and renderer test for this jump lane is still required.
- The [route lifecycle](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/posix_master/jump.rs) uses one private verified OpenSSH master per independently pinned endpoint. The [mux handoff](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/posix_master/session/forward.rs) passes only the two pipe ends for the next endpoint. Stock OpenSSH consumes the other ends directly with no shell or forwarding listener. The route keeps each forward scoped to its control socket and retires the retained children and sockets in reverse order.
- The [per-hop test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/posix_master/tests/live_jump.rs) rejected a replaced bastion key at leg 1 with zero authentications at either endpoint. It rejected a wrong destination pin at leg 2 with zero destination authentications. Missing pins and resolved endpoint repeats stopped before any client launch. With both pins correct, two fixed folder reads used one destination authentication and checked residual-lease cleanup. The [runner](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/live-jump-fixture.sh) confines both listeners to a private user, network and PID namespace and limits bastion forwarding to the fixed destination. Across both live cases a syscall trace observed nine stock SSH execs, five bastion dials by those clients and four destination dials by bastion forwarding, with zero direct destination dials.
- The [config tests](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/config/jump_resolution_tests.rs) confirmed ordered aliases, explicit login and port overrides and IPv6 resolution. Each descriptor revalidates its sources. Resolved endpoint cycles, nested routes and mixed proxy declarations fail closed. The [startup binding](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/jump_binding.rs) requires the approved private Linux fixture, keeps alternate network operations closed, and emits redacted leg and stage in the native diagnostic. Unselected startup still refuses active jump policy.
- **Focused validation.** The owner binary built on Debian. Both live cases passed. The config and POSIX master filters passed 72 and 22 tests on Debian, and 73 and 28 on macOS. The two pre-existing macOS live cases were outside this run. Crate formatting, shell syntax, direct file lengths and owner Clippy passed. Debian Clippy used --no-deps because a pre-existing unused function in the remote-agent dependency failed the strict dependency-inclusive run.
- **Remaining acceptance.** The approved pre-pinned native Linux lane serves the selected owner. The normal app still needs to select it and surface the route diagnostic. Route-scoped first-use trust approval and login rejection qualification remain open. Live multi-bastion and IPv6 routes, the separate restricted connector, port 22 and shipped defaults remain unverified. The renderer pick, trust, browse, save and reopen case for the existing direct alias remains open. A saved alias whose effective route changes still needs a product confirmation decision before dialing. S1–S3 and all release and cutover gates remain open.
