### S1 jump first-use trust, opt-in Linux fixture

- The [native observer](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/posix_master/jump/observe.rs) uses the protected fixture binary as a fixed, credential-free host-key lookup child. It records one presented Ed25519 key and returns no known-host records, leaving strict verification in force so the observation stops before user authentication. A downstream probe takes the existing Rust-owned forward through the verified prefix, with no direct-dial fallback or socket listener. User-supplied host lookup commands remain inadmissible. The authenticated handshake separately enforces the approved key.
- The [approval implementation](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/jump_binding/first_use.rs) binds the one-use handle to the selected leg, exact observation, effective route and trust-input stamps in the current owner and generation. Approval re-observes the same leg and revalidates the inputs before the append-only trust write. The picker clears a canceled browser handle before forwarding it to Rust. Connect/Disconnect revoke the native challenge. If probe cleanup cannot be confirmed, its reserved directory fences re-entry.
- The [live Debian integration](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevRuntime.jumpFirstUse.linux.test.ts) verified cancellation and replay refusal, stale approval after Disconnect, no destination socket attempt until the bastion was approved, zero destination authentications during its inspection and approval, preservation of an intervening conflicting destination pin, and browsing after both approvals. Changing the effective alias endpoint while a challenge was pending refused approval and further Browse with no additional authentication. The test covers the bound running owner; a general saved-alias retargeting decision across profiles remains open.
- **Normal Chromium dev flow.** On disposable Debian 13 arm64 with stock OpenSSH 10.0p2, `BIGBUD_SSH_OWNER=1 BIGBUD_SSH_OWNER_LINUX_JUMP=first-use-disposable BIGBUD_NO_BROWSER=1 bun run dev` selected the empty-pin fixture in the private network namespace. Chromium chose `workspace` from the actual SSH config picker. Canceling hop 1 left both pins empty. A fresh Browse displayed hop 1 again, then hop 2 only after hop 1 approval. Each written pin fingerprint equaled the one displayed at its prompt. The browser observed zero destination authentications before destination approval and successfully browsed the fixture after both approvals. The Debian [trace](/tmp/bigbud-jump-first-use-tIfiICyb/syscalls) counted three destination dials, zero before bastion approval, zero direct by the SSH clients and one between the two approvals for observation. The [destination prompt screenshot](/tmp/bigbud-jump-first-use-tIfiICyb/destination-challenge.png) and [Playwright driver](/home/bbdev/workspace-mode-proof.1MZti8/jump-first-use-browser.mjs) are on the same host. Earlier cold-browser runs timed out during render. The successful run aborted external Google Fonts requests in the network-isolated browser and exercised the real app and RPCs.
- The scoped Debian run passed all four first-use, cleanup and SQLite tests in three files. Its trace at `/tmp/bigbud-first-use-integration-AH5cQocI/syscalls` observed eight forwarded destination dials across the three fixtures and zero direct destination dials by the SSH clients. Focused macOS crate checks passed two jump config tests, four platform-default cases and nine wire/grant checks. The focused TypeScript picker and codec run passed 22 tests, and the launcher suite passed 25. Scoped formatting, lint, lengths, the server, web and contracts typechecks, and owner Clippy passed. Debian Clippy used the previously recorded dependency exclusion. The approved guest still has active stock GSSAPI and has no nc executable available for qualification. S2 authentication-in-flight cancellation and all release and cutover gates remain open.

## S3 replacement agent across backend process restart

The [replacement regression](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-agent/remoteAgentReplacement.acceptance.test.ts) ran real backend services in two separate Bun processes and real Linux arm64 agent supervisors with protocol 1.9. Both backend processes opened the same SQLite inode. The first instance returned a nonzero output cursor after the mutation ran, with the operation result still unresolved. The replacement appeared at the same private Unix socket with a new epoch and instance. The backend rejected stale recovery after hello, sent no attach or redispatch, and preserved the old persistence records. A fresh authorized epoch opened a new operation. The remote witness contained exactly one original mutation and one fresh mutation.

The test ran the native agent's real proxy in a network-disabled disposable container using the [backend fixture](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-agent/remoteAgentReplacement.acceptance.fixture.ts). The launcher was the container's stdio bridge. Full app startup, installed SSH transport composition and Files watch delivery or native snapshot cancellation remain separate. The three focused Vitest files passed six tests. Explicit formatting, lint, line counts and the server typecheck passed.

## Caller inventory

Local initiating server profiles are **D** (desktop packaged), **V** (desktop dev / Bun server dev), and **N** (standalone Node `bigbud`). **R** is deployed Linux backend. R has shared runtime layers, not a blanket SSH prohibition; confirmed R paths and still-open reachability are distinguished below. `runSshCommand`, `runSshLocalCommand`, resolved invocation fields passed to subprocesses, and PTY specifications are launch edges even if the final executor never references `ssh` by name.

| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| C01 D/V/N; R shared reachability open / SSH control                | [Discovery](../../apps/server/src/ssh/sshConfigDiscovery.ts) reads user/system includes; [target parsing](../../apps/server/src/ssh/sshExecutionTarget.ts) handles legacy aliases and structured auth/user/port/transport/key IDs; [builder](../../apps/server/src/ssh/sshCommand.ts) supplies argv and client env. Ambient OpenSSH can indirectly launch proxy/jump/config helpers or reuse an external master.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `listAliases`, immutable descriptor used by every admission. P01–P04: max include depth/file/size, same host but different identity, alias/non-default port, IPv6 and no ambient execution.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| C02 D/V/N; R shared reachability open / SSH control                | [Verification RPC](../../apps/server/src/ws/wsExecutionTargetVerification.ts) → [verification](../../apps/server/src/ssh/sshVerification.ts), [trust](../../apps/server/src/ssh/sshHostTrust.ts), [agent](../../apps/server/src/ssh/sshAgent.ts), [password session](../../apps/server/src/ssh/sshSession.ts). `ssh` trust and login probes, `ssh-keygen -y`, `ssh-add` discovery/load, `ssh-agent -s`; sync and async paths; askpass helper; `-O check/exit`, POSIX-style socket/master, password **and** keyboard-interactive eligible. ## Policy compatibility fixtures

These rows freeze what to compare, not executable fixtures or OS qualification. Preserve the [v1 effective-config policy](2026-11-22-rust-ssh-ownership-plan.md) as written; discrepancies become explicit review gates, not implicit ambient fallback.

| ID                                   | Comparison and required negative evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P01 descriptor and source loading    | Isolated legacy/structured target vectors with `Host` aliases/patterns/negation, user-before-system first-value and ordered additive identities, IPv6, explicit fields win, `HostKeyAlias`/custom port, include glob ordering and limits 16 levels/64 files/256 KiB each, nonmatching glob empty. Cycles, partial unreadable policy, unsupported expansion and applicable `Match exec` fail before DNS/helper/network/unlock. Non-executing `Match` works; unrelated stanza does not poison selected route. No unchecked `ssh -G`.                                                                                   |
| P02 trust/auth descriptor            | Multiple known-hosts files, hashed/port/alias/revoked/CA cases; approve only safe first user file, `sshTrustStoreReadOnly` if unwritable, never edit global file or bypass conflicts. Swap key between inspect/auth, change config between inspect/approve, stale generation/epoch, outside master and changed/revoked key reuse. Check exact rejection before credentials, no probe after durable Disconnect.                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| P03 routes                           | Two-hop `ProxyJump` positive, check each hop and final counts, reject changed hop before downstream auth, route mismatch/cycle and >4 hops. Approved custom absolute PATH qualified `nc %h %p` and absolute helper positive; substituted marker, extra flags/wrapper/shell and unsupported options negative; zero marker execution and no direct retry. No helper dependency on direct/jump paths.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| P04 platform default config/env      | Test shipped system defaults per OS; unsupported active directives are an explicit `sshConfigUnsupported` gate (source/directive/remedy, no value), helper substitution `sshRouteUnavailable`. Exercise owner overrides for control sockets, strict trust/UpdateHostKeys, no ambient forward/LocalCommand/RemoteCommand/KnownHostsCommand/agent-forward/environment, and only allowed `LANG`/`LC_*` where permitted; deny foreign grant before any transport work.                                                                                                                                                   |
| P05 separate key/passphrase/password | Isolated key files and selected agent socket including `IdentityAgent none`, key passphrase vs login password vs keyboard-interactive. POSIX one owner master, no retained login password; target/profile/generation key separation. Keyboard-interactive historical allowance is not qualification; OTP/MFA must receive no password. An already-supported combination lacking evidence blocks cutover absent explicit compatibility decision.                                                                                                                                                                      |
| P06 Windows                          | No ControlMaster; native askpass identity and request roles, early/foreign/late grant, 20 s grant expiry, 10 min user-idle vs background operations, sign/image and prompt isolation. Cache only one released credential plus positive acceptance in the dedicated strict password-only probe on the same child. Wrong password, no-prompt success without cache, partial/multi-method auth, post-release interruption and stale generation. Unqualified `windowsPasswordUnsupported` before collection; missing qualified helper `askpassUnavailable`; owner absent `sshOwnerUnavailable` and local work available. |

## Focused fixture and baseline queue

For **every** C/profile pair: record artifact/commit, OS/arch, runtime and resolved OpenSSH version, approved origin/target and case ID, redacted argv/env **shape**, executable identity/parent/exit/signal and spawn counts (including wrappers), operand schema/cap/result/error, cold/steady latency and teardown/reconnect duration. Capture leased stream behavior at EOF, cancellation and slow reader; record PTY survival as same durable binding plus the same process, not just a restored pane. Use isolated temp homes, only synthetic keys and owned loopback sshd when authorized for the next fixture pass. No real credentials, hostnames, user configs or ambient masters. The shared TS/Rust golden set must freeze wire bytes and normalized operand digests, not reuse untrusted shell input. Candidate wire artifacts now exist for the S1 control and picker slice only; see the recorder and contract evidence below.

### Focused baseline checks recorded

On the inspected checkout, server Vitest passed **31 tests across four files**: folder listing, SSH config discovery, host trust, and disconnect guard. These cover the existing local and mocked fixture behavior only. They do not exercise a real SSH transport, freeze cross-language wire bytes, measure lifecycle timing, or qualify a deployment profile.

Peer enrollment review found an authorization-preserving candidate mapping for G3: `ensurePeerCoordinatorKey()` returns only public metadata for a profile-bound dedicated identity, and `inspectPeerHostKey(host, port)` is a one-shot exact-target enrollment action that neither releases credentials nor saves a pin. Keep both separate from `openPeerRelay(pairingId)` and the worker's own grant. The backend retains explicit pin confirmation. The approved relay must use the same coordinator identity that its grant binds. The implementation plan adopts this catalog mapping; it is not owner implementation or runtime qualification.

Three focused peer tests passed (**9 tests**): pairing, permission narrowing, and the peer grant owner route. The pairing suite uses injected key generation and host-key inspection; this is baseline authorization evidence, not a live relay or host-key inspection test.

Git review confirmed G1 is in scope even for a local workspace: explicit fetch/pull/push and background status refresh can select an SSH repository remote, and PR checkout/materialization can delegate transport to Git or `gh`. The `gitRemoteTransport` contract binds the authorized action and repository to the effective SSH destination, repository path, target generation, and read or write service; a read grant cannot push. Rust owns the SSH stream; Git retains pack/ref semantics and the backend retains product authorization and workspace reservations. First qualify the effective fetch/push URLs, rewrites, multiple destinations and indirect process ancestry. `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` alone does not establish exclusivity. Keep G1 runtime parity open for custom Git transports: preserve required workflows through a qualified mapping or obtain an explicit compatibility decision before cutover; no silent protocol substitution, dropped destination, or ambient fallback.

Thread-command review found two different G2 authorities: `runThreadShell` binds the accepted user command to its thread and backend-resolved target/cwd; `runProviderWorkspaceShell` binds the provider invocation to its authorized workspace. Both carry only the intended remote shell text, never local executable or SSH options; neither uses the ACP grant. Fixed provider reads, writes, search, listing and patch operations should use typed workspace verbs, not pass the existing raw process envelope into the Rust owner. Exact limits and provenance differ across chat shell, shared MCP templates, Pi and the in-process Copilot tool. The legacy chat-shell active set is runner-instance-local, not proven single-flight across live dispatches. Record overlapping dispatches rather than asserting a shared lock. The plan adopts these mappings; wire and product-role evidence remain open.

The Git synchronization fixture passed **7 tests** against local repositories; the thread dispatch, remote-workspace callback, workspace authorization, and invocation-flight fixtures passed **10 tests**. Together with the earlier runs, this is **57 passing tests across 12 explicitly selected files**. These runs establish local or mocked baseline behavior only and do not qualify a new wire operation or a release profile.

### S0 recorder and contract evidence

- The [operation inventory](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/docs/plans/2026-11-22-rust-ssh-ownership-s0-operations.md) records legacy operands, authorization provenance, limits and errors for C01–C14 and G1–G3. The later terminal/provider supplement below records the corresponding limit and authority contracts; inventory evidence is not full production wire or parity evidence.
- The [release-profile and admission evidence](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/docs/plans/2026-11-22-rust-ssh-ownership-s0-profiles.md) distinguishes six standalone native packaging targets from clean Node installs. A deployed graph can reach shared folder-list and SSH verification routes despite its explicit denial of outbound deployment and client-relay launch. Neither finding qualifies those artifacts or their future SSH-owner discovery. The [offline release preflight](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/release-evidence/README.md) fingerprints a supplied artifact and enumerates missing trace references. A checkout server bundle was fingerprinted; no matching installer, standalone package archive, or deployed graph was found in the inspected checkout paths. A complete checklist still reports `preflight-only`. The installed older macOS arm64 package passed local signature verification but is the active live app and was not relaunched. An offline checkout standalone install failed. Both cached Linux architectures ran the checkout bundle's capability probe; no release-trusted deployed graph or Windows qualification guest was found. Exact prerequisites are in the profile note.
- **F10 / S1 slice:** The [wire verifier](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/README.md) checks 45 framed byte vectors, four directional length prefixes, and 11 operand digest cases in both TypeScript and Rust. Client-to-owner payloads are capped at 64 KiB and owner-to-client payloads at 8 MiB before payload allocation. The [Rust admission fixture](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/verify-rust/tests/grant_admission.rs) checks that only the current dispatcher can issue the approved grant, then checks class/scope/expiry, replay, and disconnected admission with a dummy transport. These preserve omitted vs empty path on the wire while normalizing both to home for the candidate digest. They distinguish explicit `truncated: false` from absent metadata. Malformed and denied-use vectors are not live owner admission tests. This was the initial wire checkpoint; later evidence below covers additional proof vectors and isolated enforcement, not full production wire or runtime coverage.
- **F01:** The [picker recorder](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshPicker.launchRecording.test.ts) records local child invocations in synthetic homes. The five focused cases cover alias discovery without a child, explicit-key preparation and folder listing, exact trust prompt matching, picker output/error limits, and no child after the process-local disconnect flag is set. The recorder itself does not prove real SSH authentication or durable Disconnect across restart.
- **F01 live baseline:** The [legacy picker loopback fixture](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshPicker.loopback.test.ts) exercised unknown-host denial, exact-key approval, two public-key-authenticated listings, and wrong-pin denial against a disposable sshd. The launcher recorded seven SSH and four key-probe invocations before Disconnect, and none after. On macOS 27.2.1 arm64, Node 25.2.1, and client/server OpenSSH 10.5p1, the single measured run saw 547 ms for unknown-host rejection, 135 ms for approval, 135/133 ms for the two listings, and 127 ms for wrong-pin rejection. These are local observations, not thresholds. The second listing was a fresh connection. A follow-up run used the actual SQLite store and folder/connection Effect handlers. With the storage scope closed and the in-memory registry reset, a new binding on the same DB rejected the picker with zero SSH or key-probe launches. Explicit Connect cleared the flag durably, and the next read succeeded. An independent read failed while the owned sshd was down and succeeded after restart on the same port and key, with no change to trust. This is storage rebinding and a fresh read after outage, not a whole backend process restart, a WebSocket auth check, or an in-flight reconnect proof.
- **F01/V-B:** The [direct-source Bun launch trace](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/server.sshOwnerBaseline.test.ts) exercised authenticated WebSocket listing and Disconnect with a synthetic SSH executable. It proved that a restarted backend uses the same DB file and disconnect timestamp and launches no more children for the target. Getting the source backend running exposed an eager Node-only SQLite import in Cursor usage credentials and an unnecessary password-socket directory at startup. Cursor now selects a matching read-only SQLite backend at read time, and the socket directory is created on demand for legacy password sessions. This is not a packaged-profile or Rust-owner trace.
- **F03/G1:** The [initiating Git recorder](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitCore.service.status.sshRecorder.test.ts) uses real Git pack/ref handling with a local recording executable in place of SSH. Local-only status launches no transport; refresh, fetch and pull request upload-pack at the rewritten read destination; one push requests receive-pack at both rewritten push URLs and updates both local bare repos. A follow-up verified the legacy precedence `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` → repository `core.sshCommand` → `GIT_SSH`, preserving the rewritten read destination. This does not cover actual OpenSSH for Git, `gh` delegation, or how the Rust owner will qualify custom transports.
- **F04/X2:** The [direct SSH terminal recorder](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal-host/localTerminalHost.directSsh.it.test.ts) verified a single synthetic `ssh -tt` child of the real local macOS arm64 native terminal host. This is not the packaged cross-platform or production fence proof.

A changed key launches only the trust probe. Four new cases passed; neither the actual WebSocket authorizer nor a remote deployment was exercised.

### Carry-forward reviewer cases (not S0 claims)

The [migration registry](../../apps/server/src/persistence/Migrations.ts) ends at 180 on this baseline; recheck slot 181 before adding the shared A/B migration. Both actual artifacts must have the same schema ceiling. For the later storage implementation, reuse the operational TypeScript [SqlClient connection reservation](../../apps/server/src/persistence/SqliteClient.connection.ts) for a top-level `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. The [Node SQLite worker bridge](../../apps/server/src/persistence/NodeSqliteClient.worker.bridge.ts) documents that interrupted waiters do not cancel accepted SQL; timeout is not proof of rollback. Settle the transaction or supersede its epoch transactionally before hydration and admission.

- **S2 lost committed Disconnect reply with a surviving native owner/PTY:** start a real PTY, commit Disconnect in the actual SQLite DB via the private TypeScript storage adapter, drop the committed reply, and restart the backend **without** killing the Rust owner or PTY first. Force storage rebind/hydration; verify Rust keeps admission closed, reconciles the committed flag/sequence, revokes the old target generation and its live leases before reopening admission to eligible targets, and neither reattaches nor spawns for the disconnected target. Check exactly-once revocation, stale writer/callback fencing and newer-generation lease isolation. An owner restart that happens to kill the process is not this test. TypeScript executes SQLite; Rust decides SSH admission and transport revocation.
- **S3/S4 A reads actual B pane records after drain:** generate real durable pane records with B, drain/retire B under the release fence and start the supported A binary against the same DB/WAL and real downgrade guard. Exercise A pane lookup/reattach/restore and verify a confirmed drained pane stays closed without resurrection. In a separate uncertain-termination case, preserve the unresolved reservation and refuse unsafe rollback/admission rather than dropping its fence or opening a replacement; prove the retained binding and no duplicate spawn. A mock or raw SQL compatibility read is not evidence of A's pane behavior.

- **G2 callback admission:** The [offline callback baseline](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerBaseline.callback.test.ts) verified malformed IDs and delayed invocations made stale by Stop/resume are rejected before the mocked SSH command runner. A fresh callback under the new generation does reach the runner. The inspected authority sources match `v0.4.0`; this does not prove termination of previously accepted remote work.

- **G2 cancellation after dispatch:** The [OpenCode callback abort fixture](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerBaseline.cancellation.test.ts) observed a 499 response and cancellation reaching the same synthetic target and invocation-specific control directory before a dispatched mocked command completed. This complements Stop before dispatch; it does not prove real remote process exit. Inspected sources match `v0.4.0`.

### Implementation follow-through (native final builds deferred)

- **S2 / trust:** The exact Ed25519 trust store now preserves bounded standalone comments and blank lines around a single plain or hashed pin; temporary SSH snapshots still contain only that exact pin. Competing records, CA/revocation markers, malformed records and unsafe files still fail closed. This is not ambient multi-file trust, GSSAPI, or password/askpass parity.
- **S2 / lifecycle:** The [owner's background health admission](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/reconnect.rs) applies bounded jittered delay after transient failures and pauses background probing on trust, authentication, or config refusal. A cooldown is unavailable, not a newly observed reachability failure. No retry or unlock is scheduled. Committed Disconnect rotates the fence before cleanup; terminal reap failure does not skip agent-proxy cleanup, and uncertain local reap prevents rebind acceptance. A redundant Connect with an owned terminal or live proxy is denied before the durable transition; the live terminal regression verified the original lease remains usable. This is not a generalized multi-consumer reconnect controller.
- **S3 / direct Git:** The [isolated live bridge](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.git.test.ts) verified staged/unstaged numstat, `origin/HEAD`, and the configured origin URL through fixed native recipes, in addition to porcelain status and the worktree probe. The new action and grant vectors match in both languages. These are local Git metadata reads on the remote workspace, not initiating fetch/push, `gh`, or arbitrary Git execution.
- **S3 / initiating Git:** The [tracked-branch push selection seam](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitCore.initiatingPush.ts) binds one approved SSH destination, resolved repository, exact refs and source object ID. Focused tests denied missing/stale approval, multiple effective push destinations, owner loss and ambiguous results without legacy retry. Native Git descendant enforcement, general fetch/push and `gh` remain open.
- **S3 / Pi, relay, deployment:** The typed [Pi lifecycle](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/Pi/RpcProcess.owner.ts), [client relay](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-backend/clientRelay.ssh.owner.ts), and [read-only deployment probe](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-backend/deployment.ssh.owner.ts) have focused source/adapter coverage. None is selected in production or backed by a qualified native recipe. The two focused relay receiver files passed all 13 lease cases in the disposable Linux guest after the Darwin run hit its intentional Linux-only guard. This was a component check, not the deferred final build or signed deployed graph.
- **S4 / rollback input:** The [offline matrix](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/release-evidence/matrix/README.md) now requires its own pane-record rollback reference per supported A/B cell. A generic DB rollback does not qualify; a confirmed closed pane must not respawn, and uncertain termination must retain the reservation and block unsafe rollback. The checker does not authenticate the references or accept S4.

### S1 continuous routed trace and S2 checked lease retirement

- **S1 / one continuous live trace:** The [authenticated Mac WebSocket trace](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.websocketLifecycle.test.ts) used stock OpenSSH 10.5p1 and an isolated literal IPv4 endpoint on a non-default port (debug owner SHA-256 `9d5f7c5a3e3a4c56d78c92565c202799835c80ff6cc345e2f699055f8dde59ee`). Unknown host produced a challenge without authentication; product approval wrote the exact pin, and a first read authenticated. Stopping the listener and letting its idle master expire produced a typed failure. Restoring the listener made no connection until a fresh read recovered on the same owner. Disconnect removed the owned lease and blocked further picker and monitor requests. After recreating the HTTP/WebSocket scope, restarting the native owner and reopening the same SQLite file, the disconnected checks added zero server connections, authentications, master leases, or legacy-recorder launches. Explicit Connect alone did not dial; the next read authenticated once and listed the expected folders. This is not a backend-executable restart, a DNS syscall audit, or an all-process execution trace; port 22 remains blocked.
- **S2 / retained child retirement:** The [new macOS live retirement case](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.posixMasterRetirement.test.ts) used two separate disposable OpenSSH masters. Stale generation and a mismatched kernel mux peer did not disturb the independent master. The test killed only the retained owned child, then used non-consuming `waitid` observations to verify exit was pending before checked invalidation and reaped afterward. Untrusted directory permissions blocked cleanup and replacement even after reap. With permissions restored, checked retirement removed the old lease and a new owned master answered mux. This demonstrates retirement when the live owner retains exact child authority, not adoption or automatic cleanup of an orphan after owner death.

### S2 open: automatic retirement after owner death

Automatic retirement remains disabled. The inherited OpenSSH stdin lock was rejected: the owner can die between spawn and the positive check, the check cannot prove SSH retains stdin until exit, and duplicating a writable descriptor does not make it read-only. A free lock is not proof of exit. The residual fence still refuses startup on uncertainty; the crash test's refusal is unchanged.

Unverified candidate for later evaluation: durably bind the process ID and start time to the spawned master, then read-only verify that this exact process instance no longer exists. Never signal or adopt it. Confirm that the evidence meets the plan's prohibition on ownership by PID alone, close the spawn-before-record crash window, and bind the Linux process namespace or refuse across a fresh namespace. This must be resolved before S4 activation, when a crash on product roots could otherwise require manual intervention. It does not block independent S1–S3 work.

### Native development verification (not release qualification)

- **macOS arm64:** The normal browser app exercised trust approval, workspace creation, the Files tree/text preview, and an interactive direct SSH terminal through Rust. Disconnect settled the pane's durable route and denied browsing after a renderer reload until explicit Connect. The user separately reported that the opted-in Electron dev app works. Neither run establishes general macOS SSH compatibility or release packaging.
- **Linux arm64:** A disposable Debian 13 guest built the owner natively and ran the normal app with system OpenSSH 10.0p2 in a private Bubblewrap namespace. Native Chromium verified first-use refusal, explicit pin approval, nested listing, workspace save, Files tree/text preview, durable Disconnect across a renderer reload, and explicit Connect. The [manual runbook](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/MANUAL-TEST.md#linux-normal-app-picker-browser-verified) records the exact startup and remaining lanes. No Linux Electron or terminal result is claimed.
- **Windows picker implementation:** The [Windows dispatcher](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/windows_picker.rs) reuses v1 grants, explicit trust, and the durable storage exchange over the verified pipe. The fixed transport resolves inbox OpenSSH and allows only the bound loopback public-key route. Idle waiting preserves the pipe without consuming bytes; a partial-frame timeout still retires it. The normal source dev launcher now accepts one operator-selected private fixture, and the native bridge retains and verifies the owner process across the named pipe before relaying v1 requests. The backend starts and hydrates that owner before exposing the browse-only picker; missing or invalid startup inputs do not start legacy SSH. Windows x64/arm64 cross-checks and focused simulated startup, fixture admission, and picker tests passed. Actual Windows startup, inbox authentication and listing, process-lineage evidence, askpass, and ConPTY remain open.
- **Installed Linux agent input:** The new guest produced and identified a native development agent at protocol 1.9 and verified inheritance of all three data descriptors through Bubblewrap. The [installed Linux proxy test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.agentProxyInstalledLinux.test.ts) passed twice: the fixed, startup-bound `--proxy` route reached the same supervisor epoch and instance seen directly over the private Unix socket. Ending proxy stdin produced EOF and exit 0 while the same supervisor remained reachable. This is native development evidence, not normal production composition or release qualification.

### Isolated implementation while release evidence is pending

Local proof and release qualification are separate. The S0 closure covers only contracts and local baselines. The approved default-off dev-only composition may exercise the product path in a separate dev ownership domain before handoff: picker, then files, then terminal. Release builds keep it disabled even with a dev override. Do not connect the proof owner to a production target or caller, dispatch production writes to its private store, activate S4, or start transfer M0 on local evidence.

## W3.2 drain-on-quit and W3.6 startup refusal (25 November 2026)

Focused implementation of the Build A drain core and the Build B startup-refusal core. No installer, updater, CLI `--for-upgrade`, systemd `ExecStop`, production B activation or native owner run was exercised; automatic retirement remains disabled and no product SSH path was promoted.

- **Drain core / U5/U8.** [sshLegacyDrain.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshLegacyDrain.ts) accepts only `installer`, `updater` or `cli-for-upgrade`; any other trigger returns `unsupported` before a service stops. It then stops monitors, relays and providers, lists panes, asks for consent, closes each recorded pane, exits only A's own masters, commits the retirement record and marker, checkpoints the WAL, and releases the lock. Direct-SSH panes drain even in keep mode; local panes are untouched; remote-agent panes are preserved when U6 keep mode is on. Cancel closes nothing, writes no marker and releases no lock. A close or master failure before commit writes no marker and releases nothing; the whole pre-commit sequence is bounded to 15 s by default.
- **Owned masters only.** [sshSession.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshSession.ts) exposes the masters recorded in `passwordSessionByExecutionTargetId`; the split [sshSession.ownedMasters.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshSession.ownedMasters.ts) runs `ssh -O exit` only for those entries. It never scans disk or ambient configuration, and a missing socket is already closed. The askpass writer moved to its own module to keep `sshSession.ts` inside the 400-line limit.
- **Migration 185 / Build A ceiling.** [185_SshLegacyRetirement.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Migrations/185_SshLegacyRetirement.ts) adds `ssh_legacy_retirement` and `ssh_legacy_retired_marker`, with a foreign key so the marker cannot exist without its record. It is registered after 184 in [Migrations.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Migrations.ts); 184 is unchanged. [LegacyRetirement.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Layers/LegacyRetirement.ts) writes both rows in one `withTransaction`, treats a same-artifact re-run as `already-retired`, refuses a different artifact after retirement, and checkpoints the WAL before returning. A busy checkpoint is a typed failure; the caller therefore does not release the lock.
- **Consent UI.** [SshLegacyDrainDialog.tsx](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/web/src/components/terminal/SshLegacyDrainDialog.tsx) lists thread/pane identity and route with only Cancel, Save and close, and Drain now. Cancel and Escape abort without draining; retry re-lists after an error; there is no restart affordance. With `reason: "startup-refusal"` it shows the W3.6 fence and the explicit **Drain identified panes** action instead of upgrade wording. The component takes a transport-supplied flow and performs no drain itself; installer/updater and systemd composition remain W3.3/W3.4.
- **W3.6 startup refusal.** [sshOwnerStartupRefusal.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerStartupRefusal.ts) reads the marker before an owner start. Missing marker and non-empty pending items produce the typed `legacyEntryFenced` (reasons `retirement-marker-missing` and `legacy-panes-pending`); a missing owner after a valid marker produces `sshOwnerUnavailable`, and the schema-too-new error remains separate. `startSshOwnerAfterLegacyGate` never evaluates the owner start on any refusal, and the explicit `drainIdentified` action is never invoked by startup. The production B composition is deferred to W3.7.
- **Web copy.** [ssh.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/web/src/lib/ssh.ts) maps `legacyEntryFenced` to a distinct message that names the explicit pane drain; `sshOwnerUnavailable` still maps to no ownership message.
- **Tests run.** `sshOwnerPickerProof.legacyPtyDrain.test.ts` (8 tests: order, unsupported trigger, Cancel, keep/U6 filtering, save-then-close, partial close failure, master failure, owned-only `-O exit`), `legacyRetirementMarker.test.ts` (6: one-transaction rollback via trigger, idempotence/conflict, WAL truncation on a file DB, commit-then-release order, artifact conflict keeping the lock, crash-before-marker), `server.sshOwner.startupRefusal.test.ts` (5: missing marker, valid marker, partially drained, owner missing, explicit drain action), `185_SshLegacyRetirement.test.ts` (1), plus `Manager.quitPolicy.test.ts`, `localTerminalHost.directSsh.owner.quit.test.ts`, `Manager.lifecycle.test.ts`, `Manager.hostLifecycle.test.ts` and the four `sshSession` files. The combined focused run passed 65 tests in 16 files (including the 20 new tests, quit-policy/direct-SSH, lifecycle, all four `sshSession` files, and the 184/downgrade/rollback layer tests). The dialog browser file passed 7 tests (including the startup-refusal variant); `apps/web/src/lib/ssh.test.ts` passed 8.

## W3.3 X2 host verbs and W3.4 ExecStop contract (25 November 2026)

Focused implementation of the terminal-host retirement protocol and the systemd/CLI stop contract. W3.4's production backend drain hook is **not** installed; the command reports `drain-hook-unavailable` rather than writing an empty marker. No systemd unit was installed, no real upgrade ran, and automatic retirement remains disabled.

- **Protocol 1.10 / additive frames.** [terminal_host.proto](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/remote-agent/terminal_host.proto) adds inventory and `retireSshSpawn` messages, imported by [v1.proto](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/remote-agent/v1.proto) as frames 128–131; existing field numbers are unchanged. `pty.proto` adds optional thread/pane/kind fields 18–20 to `PtyCreateRequest`. Both `PROTOCOL_MINOR` constants and the shared client/agent hello goldens moved from 9 to 10. The capability is `terminal.host-retirement` v1, advertised only by a local desktop terminal host.
- **Inventory semantics.** [terminal_host.rs](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-remote-agent/src/session/terminal_host.rs) returns at most 64 panes sorted by PTY id, with exact `lease_id == pty_id`, `thread_id`/`pane_id` stored at create time, `kind` (`local` or `managed-ssh`), the host artifact id (`version@digest`) and per-pane retirement eligibility. `truncated` is explicit; zero or >64 `max_panes` is refused. No paths, environment values or secrets cross the wire.
- **Retirement semantics.** The request is all-or-nothing and requires the exact epoch and build digest plus 1–64 unique bounded lease ids. Every lease must be an exact live managed-SSH pane; a foreign, tampered, duplicate, local or reaped lease refuses the whole request (`HOST_LEASE_MISMATCH` / `HOST_RETIREMENT_INVALID`) without state change. On success the host writes `ssh-spawn-retired` under its private state root before acknowledging, refuses later managed-SSH creates with `SSH_SPAWN_RETIRED`, and keeps local panes usable. A host restart reads the marker, so retirement survives. A missing host, non-desktop host or wrong epoch/build is typed and never inferred from missing panes.
- **TS client.** [localTerminalHost.retirement.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal-host/localTerminalHost.retirement.ts) validates capability, epoch, lease equality, the 64-pane bound, string bounds and the exact acknowledgement. A host without the capability raises `TerminalHostRetirementUnsupportedError`, which is the signal for B to require host exit. The capability is re-exported beside [localTerminalHost.launch.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal-host/localTerminalHost.launch.ts). Terminal-host creators now pass thread/pane identity through the protocol 1.10 create fields.
- **W3.4 CLI/unit contract.** [cli.backend.drain.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/cli/cli.backend.drain.ts) acquires the offline lock, runs the drain and returns only on a committed drain; the drain owns the release, and refused/cancelled/failed paths release nothing. `backend stop --for-upgrade` is parsed only for `stop` and passes `forUpgrade` to the native operation; a non-stop flag use exits 1 without native work. Follower sets return a typed refusal rather than being abandoned. The deployed unit renders one `ExecStop={launcher} --backend-service drain {path}` before `ExecStopPost`; the Rust action invokes the installed CLI with `backend stop --for-upgrade`. Because the production hook is absent, that CLI path currently fails `drain-hook-unavailable` with no marker.
- **Tests run.** TS: `localTerminalHost.retireSshSpawn.test.ts` 5, `remoteAgentProtocol.terminalHost.test.ts` 5, `cli.backend.drainOnStop.test.ts` 6, the new `cli.backend.test.ts` flag case plus existing cases (17 across the CLI pair), and the focused six-file protocol/CLI/host run 58 passed. The full `src/terminal-host` directory plus the W3.2 drain/marker/refusal/migration tests passed 147 with 16 platform skips across 25 files. Rust: protocol 24 library + 4 integration tests, `terminal_host` 4, `backend_service` 71. Server typecheck passed; `cargo fmt` and scoped strict Clippy for `bigbud-protocol` and `bigbud-remote-agent` passed.
- **Written but not run.** A real stock terminal host receiving the verbs, a real systemd `ExecStop`, a real `backend stop --for-upgrade` success, the installer/updater hook and peer-coordinator follower drain. The Rust lifecycle tests use in-process PTYs on macOS, not the packaged Linux host.

## W3.4 backend drain endpoint and CLI consent (25 November 2026)

Added the authenticated same-user backend drain control surface and wired `backend stop --for-upgrade` to it. The live backend drain handler is still unregistered, so the command fails `unavailable` rather than writing an empty marker. No systemd unit was installed and no real upgrade ran.

- **Endpoint shape.** [backendDrain.protocol.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/startup/backendDrain.protocol.ts), [backendDrain.server.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/startup/backendDrain.server.ts) and [backendDrain.client.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/startup/backendDrain.client.ts) define a Unix-socket-only control channel under `<stateDir>/control/backend-drain.sock`, mode 0600, plus a per-process 32-byte token in `backend-drain.token` (0600, same-uid and owner-checked on read). Requests are one newline-delimited JSON object, at most 8 KiB, admitted within 2 s, and must carry protocol 1 plus the exact token. There is no TCP/remote listener, no ambient token and no unauthenticated path. Statuses are `drained`, `consent-required`, `busy`, `unknown-artifact`, `timeout`, `cancelled`; errors are `invalid-request`, `unauthorized`, `unavailable`, `failed`.
- **Handler registration and serialization.** [backendDrain.handler.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/startup/backendDrain.handler.ts) is the process-local registry. A second concurrent drain returns `busy` without invoking the handler; an unregistered backend returns `consent-required` for UI consent and `unavailable` for non-interactive consent; `isBackendDrainRunning` exposes the in-progress state for admission tests. [backendInstance.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/startup/backendInstance.ts) starts the socket under the held lock and closes it with the backend; a socket failure is logged and leaves local features working.
- **CLI.** `backend stop --for-upgrade` accepts `--i-have-saved-my-work`; both flags are rejected on other subcommands. With the flag the request records `non-interactive` consent; without it the request asks for UI consent and a non-drained status exits non-zero. Foreign token, unknown artifact, concurrent drain, cancellation, timeout and failure all return distinct typed reasons with no marker. The offline `runBackendForUpgradeStop` path remains for injected/offline tests; the real CLI no longer returns `drain-hook-unavailable` before reaching the endpoint.
- **Follower request.** `requestFollowerDrain(count, coordinator)` calls a real coordinator function; a partial count or rejected request returns a typed refusal and never abandons followers. The live coordinator drain function is not registered.
- **Tests run.** `backend.drainEndpoint.auth.test.ts` 5 (owner-only socket/token, foreign token, unknown artifact, concurrent busy/serialization, missing handler); `cli.backend.drainOnStop.production.test.ts` 6 (endpoint reachable, consent-required without flag, success with flag, distinct cancelled/timeout/unknown statuses with no marker, follower supported/partial/unreachable); `cli.backend.test.ts` 11 (including flag parsing); `cli.backend.drainOnStop.test.ts` 6. A combined focused run of these plus the W3.2 drain/marker/startup/migration tests passed 48. Explicit-path formatting, lint and the 400-line limit passed. Server typecheck had no errors in this pass's files; the repository-wide command was blocked by concurrent `git/Layers/**` and `provider/Layers/**` edits owned by other agents.
- **Written but not run.** A real systemd `ExecStop`, a real live backend drain handler, the installer/updater hook, a real peer coordinator drain and a real upgrade. The endpoint is Unix-only; Windows drain control is not implemented.

## W3.4 live backend drain handler (25 November 2026)

Registered the live drain handler under the backend lock and connected it to the existing W3.2 core. UI consent is represented by a real pending-drain registry; the WebSocket transport and real service stop effects remain open.

- **Registration point.** [backendDrain.live.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/startup/backendDrain.live.ts) is composed around `RuntimeDependenciesLive` in [server.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/server.ts), registers via [backendDrain.handler.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/startup/backendDrain.handler.ts), and unregisters on layer teardown. It requires the terminal manager, SQLite and the ownership-shutdown service.
- **Pane enumeration.** `TerminalManager.listDrainPanes` was added to [Manager.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal/Services/Manager.ts)/[Manager.session.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal/Layers/Manager.session.ts) and classifies each live session as `local`, `direct-ssh` or `remote-agent`, carrying the current keep-mode setting. The handler maps exact `threadId:terminalId` identities into the W3.2 pane model; direct SSH drains even in keep mode, local never, remote-agent only when keep mode is off.
- **Consent.** [backendDrain.consent.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/startup/backendDrain.consent.ts) publishes exactly one pending snapshot with pane list and deadline. `decideBackendDrain` accepts one decision; late/duplicate/unknown ids return `refused`. No UI subscriber yields `consent-required`; cancel/timeout returns before the core, so no pane closes, master exits, marker or release occurs. The existing dialog component was exercised against the pending/decision shape in `SshLegacyDrainDialog.consent.browser.tsx`.
- **Core order.** The handler calls `runLegacyDrain` with `cli-for-upgrade`: stop callbacks, pane closes, `drainOwnedSshMasters`, `commitLegacyRetirement` (single record+marker transaction + WAL checkpoint), then `releaseLock`, which requests backend shutdown so the lock is released by the normal finalizer. Unknown artifacts, a second concurrent drain, commit failure and shutdown absence are typed failures before/around the core with no marker on pre-commit failure.
- **Offline rule and followers.** `planOfflineDrain(0)` permits an offline drain; any positive count refuses `offline-panes-unknown`. `requestFollowerDrain` requires an exact coordinator count and refuses partial/rejected/unreachable sets without abandoning followers.
- **Tests run.** `backendDrain.live.test.ts` 6 (classification/order, unknown artifact, concurrent busy, no-UI consent-required, cancel no-marker, one-decision proceed, commit failure), `backend.drainEndpoint.auth.test.ts` 5, `cli.backend.drainOnStop.production.test.ts` 7, `cli.backend.drainOnStop.test.ts` 6, `cli.backend.test.ts` 11, plus the W3.2 drain/marker and manager lifecycle tests; focused combined run 58 passed. `SshLegacyDrainDialog.consent.browser.tsx` 2 passed. Windows drain control remains unsupported.

[rpc.backendDrain.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/packages/contracts/src/server/rpc.backendDrain.ts) adds the additive RPCs; no existing field names or numbers changed.
- **Server handlers.** [wsRpcHandlers.backendDrain.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsRpcHandlers.backendDrain.ts) reads `pendingBackendDrain` (reconnect redelivery) and calls `decideBackendDrain` exactly once. Unknown/late ids return `already-decided` when the id was decided, otherwise a typed `BackendDrainConsentError`; decisions are bounded and typed. The WS route remains the existing authenticated `/ws` path; a missing token is refused before any handler.

## W2 caller tracks: T-git, T-prov, T-shell (25 November 2026)

Selected dev domain only. No product-path promotion, no legacy fallback. Each track is partial where stated; nothing here is S3 or S4 acceptance.

- **T-git.** Typed mutation verbs were added for `restoreTrackedPaths`, `cleanUntrackedPaths` and `stagePaths`, plus discard's status and toplevel reads (`ProofWorkspaceGit.paths = 3`; actions 7–12). Recipes use exact argv, `:(top,literal)` relative paths only, 30 s / 1 MB caps, and refuse raw options, env, stdin and timeout overrides. Untracked line counts are accurate for up to 32 paths through one bounded `git diff --no-index`, with a typed `{counted:false, reason}` at the Git layer — the public status contract still renders `0/0`, so the UI cannot yet distinguish those. Explicit fetch and background upstream refresh go through `InitiatingFetchOwner` with the existing 5 s / 15 s / 5 s timing; the push binding is product-composed. Ambient `GIT_SSH*` variables are refused without launching. **Still refused:** `pull`, PR fetch and `gh` SSH traffic, because no owner fetch/push stream service or `git_remote_transport` product service is composed; native lease cancellation and an explicit `outcomeUnknown` for mutations are not implemented. Checks: 131 focused tests, 7 TS and 3 Rust goldens, Rust workspace-git 8, scoped Clippy. Live SSH written-not-run.
- **T-prov.** The Pi owner seam is product-imported behind the dev selection: selected remote starts use the typed lease (pipes, exit, cancel) with frozen operands and no SSH spawn, and the capability probe is a bounded typed `probeProvider` (5 s, 4 KiB) instead of `execFile`. `PiRpcProcess.child` is optional so no consumer receives a fabricated child. OpenCode v1 is composed the same way with the owner-selected listener and confirmed cancel; the legacy remote child now hits the fence, and the v2 refusal is unchanged. The Codex synchronous version probe was deleted; the sync launcher throws `REMOTE_CODEX_SYNC_LAUNCH_REFUSED` and the async path keeps "cancel during probe suppresses spawn". Checks: 144 focused, Pi 63, OpenCode 585 (3 skipped), Codex 80, server typecheck, protocol goldens, scoped Clippy. **Not parity:** the Rust provider grant is startup-pinned to one proof fixture and the dev runtime binds no provider fixture, so real Pi/OpenCode sessions get a typed refusal; Pi session-file resume is refused by the recipe. Both are reported, not silently dropped.
- **T-shell.** `run_acp_command` (69), `run_thread_shell` (67) and `run_provider_workspace_shell` (68) were added with replies 41–43 and errors 32 (`SHELL_TIMED_OUT`) / 33 (`SHELL_CANCELLED`) in the new [v1.shell.proto](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/v1.shell.proto). Thread and provider shells use a fixed recipe with 30 s default / 120 s ceiling, 8 MiB combined output with an explicit `truncated` flag, one active command per thread, lease cancellation returning `shellCancelled`, and a typed timeout; ACP stays `pwd`-only with output, exit and cancel, stdin ignored and no interactive conversion. ACP is interactive-class and both shells are background-class; Rust and golden vectors prove the grants are not substitutable across roles. Checks: 16 passed / 1 skipped across the three named files, 36 related, Rust shell 10, protocol 9 TS / 3 + 7 Rust. **Limit:** the product ACP bridge still advertises `terminal: false` because no approved-terminal producer exists, so C11 remains an owner capability rather than product parity.

## W3.4 drain consent over WebSocket and coordinator drain (25 November 2026)

- **Contracts.** `server.getBackendDrainConsent` returns the pending `drainId`, at most 64 panes with thread/terminal identity, surface, keep mode and `deadlineAt`, with no paths or secrets; `server.decideBackendDrainConsent` accepts exactly `cancel`, `save-and-close` or `drain-now`. Results are `accepted`, `already-decided` or a typed `BackendDrainConsentError`.
- **Semantics.** Reads always return the current pending snapshot, so a reconnect re-delivers the same drain instead of starting one. Decisions route through the existing `decideBackendDrain` registry; a second client gets `already-decided`; cancel resolves before any close, master exit, commit or release. The root UI mounts `SshLegacyDrainConsentHost`, which polls, renders the existing dialog and sends one decision.
- **Coordinator.** `PeerDispatchService.drainFollowers` drains unsettled dispatches through the existing peer cancel path and wakes `cancel-pending` followers; it fails typed on an unknown dispatch and the live handler treats local mode as zero followers. Partial, rejected and unreachable sets stay typed refusals.

- **Still open.** Monitor/relay/provider stop effects remain placeholders — the real teardown happens at the shutdown that follows the drain. The installer/updater hook, a real systemd install, a real upgrade run, a deployed coordinator run and Windows drain control were not exercised.

The TS generator and the shared frame and digest were corrected to the canonical ascending encoding; the Rust vectors were completed to emit `proof_opencode_service_open` and `proof_pi_open_resume`.
