## W3.7 per-target production admission (25 November 2026)

Protocol-and-owner-core slice only. Legacy TypeScript SSH executors are untouched, `SSH_OWNER_PRODUCTION_ACTIVATION` stays off, and no commit, tag or release artifact was produced. Host: macOS arm64 developer machine with the stock `/usr/bin/ssh` image; **no live multi-host SSH endpoint was available**, so every multi-target result below is a disposable fake owner (TS) or a registry/admission unit test (Rust), not a live SSH run.

- **Wire (additive only).** `Request.target_descriptor = 6` carries `TargetDescriptor` (`target_id` 1, `kind` 2, `host` 3, `port` 4, `user` 5, `auth_mode` 6, `identity_reference` 7, `host_key_alias` 8, `trust_reference` 9, `generation` 10). Bounds: encoded descriptor ≤ 4 KiB, target id 1–1024 B, host an IP literal ≤ 45 B, user 1–64 B of `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, absolute literal identity/trust paths ≤ 1024 B and distinct, host-key alias ≤ 255 B, generation 0 or 32 B. No credential bytes, raw config text or transport options cross the wire. `HelloAccepted.multi_target = 7` reports an owner started without a pinned target and with no startup generation. New typed errors `ERROR_CODE_TARGET_UNKNOWN = 34` and `ERROR_CODE_TARGET_MISMATCH = 35`. Goldens: `target_descriptor_request`, `hello_accepted_multi_target`, `error_target_unknown`, `error_target_mismatch` on both sides plus one grant digest vector (`v1.golden.grants`), computed from the canonical operand rule.
- **Startup contract.** Production argv is `--production --profile <binding> --state-dir <dir> --ssh <path> --keyscan <path> [--system-config <path>] [--alias-config <path>] [--attach-socket <path>] [--workspace-root <path>]`. The eight fixture positionals (`<target> <ssh> <keyscan> <host> <port> <user> <identity> <known-hosts>`) and every fixture flag are `production_fixture_conflict`; `--system-config` requires `--alias-config`. The profile binding is still recomputed from the state directory, and the profile lock is unchanged.
- **Admission model.** A `Registry` caches one resolved `(transport, trust, storage, generation)` per target id. Grant issuance validates and caches a new descriptor (bounds, key-only auth, alias or structured kind, identity/trust files, images, policy) with no dial, mints the target generation, and binds the grant to the exact target id and generation. Consumption re-checks the target (`TARGET_MISMATCH`) and generation (`STALE_GENERATION`), a descriptor for the same id with a different digest is `TARGET_MISMATCH`, an unknown descriptorless target is `TARGET_UNKNOWN`, and the registry caps at 8 targets. The active target's transport, trust, storage, generation and reconnect state are moved into the owner for the length of the request and swapped back afterwards, so every existing dispatch path observes its exact target. Fixture mode keeps its one pinned entry and the descriptorless path, exercising the same code.
- **Per-target durable state.** Each target hydrates its own writer epoch/sequence/disconnect snapshot through the authenticated pipe on its first non-local request; a failed or refused snapshot answers `STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE` and ends that session. `rebind` invalidates every cached snapshot and re-hydrates the bound target. **Deviation from "hydration unchanged":** the startup snapshot acknowledgement now covers only unresolved categories (retirement mirror, pane bindings); the writer epoch, sequence, disconnect flag and alias binding are checked per target at its first admitted request, because a target-free owner has no startup target to acknowledge. The owner enforces the check, so it cannot be skipped by the client.
- **Client.** The production client spawns and attaches without a target (`resolveSshOwnerLaunchFacts` carries an optional primary descriptor for hydration and attach verification only), sends a descriptor with every target-scoped request, and exposes `handle.target(descriptor)` for per-target requests. A production client refuses an owner that reports `multi_target = false` as `version-incompatible`. Attach and the attach capability are unchanged.
- **Checks.** Owner crate: `cargo fmt --check` clean, strict Clippy clean, 65 library + 425 binary (6 ignored) + 8 + 2 integration tests. Protocol: 10 TS goldens and 3 + 9 Rust goldens/admission tests. Server: 37 focused tests passed and 6 live-gated tests skipped across 15 files, server typecheck clean, explicit-path `oxfmt`/`oxlint` clean on 27 files, no changed file above 400 lines. One pre-existing failure was reproduced on a clean `HEAD` worktree and is not part of this change: `sshOwnerPickerProof.client.windows.test.ts` expects a profile echo its fake dispatcher never sends.
- **Remaining before activation.** No live two-host run; per-target ControlMaster/PTY/agent/OpenCode leases are still fixture-singleton objects (`picker_master`, `terminal`, `agent_proxy`, `opencode_lease`) because production only admits trust/connect/disconnect/list/health/alias verbs and all other verbs are refused with `operation_denied`; Windows keeps a single pinned target; alias descriptor binding is verified when the target is first used, not at boot; and neither the activation flag nor any legacy executor changed.

## W3.7 per-target lane state (25 November 2026)

Owner-core slice only, on top of the per-target admission slice. Legacy TypeScript SSH executors, the activation flag, the production verb allowlist and every wire field are unchanged, and no commit, tag or release artifact was produced. Host: macOS arm64 with the stock `/usr/bin/ssh`; **no live multi-host SSH endpoint was available**, so no concurrent live master, PTY, proxy or OpenCode run is claimed.

- **Per-target lanes.** `owner::targets::Lanes` now holds the POSIX master picker, PTY lease and reservation, agent proxy lease, OpenCode lease, and the two uncertainty flags. `Slot` carries them beside `transport`, `storage`, `trust`, `reconnect` and `generation`, and `enter_target`/`leave_target` swap them with the requested target. Two resolved targets therefore hold independent lane state, and a cancel, trust loss, ambiguous write or generation change for one target cannot reach the other's lanes. Fixture mode keeps its one pinned target and its startup-created master, so its behaviour is unchanged. An owner-wide writer handoff or launcher retirement tears down every target's lanes (`revoke_all_network_leases`), while a durable Disconnect tears down only the bound target (`revoke_network_leases`).
- **PTY identity.** `TerminalPreparation` and `LiveTerminal` carry a `LeaseScope` of the exact target plus generation. Cancel, open, status, input, resize, replay and close all reject a foreign target or a stale generation with `operation_denied`; the target's lanes are also never visible to another target. Replay bounds and the 75 s proof / 30 min dev lifetime are unchanged. `PickerProofBoundTarget` now also exposes the terminal client, so each bound target uses its own generation for terminal requests.
- **Grant budget.** `Grants` keeps the owner-wide `Budget` and adds one `Budget` per target scope (bounded to 8 scopes; owner-scoped alias discovery shares one bucket). Issue admits both, consume/expiry/revocation release both. A noisy target reaches its own background ceiling (`24` of `32` with the default reserve) while its own interactive reserve and every other target's reserve stay available; the global ceiling is unchanged and still cannot be spent by background work.
- **Alias re-verification.** Every resolution of an already-cached descriptor now re-runs `TargetAuthority::bind`, which re-reads the owner alias policy and compares host, port, user, identity and trust with the descriptor. A changed policy drops only that target's cached transport, storage and live lanes and refuses typed (`config_unsupported`); the descriptor digest, target id and generation stay, so a later resolution can re-bind without a false `stale_generation`. Other targets keep their transport, generation and lanes.
- **Still fixture-only, and therefore still refused in production.** The POSIX master lane validates a disposable loopback fixture (`validate_fixture_for_auth`), the PTY runs the proof command, the agent proxy consumes the three startup-transferred descriptors, and OpenCode needs its fixture or product recipe, none of which a target-free production owner starts with. Production therefore still admits only trust/connect/disconnect/list/health/alias; all other verbs answer `operation_denied` before a lane is touched, rather than sharing one fixture resource across targets. The agent proxy can hold one live lease at a time per process because startup transfers a single pipe set, so two concurrent agent proxies are not supported even though lease state is per target. Per-target masters can only exist once a product master lane is admitted for a non-fixture host.
- **Checks.** `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy -p bigbud-ssh-owner --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test -p bigbud-ssh-owner` green with 65 library + 433 binary (6 ignored) + 8 + 2 integration tests, including 8 new focused tests (lease scope, lane isolation, alias re-verification, per-target budget reserve, two-picker isolation, and three owner-level PTY identity tests). Protocol goldens unchanged and green on both sides (10 TS, 3 + 9 Rust) because no field changed. Server: `sshOwner.productionTarget.leases.test.ts` plus the existing per-target, policy, spawn, attach, hydration, lifecycle and launch files pass (28/28 across 8 files in the focused re-run); `bun run --cwd apps/server typecheck` exits 0; explicit-path `oxfmt`/`oxlint` clean on the three changed TS files; no changed file above 400 lines.
- **Remaining before activation.** No live two-host run and no live concurrent lease evidence. Windows still keeps one pinned target. Production masters, PTY, agent proxy and OpenCode need product resource providers and their own admission change before the allowlist can grow. Auto-retirement, the structural checker's S4 mode and activation itself are unchanged.

## W3.7 production master and PTY providers (25 November 2026)

Owner-core provider slice. Legacy TypeScript SSH executors, the activation flag and every wire field are unchanged, and no commit, tag or release artifact was produced. Host: macOS arm64 with the stock `/usr/bin/ssh`; a disposable loopback sshd was created through the existing sandboxed test fixture for the live run below.

- **Production master.** A resolved target's `MasterPicker` now has a `Production` mode. It validates the bound system image (`/usr/bin/ssh` identity, revalidated file stamp), the target's own policy, identity files and explicit known-hosts pin, with the fixture's `validate_fixture_for_auth` loopback requirement removed. The control path is a fresh 0700 `bbcm-*` directory under the owner's state root (`<state>/ssh-owner`), with every ancestor checked as root- or owner-owned and not group/other writable. The state root is shared by every target, so it is not scanned for `bbcm-*` residue (that would confuse one target's live master with another's); the retirement journal and the per-lease kernel peer/inode check remain the crash fence. The command keeps `-F /dev/null`, strict host-key checking, no ambient config/agent, `env_clear`, and the 10 s bounded ready deadline. The first folder list opens the master and later lists reuse the same process; the existing retirement record (`Kind::Master`) is written before spawn.
- **Production PTY.** A production reservation keeps the request's own absolute remote cwd and dimensions, and requires reservation-before-dispatch (the empty-lease fixture proof path is refused in production). The open path revalidates the production image, pins the target's trust snapshot, and runs the target's `"${SHELL:-/bin/sh}" -l` after a quoted `cd`, printing an owner launch witness before `exec`. It never uses the fixture proof command. The 30-minute hard lifetime, 64 KiB sample/replay retention and per-target/generation `LeaseScope` are unchanged.
- **Admission.** Production admits `prepare_terminal`, `cancel_terminal_reservation`, `open_terminal`, `terminal_status`, `terminal_replay`, `terminal_input`, `terminal_resize`, `terminal_cwd` and `close_terminal` in addition to trust/connect/disconnect/list/health/alias. The agent proxy and OpenCode verbs still answer `operation_denied`. The fixture master's restrictive admission gate now applies only to fixture lanes, so production can list folders and hold a terminal at the same time.
- **Refusals and ownership.** Unsupported image, an absent pin, a changed/multiple host-key record, an unresolved alias, a policy-rejected alias and a wrong-mode identity all refuse before a master child is spawned; a resolved good target keeps its transport, generation and lanes. Stale generations cannot attach, close or reuse a master or PTY. A target policy change drops only that target's transport/snapshot/lanes and, in production only, clears the old master trust pause after a confirmed reap. Fixture admission, loopback restrictions and trust pauses are unchanged.
- **Live evidence (run).** `owner::posix_picker::production_live_tests::production_master_is_reused_and_the_login_pty_round_trips` ran against a disposable sandboxed loopback sshd: trust inspected verified, the production master listed folders twice on the same retained process, and a login-shell PTY accepted input, resized 80×24 → 100×40, replayed the echoed marker and closed with a checked reap. Replacing the pinned host key with a different valid key made the next list answer `host_key_changed` and tear down only that master. The test stays `#[ignore]`d for ordinary runs and is gated by `BIGBUD_SSH_OWNER_PRODUCTION_FIXTURE=approved-disposable` plus root/port/user env; it was run explicitly for this slice.
- **Tests written, not live.** `production_terminal_tests` (reservation/cwd, no fixture shell, stale generation, workspace-root fence, per-target reservations, production list lane, fixture/production admission parity), `posix_picker.production.tests` (unsupported image, changed host key, absent pin, per-target generation), `targets.lanes.tests::production_refusals...`, and `sshOwner.productionTarget.terminal.test.ts` plus the updated `sshOwner.productionTarget.leases.test.ts`. A live two-host or concurrent master/PTY run was not performed.
- **Checks.** `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy -p bigbud-ssh-owner --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test -p bigbud-ssh-owner` green with 65 library + 446 binary (7 ignored, including the live provider test) + 8 + 2 integration tests; the focused `production` filter is stable over ten repeated runs. Repeated whole-crate runs exposed pre-existing non-deterministic `opencode_v1::bridge` and `trust_store` tests (WouldBlock/TrustStoreReadOnly) that are unrelated to this slice and were not changed. Linux target `cargo check --all-targets` compiles; two pre-existing Linux-only breaks from the previous slice (stale `picker_master` and an `io::Result` conversion) were fixed. Server: the eight production client/target files pass (30/30, including the new terminal file), `bun run --cwd apps/server typecheck` exits 0, explicit-path `oxfmt`/`oxlint` clean on the three changed TS files, and every changed file is at or below 400 lines (production master/link split into `posix_master/production.rs` and `posix_master/link.rs`; picker production lane split into `posix_picker.production.rs`).
- **Still refused or open.** Agent proxy and OpenCode remain fixture-only and typed refused in production. Windows keeps one pinned target; `BIGBUD_SSH_OWNER_PRODUCTION_ACTIVATION` and the legacy executors are untouched. No live multi-host, Windows or concurrent-owner run; the automation flag and S4 activation are unchanged.

## W3.7 per-target descriptor resolution and owner-side DNS (26 November 2026)

Server descriptor resolution plus an owner-core change. The activation flag, every legacy executor and the production verb allowlist are unchanged, and no commit, tag or release artifact was produced. Host: macOS arm64; **no live DNS-named SSH endpoint was used**, so host-name resolution is covered by unit tests with a scripted resolver plus one system lookup of a reserved `.invalid` name.

- **Wire (semantic widening plus one additive code, protocol stays 1.0).** `TargetDescriptor.host` (field 3, unchanged type) now admits an RFC 1123 DNS name of at most 253 bytes besides an IP literal; the earlier 45-byte IP-only bound is gone. New typed error `ERROR_CODE_TARGET_HOST_UNRESOLVED = 37`. Goldens: `error_target_host_unresolved` added to `v1.golden.frames`, `v1.golden.errors.ts` and the Rust `goldens.rs` table. An older owner refuses a name as `invalid_operands`, which is a safe typed failure. Like the earlier W3.7 additive fields and codes 34–36, this does not bump the handshake version.
- **Owner-side DNS.** [`targets.descriptor.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/targets.descriptor.rs) parses `Host::Ip | Host::Name` (the unspecified address, malformed labels and an all-numeric final label are refused). [`TargetAuthority::bind`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/targets.rs) resolves a name through [`targets.dns.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/targets.dns.rs) (system resolver on a helper thread with a 5 s deadline). A lookup failure or timeout is `target_host_unresolved`, refused before any dial. The address is stored only in the `TransportConfig` owned by the registry slot, so it never outlives the entry. Because `bind` already runs on every resolution for alias re-verification, every resolution performs a new lookup. An alias descriptor's resolved address must still equal the alias policy's `HostName`, which the owner's config resolver still requires to be an IP literal.
- **Server resolver.** [`sshOwnerProductionTargets.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionTargets.ts) maps a runtime `ssh:` id to the descriptor. It reads identity and known-hosts from the user's `~/.ssh/config` through `resolveProfileHostAuth`, which keeps credential paths out of the picker hints. An explicit `keyPath` overrides the configured identity. A missing `UserKnownHostsFile` falls back to the default `~/.ssh/known_hosts` only when that file exists. A DNS name passes through unresolved. Password auth, agent-only auth, a missing identity, user or known-hosts file, a refused config source (`Match`), an unknown alias and an invalid descriptor are typed `SshOwnerTargetRefusal` reasons. An alias descriptor carries the bare alias as its target id, because the owner re-resolves alias policy by that id. `SshOwnerProductionBootLive` now publishes this resolver as `descriptorFor`.
- **Host keys under the name (follow-up, same day).** `TransportConfig.host_identity` carries the DNS name and an explicit descriptor `host_key_alias` (now parsed and owner-validated with the same `safe_token` rule as config `HostKeyAlias`). The transport always dials the resolved address, but known-hosts reads, approvals and the SSH `HostKeyAlias` use: the policy `HostKeyAlias`, else the descriptor alias, else the name's own label (`name`, or `[name]:port` off port 22), else the scanned address. IP-literal targets are unchanged. An address-keyed record never verifies a named target, and a changed key under the name still refuses (`host_key_changed`). Tests: [`host_identity.tests.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/host_identity.tests.rs).
- **Alias `HostName` by DNS (follow-up).** [`config/host_name.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/config/host_name.rs) is the single host rule for both the descriptor and the alias policy. Production admits a DNS `HostName`; fixture routes stay numeric loopback, and a name combined with an explicit `AddressFamily inet`/`inet6` is refused because it cannot be checked before resolution. `bind` compares the policy and descriptor hosts unresolved (name with name, address with address), checks that an explicit descriptor alias equals the policy `HostKeyAlias`, and only then resolves once through the `HostResolver`. Transport revalidation compares the re-read policy host against the bound name or the dialled address the same way. Fixture jump legs remain numeric.
- **Open.** The 5 s lookup thread is abandoned, not cancelled, on timeout. The owner's plain single-record trust store still fails closed if it holds a record for another label. No live run.

Provider slice (W2 T-prov, decision U13). The activation flag and every wire field are unchanged; no commit, tag or release artifact was produced. OpenCode CLI pinned to the v2 client version 2.1.4: `@opencode/cli-linux-arm64@2.1.4` from npm, integrity `sha512-drw0LWMG0niaE94ePXm1h7qjFBZZRcSZD3gact3hx3OYPXesp7xrOt0bn3sicYNUN6fojERrDKR6jYrXmZX+rA==` (matched), executable SHA-256 `7f32303fcd2d41e54c9ea36d4e4d4029b916901f3fbe5ad3fcee8f1b877b8bba`, 236,112,888 bytes, placed only in the scratch directory. Nothing was installed globally.
- **First run failed; root cause fixed.** The owner reported `starting` three times and then `rejected`. The shared executable guard compares the file size with `$limit`, but the v2 launch script never set it, so every real binary failed validation with exit 126 (reproduced directly: unset limit `INVALID`, 512 MiB limit `VALID`). [owner_opencode_v2.rs](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_opencode_v2.rs) now passes its own 512 MiB `LIMIT` as `$7`. The previous v1 ceiling (128 MiB) is below the 2.1.4 binary. Two Linux-only v2 unit tests had never compiled or passed: a `u16` overflow in the port-range assertion and a `--stdio` assertion that ignored the inlined supervisor. Both are corrected.
- **Live evidence (run).** The new [native test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/OpencodeV2/ServerManager.sshOwner.native.test.ts) (fixture: [ServerManager.sshOwner.native.fixture.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/OpencodeV2/ServerManager.sshOwner.native.fixture.ts)) starts the real owner with `--opencode-v2-service` and `--workspace-root`, approves first-use trust, and acquires through `startSelectedV2OwnerProcess`. A direct `/api/info` on the owner-selected local port answered 401 without the credential and 200 with `version: 2.1.4` with it. sshd recorded exactly one session command per acquisition. On `close` and on owner `disconnect` (death listener fired once), `isRunning()` became false, the exact local port refused TCP (`ECONNREFUSED`), and no further command started. The test passed 2/2 in three consecutive runs before the deletion, and 2/2 in three more consecutive runs on the post-deletion tree (owner binary SHA-256 `640f7ea9aa5bf7a233ce0f3347d07657b7572d0004a4e61c0579e48b61aea9af`). The Linux-only `opencode_v2` unit tests pass (10/10).
- **v1 SSH transport deleted (only after the run above).** Removed: the owner's `owner_opencode_v1` recipes (`owner_opencode_v1.rs`, `product.rs`, `supervise.py`, `tests.rs`), the `--opencode-v1-fixture` and fixture `--opencode-service` flags, the production `--opencode-service` flag (now refused as `conflict`), the `opencode_recipe`/`opencode_service` owner state, and the scope-less and v1-scoped `ProofOpencodeOpen` arms. A request without a v2 service scope is now `operation_denied` before any lane or SSH child exists. - **Kept on purpose.** The library module `bigbud_ssh_owner::opencode_v1` still exists. Its `Bridge` is the private-socket bridge that the v2 session runner uses. Its v1 `Startup` gate is now unused by the binary, and its own tests still cover it. Renaming or removing it is a follow-up.
- **Checks.** macOS: `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 65 + 481 (8 ignored) + 8 + 2 passed. Linux guest: strict Clippy `--all-targets` clean (with `-j1`, `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0`, because `rustc` was OOM-killed otherwise); `cargo test -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 65 library passed and 499 binary passed with 2 failures in untouched Git modules (`transport::workspace::git::tests::ordinary_nonzero_exits_without_discovering_parent` sees Git's filesystem-boundary message because the scratch is a tmpfs mount; `git_receive::tests::command::advertises_an_empty_fixture…` exits 126 there). ## W3.7 production OpenCode v2 (26 November 2026)

Production-owner slice following U13. The activation flag and every wire field are unchanged; no commit, tag or release artifact was produced.

- **Starting state.** The production verb allowlist already admitted the OpenCode verbs on Linux, and the per-target lane, generation scope and grant budget came from the earlier W3.7 slices. Three gaps still blocked an activated local build. On macOS the whole OpenCode lane was compiled out (`cfg(target_os = "linux")`), so admission refused it. The production v2 recipe required a single startup `--workspace-root`, which a multi-target owner cannot supply. And the production launch profile in `server.ts` never carried the v2 binding.
- **Owner (Rust).** The OpenCode v2 lane (`owner_opencode`, `owner_opencode_v2`, `owner/opencode.rs`, `transport/opencode.rs`, lane, admission, startup flags) now builds on macOS and Linux initiating hosts. The remote launch guard still requires Linux (`uname -s`, `/proc/self/fd`). [`ProductRecipe`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_opencode_v2.rs) takes `root: Option`. A fixture recipe still pins one root. A production recipe admits each request's own absolute, normalized, control-free cwd (not `/`, not the binary), which is the production PTY rule. The remote guard still requires a trusted owner- or root-owned directory chain. Production `--opencode-v2-service` no longer needs `--workspace-root`. The lease keeps everything it had: the per-target `Slot` lane, exact target plus generation scope, the per-target grant budget, the target's own trust and identity policy through `pinned_opencode_ssh`, bounded readiness and close deadlines, an owner-chosen remote and local port, a per-lease password, and confirmed teardown.
- **Server (TS).** [`readProductionOpencodeV2Service`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerDevRuntime.providerServices.ts) reads `providers.opencodeV2.binaryPath` from `config.settingsPath` with the same parser as dev. `server.ts` passes it into the production `ownerProfile`. That is a one-line composition change in a file other work also touches. Launch facts and client startup accept a v2 binding without a workspace root in production. The selected v2 composition now also admits `darwin`.
- **Bugs found by the live run, fixed.** (1) `makeHandle` read `owner.targetGeneration` eagerly. That getter throws for a target-free owner, so `startOrAttachSshOwnerProduction` failed against every real production owner. The fake owners always had a target. It is now lazy ([sshOwnerProductionClient.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionClient.ts)). That failure also left the spawned owner running, because no handle existed to reap it. The leaked process was killed by hand, and the leak path is a follow-up. (2) The production session is single-flight and refused any concurrent request. Two leases polling status on one owner raced and failed with `operationDenied`. OpenCode verbs now wait for the in-flight exchange, which is bounded by its own deadline, and then dispatch in one synchronous step ([sshOwnerPickerProof.client.session.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.client.session.ts)). (3) A v2 startup unit test failed on macOS with `WouldBlock`: it called `accept` on the non-blocking reserved listener. It now waits with a bounded loop.
- **Live evidence: production owner, two targets (run).** The new [production native test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/OpencodeV2/ServerManager.sshOwner.production.native.test.ts) ([fixture](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/OpencodeV2/ServerManager.sshOwner.production.native.fixture.ts)) ran on the Debian 13 arm64 guest as `bbdev`, in a fresh network and mount namespace (`unshare -nm` as root, then `runuser`). Plain `bwrap` maps root-owned ancestors to the overflow UID, which the attach-socket check correctly refuses. Remote `~/.local` was bind-mounted to tmpfs because the guest root filesystem is out of space. The test starts the real `bigbud-ssh-owner --production` with no target, two generated-key loopback sshd targets and the pinned 2.1.4 CLI. It approves trust per target and goes through `makeProductionV2OwnerSource` and `startSelectedV2OwnerProcess`. Each target reached `ready` with a 64-hex password. `/api/info` answered 401 without it and 200 (`version 2.1.4`) with it. Ports and passwords differed per target, and each sshd recorded exactly one session. Closing target 1 refused its port while target 2 still answered 200; closing target 2 then refused its port. The final tree passed five consecutive runs together with the fixture-mode native test (3 passed, 1 skipped per run). No `serve --stdio`, owner or sshd process was left behind.
- **Live evidence: macOS initiator (run).** A macOS 27.2.1 arm64 production owner, using system OpenSSH 10.5p1 and its debug build, reached a disposable sshd in the Debian guest (`192.168.139.96:42922`, generated host and client keys, explicit known-hosts pin). The external-target case of the same test passed three times on the final tree after three earlier passes: `ready`, then 401/200 at version 2.1.4, then close and `ECONNREFUSED`. The guest sshd log shows one session per run (9 total), and no OpenCode process remained. The guest sshd and the macOS key copy were removed afterwards.
- **Linux Git test failures (U13 report), resolved.** Both failures reproduce on a clean `git archive HEAD` export (`a2c44ae28a`) in `/tmp/oc2-head`. That export needed the one-line `u16` test fix to compile its Linux tests at all. So both predate U13. (a) `ordinary_nonzero_exits_without_discovering_parent` was environment-only: the plain-Git baseline walked up to the tmpfs `/tmp` mount and printed Git's filesystem-boundary message, while the recipe stops at its ceiling. The baseline now uses the same `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` bound. (b) `advertises_an_empty_fixture…` was a real product bug. Debian's Git 2.47 writes `[extensions]` after `[core]` in a SHA-256 bare repository, but Git 2.52 (macOS) writes it before. The receive-advertisement guard accepted only the newer order, so every SHA-256 fixture on Debian failed with exit 126. The guard now accepts both exact byte layouts.
- **Checks.** macOS: `cargo fmt --check` clean; strict Clippy `--all-targets` clean; `cargo test -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 65 + 495 (8 ignored) + 8 + 2 passed. Linux guest (`-j1`, `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0`): fmt clean, strict Clippy clean, 65 + 502 (5 ignored) + 8 + 2 passed, with no failures. Server: 16 focused files passed on macOS (83 tests); explicit-path `oxfmt`/`oxlint` clean; `bun run --cwd apps/server typecheck` result is in the report; every changed file is at most 400 lines.
- **Open.** The remote executable comes from `providers.opencodeV2.binaryPath`, the same field the local v2 service uses (the dev rule). A host whose local and remote paths differ needs a separate remote setting, which is a product decision. The owner can leak when production startup fails after spawn. Alias targets whose transport carries a policy are refused by `pinned_opencode_ssh` (`config_unsupported`), so only structured descriptors were exercised. Windows has no lane.

## W3.7 OpenCode v2 remote discovery, alias targets and startup reap (26 November 2026)

This follows the production OpenCode v2 slice. The activation flag is unchanged. One additive wire code was added, so the protocol stays 1.0. No commit, tag or release artifact was produced.

- **Remote executable: discovery by default, explicit override.** `providers.opencodeV2.binaryPath` is now purely local. The new optional setting `providers.opencodeV2.remoteBinaryPath` (contracts schema and patch) is a global remote override. When it is unset, the owner starts with `--opencode-v2-discover` (production and fixture). [discover.sh](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_opencode_v2/discover.sh) is owner-shipped. On the target it tries, in order: `command -v opencode` in the account's login shell (`$SHELL -lc`, killed after 5 s, output capped at 4 KiB), then `~/.opencode/bin`, `~/.local/bin`, `~/bin`, `/usr/local/bin` and `/usr/bin`. Each candidate is resolved with `readlink -e` and must pass the unchanged guard: Linux `uname`, trusted owner- or root-owned directory chain, regular file, one link, owner-only writable, at most 512 MiB, ELF magic, opened-descriptor identity. The server still requires `/api/info` to report exactly 2.1.4. A per-target override was not added, because a request must never name a program. If no candidate passes, the remote side writes a nonce-framed `missing` receipt. The owner then answers the new typed `ERROR_CODE_PROVIDER_EXECUTABLE_UNAVAILABLE = 38` (`providerExecutableUnavailable`), whose goldens were added in TS and Rust. The server reports that reason rather than a cleanup `AggregateError`, because the owner already settled the lease.
- **Lane reuse bug, fixed.** A closed or otherwise settled OpenCode lease never left its target lane, so a second start on the same target was `operation_denied` until disconnect. A confirmed-settled lease now frees the lane. An unconfirmed lease still holds it.
- **Alias targets.** `pinned_opencode_ssh` no longer refuses a transport that carries an alias policy. It applies the folder-list rule: re-read and compare the alias policy, use the policy's host CA for a certificate host, and let the lease's own keepalive options stand instead of the policy timers. Agent-backed identities and connector routes stay typed refused. For production, `server.ts` now also passes `~/.ssh/config` (and `/etc/ssh/ssh_config` when the user config exists) as `--alias-config`/`--system-config`, through [sshOwnerProductionProfileFacts.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionProfileFacts.ts). The owner parses those files itself and refuses an unsupported alias per target, so a config the owner cannot admit never blocks startup. A fixture finding: the owner requires an alias's `IdentityFile` to lie in the config file's own scope, as with `~/.ssh/config` and `~/.ssh/id_*`.
- **Startup reap.** In [sshOwnerProductionClient.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionClient.ts), every failure after the spawn now reaps the exact child before the typed error surfaces. That covers negotiation, multi-target, descriptor write and handle creation. The reap is SIGKILL plus close, bounded by `SSH_OWNER_STARTUP_REAP_MS = 3000`. The earlier leak happened because handle creation sat outside the reap path. [sshOwner.productionClient.reap.test.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwner.productionClient.reap.test.ts) injects a post-spawn failure (an owner that pinned one target) and asserts the pid is already dead when startup returns.
- **Live evidence (run, Debian 13 arm64 guest, production owner).** [ServerManager.sshOwner.production.discovery.native.test.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/OpencodeV2/ServerManager.sshOwner.production.discovery.native.test.ts) ran with no configured remote path and the CLI reachable only through a login-PATH directory that is not a known install location (`~/.profile` bind-mounted in a private mount namespace). The owner discovered it for an `~/.ssh/config`-style alias target (`bb-oc-alias`, kind `alias`, runtime `ssh:bb-oc-alias`): `ready`, then 401/200 at 2.1.4, then close and `ECONNREFUSED`. A second start on the same target then succeeded the same way, with one session per round. A configured nonexistent override produced the typed `providerExecutableUnavailable` and no ready state. Control run: the same discovery test without the PATH entry failed with `providerExecutableUnavailable`, so `command -v` was the source. Three consecutive runs passed with the two-target production test and the fixture-mode test (5 passed, 1 skipped per run), and no process was left behind. The guest runs needed `-j1` and a cleared target directory, because the guest container is capped at 4 GiB including the tmpfs scratch.
- **Checks.** macOS: `cargo fmt --check` clean; strict Clippy `--all-targets` clean; `cargo test -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 65 + 497 (8 ignored) + 8 + 2. Linux guest: fmt clean, strict Clippy clean, 65 + 504 (5 ignored) + 8 + 2. Protocol goldens: `protocol/ssh-owner/verify-rust` 3 + 9 passed; the TS goldens (`vitest --config protocol/ssh-owner/vitest.config.ts`) passed 10. Server: 22 focused files passed on macOS (99 tests); contracts settings tests passed 4; explicit-path `oxfmt`/`oxlint` clean on 27 files; `bun run --cwd apps/server typecheck` exit 0; `bun run --cwd packages/contracts typecheck` exit 0. Every changed file is at most 400 lines.
- **Open.** There is no settings UI for `remoteBinaryPath`, which is editable in the settings file only. A refused override is typed only in dev; in production it reads as "no binding". The macOS-initiator live run used an explicit override, not discovery. Discovery from a macOS owner uses the same remote script, but that combination was not run live.

## W3.7 OpenCode v2 discovery from a macOS production owner (26 November 2026)

Live run of `--opencode-v2-discover` from a macOS initiator. The activation flag and every wire field are unchanged; no commit, tag or release artifact was produced.

- **Host and inputs.** Initiator: macOS 27.2 arm64, system OpenSSH 10.5p1 (LibreSSL 3.3.6), debug `bigbud-ssh-owner --production` built on this tree. Target: the Debian 13 arm64 OrbStack guest `bb-ssh-owner-linux-dev` (`192.168.139.96:42923`), a disposable non-root `bbdev` sshd in a private mount namespace (scratch in tmpfs `/tmp/oc2-disc`). Remote `~/.local` and `~/.profile` were bind-mounted per case, so the real home was not modified. Generated client key on the Mac, generated host key in the guest, and an explicit known-hosts pin. CLI: the pinned `@opencode/cli-linux-arm64@2.1.4` executable (SHA-256 `7f32303f…8bba` re-verified). No configured remote path (binding `{}`). The target was an `~/.ssh/config`-style alias (`bb-oc-alias`).
- **First run failed; root cause fixed (server).** Every alias acquisition from macOS failed at `inspectTrust` with `configUnsupported`. The owner opens the alias config's directory by its canonical path (`Scope::open` canonicalizes the ancestors of `~/.ssh`) and rebases every in-scope `IdentityFile` and `UserKnownHostsFile` onto it. `TargetAuthority::bind` then requires the descriptor's paths to match exactly. The server's [resolveProductionTargetDescriptor](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionTargets.ts) sent the literal `~/.ssh/...` paths. On macOS `/tmp` and `/var` are symlinks, so `/tmp/…/identity` became `/private/tmp/…/identity` on the owner side and never matched. The same mismatch hits any user whose home has a symlinked ancestor. It never showed on Linux, where these paths are not links. A/B: an identical run with the `/private/tmp` key path passed. Fix: for alias descriptors only, paths under the literal or canonical `~/.ssh` are rebased onto `realpath(~/.ssh)`. Out-of-scope paths and structured targets are unchanged. A symlinked `~/.ssh` itself stays refused by the owner by design (`Scope::open` requires a real directory). Regression unit test: [sshOwnerProductionTargets.test.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionTargets.test.ts) (symlinked home). It fails without the fix.
- **Harness.** [The production native fixture](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/OpencodeV2/ServerManager.sshOwner.production.native.fixture.ts) now builds the alias descriptor through `resolveProductionTargetDescriptor`. It uses a fake home reached through a symlink, holding a real `~/.ssh` with copies of the key and pin. It also supports the alias for external targets. [The discovery test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/OpencodeV2/ServerManager.sshOwner.production.discovery.native.test.ts) gains external cases selected by `BIGBUD_TEST_OPENCODE_V2_EXTERNAL_TARGET` and `BIGBUD_TEST_OPENCODE_V2_DISCOVERY_EXPECT=ready|unavailable`. An external target reports no sshd command count, so that assertion is skipped there.
- **Live evidence (run, final tree, key in macOS `/tmp`).** (1) CLI only on the remote login PATH (`~/.profile` adds a non-standard directory; `~/.local/bin` empty): `ready`, then 401 without and 200 with the credential at `version 2.1.4`, then close and `ECONNREFUSED`, twice per run on the same target. 3/3 runs, 6 sshd sessions. (2) CLI only in `~/.local/bin` (empty profile; remote `$SHELL -lc 'command -v opencode'` printed nothing): same result, 3/3, 6 sessions. (3) CLI absent everywhere: typed `providerExecutableUnavailable` and no `ready` state, 3/3, 3 sessions. Before the fix, the `/tmp` path failed every time. The `/private/tmp` workaround then passed 4 + 3 + 3 runs (cases 1–3). An intermediate fixture that made `~/.ssh` itself a symlink failed 9/9, which is correct: the owner refuses that layout by design. Live control: with the fix reverted, case 3 failed again with `configUnsupported`. No login-shell quoting or timeout issue appeared through macOS OpenSSH 10.5. Each acquisition finished in about 1–2 s. No `opencode` or owner process was left. The guest sshd and scratch, and the Mac key directory, were removed.
- **Checks.** macOS: `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 65 + 497 (8 ignored) + 8 + 2 passed (no Rust change). Server: focused Vitest on the three touched test files passed (10 passed, 6 skipped live cases); explicit-path `oxfmt --check`/`oxlint` clean on 4 files; `tsc --noEmit` for `apps/server` with an 8 GiB heap reported no errors (exit 0). Every changed file is at most 400 lines.
- **Open.** The reworked alias fixture was not rerun on the Linux loopback (`aliasFirstTarget`) path in the guest. Real user `~/.ssh/config` layouts beyond a symlinked home were not run live.

## U14 third-party Git hosts and U15 design entry (26 November 2026)

User decisions U14 and U15 refine U7 for initiating Git. The activation flag and the wire protocol are unchanged; no commit was produced.

- **Bug fixed first.** Under `BIGBUD_SSH_OWNER_ACTIVATION=1`, background upstream refresh of a local repo with an HTTPS remote failed every ~15 s. `makeSelectedDevGitLayer` always provides `InitiatingFetchOwner`, even without an owner session, and every selected-mode fetch was sent to it.
- **Routing (U7/U14).** [GitCore.fetchRoute.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitCore.fetchRoute.ts) routes every selected-mode fetch (explicit, background, pull, PR) by the `insteadOf`-expanded remote URL. HTTP(S)/file/local path: ordinary Git with `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=http:https:file`. SSH to a non-target host: fenced local Git (sanitized env, `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=ssh`, `BatchMode=yes` unless the user's Git config names a command, 30 s cap, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` passed for the user's agent). SSH to a bigbud target, or an unreadable target set: owner binding, else typed refusal. `git://`, `ext::` and helpers: refused. Target hosts come from the project projection, with alias HostNames ([GitCore.fetchRoute.hosts.ts](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitCore.fetchRoute.hosts.ts)).
- **Quiet refusal.** Background refresh caches an owner refusal for 10 minutes and logs it once at info level.
- **Structural checker.** One reviewed allowlist entry: `GitCore.fetchRoute.ts`, rule 1, symbol `ssh` (U14).
- **Evidence.** Loopback `sshd` + bare repo not registered as a target: fetch advances; the same host registered as a target is refused with no dial. Local `file://` remote fetch advances with `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` set in the server environment.
- **U15.** Design entry in the finish-line plan §4. Not built: the existing `ProofGitUploadOpen` is bound to startup-transferred descriptors and a fixture recipe, so production needs a per-lease channel, a workspace-root authority on the owner and new protocol messages.

## U14 follow-up: background target set, BatchMode and user/port matching (26 November 2026)

- **Background refresh target set.** The shared status cache runs its lookup in the Git layer's construction context, which need not carry the project projection. `SshTargetIdSourceLive` (server composition, provided with `ProjectionPersistenceLayerLive`) now registers a process-wide target id source; `readSshTargets` uses the projection service when present and the registered source otherwise. A github.com remote is therefore fetched through the fenced path in background refresh as well.
- **No prompts with a user `core.sshCommand`.** `nonInteractiveSshCommand` appends `-o BatchMode=yes` when the user's command is plainly `ssh` (or a path ending in `/ssh`) with options only: no shell syntax, no `--`. A wrapper or any shell syntax is left unchanged (documented residual: stdin is a pipe and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`).
- **Target matching.** Remotes carry host, user and port; targets carry every host spelling plus the explicit or descriptor-resolved user and port. A match needs the same host, and the same user and port wherever both sides state them; an unstated side cannot disprove the match. A second reviewed allowlist entry covers `GitCore.fetchRoute.match.ts` (rule 1, `ssh`).
- **Evidence.** A live loopback sshd test drives `statusDetails → refreshStatusUpstreamIfStale` with no projection service in context and a registered source only: the non-target SSH remote's tracking ref advances, and the owner binding is never called.

## U15 production owner Git upload (26 November 2026)

Built in the stage order from the plan's U15 design entry. The activation flag is unchanged. No commit was produced.

1. **Per-lease byte channel (Rust).** `owner_git_upload.rs`: one `mkdtemp` 0700 directory and 0600 Unix socket per lease, one accepted connection within 15 s, then the socket is removed and the connection becomes the pinned child's stdin/stdout. 120 s cap, process-group kill on close or deadline.
2. **Workspace-root authority.** Owner-side exact-path containment (`admits`) before any process, plus the target guard (`owner_git_upload/guard.sh`) that resolves symlinks and refuses an escape (exit 126 → `operation_denied`). The server chooses the root from the project projection for that exact target.
3. **Protocol.** `GitUploadOpen`/`GitUploadLease`/`GitUploadState`/`GitUploadPhase` in `v1.workspace_git.proto`; Request 77–79 and Reply 50 in `v1.proto`. Frame and grant goldens added and checked by both verifiers (`v1.golden.gitUpload.ts`, `verify-rust/tests/support/git_upload_vectors.rs`); the frozen product-verb list now names the three verbs. No receive message.
4. **Rust admission.** `production_verb` admits the three verbs on macOS and Linux; the lease lives in the target's `Lanes` (one per target, reaped on revoke); status and close are lease-local control; `pinned_git_upload_ssh` uses the target's own key or policy-CA verification, no forwarding, and refuses agent identities and connector routes.
5. **TypeScript.** Codec `sshOwnerPickerProof.codec.gitUpload.ts` (checked against the golden frames), bound-target client methods, and `makeProductionInitiatingFetchBinding` (`GitCore.selectedStream.production.ts`), wired into `makeSelectedDevGitLayer` when no dev fixture session exists and production activation is on. Only explicit `ssh://user@host[:port]/path` remotes whose host, user and port equal the owner descriptor are served.
6. **Live run (run).** Initiator: this macOS host, debug `bigbud-ssh-owner --production` built on this tree. Target: OrbStack Debian 13 arm64 guest `bb-ssh-owner-linux-dev`, a disposable non-root `bbdev` sshd on port 42931 with a generated host key and a Mac-generated client key, workspace root `/tmp/u15/ws` (tmpfs) holding `repo.git` (main `b1873f34…`), plus `/tmp/u15/outside.git` and the escaping symlink `ws/link.git`. [The native test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitCore.selectedStream.production.native.test.ts) passed four consecutive runs: the fetch advanced `origin/main` to `b1873f34…`; an outside remote was refused before the owner was asked; the owner refused an outside path with `invalid_operands`; the symlink escape settled `operation_denied`. The guest sshd logged exactly two command sessions per run (fetch and symlink case), and no owner process, socket directory or `git-upload-pack` remained afterwards.

- **Checks.** macOS: `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner -- --check` clean; strict Clippy `--all-targets` clean; `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 65 + 499 (8 ignored) + 8 + 2. Linux guest (Rust 1.95.0, `-j1`, `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0`, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`): fmt clean; strict Clippy `--all-targets` clean; `cargo test -p bigbud-ssh-owner --lib --bins --tests` 65 + 506 (5 ignored) + 8 + 2 (the `picker_proof` example binary was OOM-killed at link in the guest and was excluded). Protocol: `verify-rust` 3 + 9; TS goldens 10; structural checker 25. Server: `tsc` clean; focused Vitest `src/git` plus 29 `src/ssh` codec/client/production files, 121 files passed (2 skipped); explicit-path oxfmt/oxlint clean.
- **Open.** `GitReceiveOpen` (push) is not built; it needs the trusted old-OID approval design. scp-style remotes to a bigbud target stay refused. A Linux-initiator live run (owner on Linux) was not done; only Linux build/Clippy/tests ran in the guest.

## W2 T-files, T-alias and T-term production slice (26 November 2026)

Production-owner slice. The activation flag and every wire field are unchanged (no Request/Reply/ErrorCode number was used from the 120–129 / 71–75 / 55–58 reservation); no commit, tag or release artifact was produced. Initiator: macOS 27.2.1 arm64, system OpenSSH 10.5p1, debug `bigbud-ssh-owner --production` built on this tree. Target: OrbStack Debian 13 arm64 guest `bb-ssh-owner-linux-dev`, a disposable non-root `bbdev` sshd (OpenSSH 10.0p2 Debian-7+deb13u4) on `192.168.139.96:42971` and `[fd07:b51a:cc66:0:3857:5fff:fed6:7a66]:42972`, generated host key, Mac-generated client key, explicit known-hosts pin, workspace `/tmp/bb-tfiles/ws` (tmpfs) with a symlink `escape` to a sibling directory. Both sshd processes, the guest scratch and the Mac key directory were removed afterwards.

- **Owner (Rust), T-files.** Production admits the workspace file verbs (read, list, snapshot, index, content search, single-frame write, staged write begin/chunk/commit/abort, exclusive create, create directory, delete entry) through the per-domain [`admission.workspace.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/admission.workspace.rs). [`root_authority`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/workspace.rs) keeps a fixture's exact startup root; a production target (no startup root) admits the request's own root, which `validate_path` still requires to be absolute and normalized (never `/`), and the remote `PATH_WALK` still refuses a symlink anywhere on it. Guarded writes, workspace Git, readiness and workspace-mode publication stay fixture-only. The terminal cwd probe uses the same rule, so a production PTY cwd preflight no longer always refuses.
- **Server, T-files.** Bound production targets now carry the workspace client (alias-aware: `ssh:<alias>` serves a bare-alias descriptor) and queue behind the in-flight exchange like leases. `withWsSshOwnerWorkspace` / `withWsSshOwnerEntryActions` take a per-request binding resolver; the dev fixture is one resolver and [`wsSshOwnerProduction.workspace.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerProduction.workspace.ts) is the production one (live registry → `descriptorFor` → `handle.target`), composed in `ws.ts` only when activation is on and no dev owner is selected. No legacy fallback: no live owner or a resolver refusal is `not-dispatched`. Production advertises folder create and delete but not New File, and refuses guarded and create-only saves (`not-dispatched`) instead of downgrading them.
- **Bug fixed (found by the first live run of the dev cases).** An unguarded write one byte over 512 KiB was reported `writeDisposition: unknown` although the client refused it before sending. `WorkspaceMutationWatchOwnerShape.maxWriteBytes` now lets `WorkspaceFileSystem.write.ts` refuse it as `not-dispatched`. The folder-op WebSocket test sent a NUL path that the contract schema refuses at the client encoder; it now asserts that refusal separately.
- **Live evidence (run).** (1) Dev selected domain on macOS with a loopback sshd: `sshOwnerPickerProof.workspaceFolderOps.websocket.test.ts` and the 512 KiB `workspaceLifecycle` case passed (were written-not-run). (2) Production: [`wsSshOwnerProduction.workspace.native.test.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerProduction.workspace.native.test.ts) — small write, staged 512 KiB write (SHA-256 `02a1ff97…aab1` matched on the guest), +1 byte refused `not-dispatched`, preview, create folder, duplicate create terminal, list, delete folder and file, second delete terminal, symlink-escape writes refused (sibling directory stayed empty), root `/` refused, guarded save refused before dispatch, pre-image intact. IPv4 structured target 3/3, IPv4 real-profile alias 3/3, IPv6 structured 1/1, IPv6 real-profile alias 2/2; 13 sshd command sessions per run; no owner process left.
- **T-alias.** The real-profile alias layout (ordered `Include config.d/*.conf`, two names on one `Host` line, `Host *` defaults after it, `IdentityFile ~/.ssh/keys/work_ed25519`, `UserKnownHostsFile` from `Host *`, home reached through macOS `/tmp`) was admitted live by the production owner over IPv4 and IPv6 as above. Unit: [`sshOwnerProductionTargets.userProfile.test.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionTargets.userProfile.test.ts) (4) and Rust [`config/user_profile_tests.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/config/user_profile_tests.rs) (3: implicit port 22 on a real destination, explicit port 22 with an IPv6 `HostName`, `Match … exec` in an included file). Finding: the server resolver refuses any non-`all` `Match`, while the owner refuses only an applicable `exec`; the server is the stricter gate, so this fails closed.
- **Checks.** `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner -- --check`: clean for this slice's files (other lanes' files show diffs). Strict Clippy (macOS and `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`): no finding in this slice's files; the run fails on `owner/provider_lease.rs` (indexing) and `duplicate_mod` for `production_terminal_tests.support.rs`, which `provider_lease.tests.rs` also loads. That duplicate module gives two `NEXT` counters, so `production_folder_listing_uses_the_target_master_lane` collides on its temp directory under the parallel whole-crate run (passes alone, 3/3). `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner`: 71 lib, 544 passed + that 1 failure (8 ignored) in the binary. Server: 14 focused files (64 passed, 2 live skips; one OpenCode source test failed only in the batch and passed 5/5 alone), live dev 2/2, web U6 copy test 5/5; `tsc --noEmit` for `apps/server` (8 GiB heap) reported 0 errors; explicit-path `oxfmt --check`/`oxlint` clean on 18 files; every changed file is at most 400 lines.

## W2 T-shell production exec leases (26 November 2026)

Production-owner slice for G2 `runThreadShell` / `runProviderWorkspaceShell`, `runAcpCommand` and ACP writes. The activation flag is unchanged; no commit, tag or release artifact was produced. Protocol numbers come from the T-shell reservation only: Request 90–94, Reply 56, no new ErrorCode (the existing `SHELL_TIMED_OUT` 32, `SHELL_CANCELLED` 33, `SESSION_QUOTA` 28, `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` 14 and `WORKSPACE_PATH_DENIED` 18 cover every outcome).

- **Wire (additive, [v1.shell.proto](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/v1.shell.proto)).** The frozen names are promoted as `run_thread_shell = 90`, `run_provider_workspace_shell = 91` and `run_acp_command = 92`, plus lease-local `shell_status = 93` (lease, `ShellRole`, output `offset`) and `shell_close = 94`, and Reply `shell = 56` (`ShellState`: lease, role, `RUNNING`/`EXITED`/`CLOSED`, one output chunk of at most 1 MiB, `output_size`, optional `exit_code`, `truncated`). Every open carries a 32-byte lease and the request's own `workspace_root`/`cwd`; there is no stdin, PTY, environment, argv or SSH option field.
- **Why leases, not the buffered proof verb.** A 120 s command on the single owner request loop would block every target's terminal, list and lease traffic, and an 8 MiB reply does not fit the 8 MiB owner frame with its envelope. Opening returns at once; the SSH child runs on a worker thread; status polls are lease-local control (no storage hydration) and queue behind the in-flight exchange like OpenCode and Git upload.
- **Owner (Rust).** [`owner/shell_lease.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/shell_lease.rs) keeps the leases in the target's `Lanes` (`Lanes::shell`), scoped to the exact target and generation (`LeaseScope`); a rotated generation retires older leases, a lease never answers a status/close naming another role, at most 4 leases per target (`SESSION_QUOTA` beyond), one running command per thread id, duplicate lease tokens refused, settled-but-unread leases dropped after 60 s, typed failures reported once and released, and an unconfirmed teardown retained (`OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`, never replayed). Close kills the local process group and confirms within 1.75 s. `Lanes::revoke` and the per-target Disconnect (`revoke_network_leases`) reap shell leases. [`transport/shell.lease.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/shell.lease.rs) validates the root (absolute, normalized, control-free, ≤ 4096 B, not `/`) and contained cwd; a fixture owner admits only its startup root. It pins the target's own trust (key, or the alias policy's host CA), refuses agent-backed identities and connector routes, and keeps the 30 s default / 120 s ceiling / 8 MiB combined-output bounds and the ACP `pwd` recipe (10 s, 8 KiB). Admission: production admits the five verbs on macOS/Linux; the ACP open is interactive-class, the shells and lease control background-class in the per-target grant budget. `process::run_with_combined_output_limit_cancellable` is now built on Linux as well.
- **Bug found by the live run, fixed: remote commands outlived cancellation and timeouts.** Killing the local `ssh -T` client left the remote command running (no PTY, so no SIGHUP): 2 survivors after abort. dash also disables `set -m` without a TTY, so the first fix created no process group (3 survivors). The production lease recipe now starts the command in its own process group (`setsid`, `perl setpgrp` fallback, exit 126 if neither exists). A remote watchdog kills the group (TERM, then KILL) when the SSH session process (`$PPID`) is gone or the remote deadline (owner deadline + 2 s) passes. dash's `kill` needs `--` before a negative process-group id. Probe on Debian dash: exit passthrough (5), remote deadline kill at about 4 s (143), 0 survivors after a client `kill -9`. Local unit tests cover cwd/exit/no added latency and the deadline kill of a backgrounded descendant.
