## W2 T-backend production deployment and relay leases (26 November 2026)

Production-owner slice for C12/C13. The activation flag is unchanged; no commit was produced. Host: macOS 27.2 arm64 initiator with stock `/usr/bin/ssh`; target: the OrbStack Debian 13 arm64 guest `bb-ssh-owner-linux-dev`, a disposable non-root `bbdev` sshd on port 42951 with a generated host key and a Mac-generated client key, pinned in an explicit known-hosts file.

- **Protocol.** New `v1.remote_backend.proto`: Request `deployment_step_open` 100, `deployment_step_status` 101, `deployment_step_cancel` 102, `client_relay_lease_open` 103, `client_relay_lease_status` 104, `client_relay_lease_close` 105; Reply `deployment_step` 61, `client_relay_lease` 62. No new error code. Frame and grant goldens on both sides (`v1.golden.remoteBackend.ts`, `verify-rust/tests/support/remote_backend_vectors.rs`); the frozen product-verb list names the six verbs.
- **Deployment steps (Rust).** `owner_backend/deployment_recipe.rs` validates one kind (probe, stage begin, manifest put, setup-input put, HTTPS fetch, laptop upload, publish, setup, start/enable, completion read, reset) and refuses any operand outside it. `deployment_step.sh` keeps the legacy remote layout (`~/.bigbud/backend-graphs`, `.staging-<request>`, `backend-setup/<request>`), exit codes (64/65/66/70/73/75) and deadlines (30 s, 60 s, 30 min, 10 min, 20 min, 5 min); setup and start must name the home their absolute paths came from. Output caps 256 KiB stdout / 16 KiB stderr. Streamed input (manifest, setup inputs, archive up to 2 GiB, exact byte count) arrives on one owner-made 0700/0600 socket accepted within 15 s; nothing rides in a frame. `owner/backend.rs` keeps one step lease per target in its `Lanes` (exact target + generation, revoked with the target), retains the settled result for reattach by the same token (a re-open with that token never runs again), cancels only that lease's process group, and settles timeout, truncation, `TransportLost` 255, signal or an unconfirmed reap as `outcome_unknown`. OpenSSH refusals before the remote ran (auth, host key, quota) are typed errors. The pinned child is the U15 constructor (`pinned_backend_ssh`): the target's own key or policy-CA trust, `-T`, no forwarding, no agent identity or connector.
- **Relay channels (Rust).** One owner relay lease per channel, at most 8 per target (`SessionQuota` beyond), running the existing installed relay recipe (node and helper hashes re-verified on the target; exit 126 → `operation_denied`) with the accepted socket as stdin/stdout, so backpressure and half-close are the socket's own. 24 h channel cap; close kills the process group and confirms the reap; an unconfirmed reap keeps the slot.
- **Server (TS).** `deployment.ssh.owner.production{,.match}.ts`: each exact legacy command maps to one typed step (anything else is refused before dispatch), the lease is driven to a settled state with abort → cancel, and an unconfirmed step is `unavailable` "not replayed". `RemoteBackendDeploymentLive` uses it and owner trust (`inspectTrust` must be `verified`) under `SSH_OWNER_PRODUCTION_ACTIVATION`. `clientRelay.ssh.owner.production.ts` is the production relay owner; `clientRelay.route.ts` selects it under activation (the drain stop hook is unchanged). Codec `sshOwnerPickerProof.codec.backend.ts`, bound-target client `sshOwnerPickerProof.client.productionBackend.ts` (queued behind the in-flight exchange, like OpenCode and Git upload).
- **D5.** `localSupervisorInstaller.ts` declares its own command envelope and imports no legacy SSH type; the structural checker reports 0 findings on it and on all 20 changed server files.
- **Bug found by the live run, fixed.** A non-streamed step reported `listening` without a socket path when the reply raced the worker start, which the client correctly rejected as a protocol error. Such a step now reports `running` from open.
- **Live evidence (run).** `deployment.ssh.owner.production.native.test.ts`, three consecutive passes against the debug production owner: probe exited 0 with `home=/home/bbdev`, `arch=aarch64` and the host-key identity; reset exited 0 (`reset`); a missing completion record exited 1 (ordinary exit, not unknown); an upload lease whose socket was never dialled cancelled, and re-opening its token returned `cancelled`; an out-of-kind operand was `invalidOperands`; four parallel relay channels each echoed 512 KiB after the caller's half-close plus the authority suffix (275–351 ms for the four), then disposal confirmed every reap; a tampered helper pin was refused remotely and disposal reported the lost confirmation. The guest sshd logged 42 command sessions across all runs (including two failed runs before the fix). No relay, step or `bigbud-oc1-*` socket remained on either side.
- **Checks.** Owner crate: `cargo test -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 71 + 545 (8 ignored) + 8 + 2 passed; T-backend unit tests 17; `rustfmt` clean on this slice's files; strict Clippy on macOS and `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` reports nothing in this slice's files (remaining findings were other lanes' in-flight terminal files). Protocol: `verify-rust` 3 + 10, TS goldens 11. Server: `deployment.test.ts` 13 (5 new production integration tests running the owner's own step script on the fixture account), `clientRelay.test.ts` 10 (3 new production relay tests: 4 parallel 512 KiB channels beside a paused reader, a 256 KiB half-close, no-owner and unconfirmed-close refusals), codec 3; explicit-path `oxfmt`/`oxlint` clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Not run / open.** The full live deployment (`BIGBUD_TEST_TBACKEND_DEPLOY=1`, written) could not run: every write under the guest account's home fails with `No space left on device`, and a tmpfs bind mount needs root. The Linux guest `cargo test` was not run for the same reason (cross-target Clippy only). Two leftover local owners first attributed to this slice's failed runs were not from it (they carried `--opencode-v2-discover`; this test passes an explicit `--opencode-v2-service`); see the follow-up below. Deployment approval evidence (`readRemoteAgentApprovalEvidence`, D4) still uses the T-agent path. `clientRelay.credentials.test.ts` fails 3 cases on macOS by design (Linux-only authority), unchanged by this slice.

## W2 T-prov production Pi and Codex provider leases (26 November 2026)

Production-owner slice for C10 (Pi composition and the Codex typed probe). The activation flag is unchanged; no commit, tag or release artifact was produced. Protocol numbers come from the T-prov reservation only: Request 80–83, Reply 51–52, no new ErrorCode (the existing `providerExecutableUnavailable` = 38 is reused).

- **Wire (additive, [v1.provider_lease.proto](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/v1.provider_lease.proto)).** `probe_provider = 80` (the reserved X-WIRE name, now allocated and removed from the reserved list), `provider_lease_open = 81`, `provider_lease_status = 82`, `provider_lease_close = 83`; replies `provider_version = 51` and `provider_lease = 52`. A `ProviderLeaseScope` names only the kind (Pi or Codex), thread, epoch, an exact remote cwd and, for a Codex start only, the typed `default_mode_user_input` flag. No program, argument, environment, socket path or session file is ever on the wire.
- **Owner (Rust).** [`owner_provider_lease.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_provider_lease.rs) compiles the whole remote command: an owner-shipped [launch script](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_provider_lease/launch.sh) discovers `pi`/`codex` (the account's login `command -v`, bounded at 5 s and 4 KiB, then `~/.local/bin`, `~/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, `/usr/bin`), resolves it with `readlink -e`, and runs the shared provider executable guard (Linux `uname`, trusted directory chain, regular single-link owner-only-writable file up to 512 MiB, ELF magic, opened-descriptor identity) before executing the opened descriptor with `--version`, `--mode rpc` or `app-server` (plus the owner-compiled `-c features.default_mode_request_user_input=true`). The environment is fixed (`env -i LC_ALL=C HOME SHELL PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`, the OpenCode v2 rule). A missing executable is a nonce-framed receipt → `providerExecutableUnavailable`; a guard refusal (exit 126) is `operationDenied`. [`owner/provider_lease.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/provider_lease.rs) keeps the leases in the target's `Lanes` (`Lanes::providers`): exact target plus generation, at most 8 live sessions per target (`SessionQuota` beyond; a cleanly settled lease frees its slot), the owner-wide grant budget (background class), an open admitted only after a recognized probe of the same kind under the current generation, and `Lanes::revoke` reaping every lease under one shared 1.75 s deadline. The byte channel is two owner-made sockets per lease (stdio and stderr, each a `mkdtemp` 0700 directory with a 0600 socket, one connection within 15 s, both removed before the pinned `-T` SSH child starts). There is no stream deadline (a provider session lives until exit, close or retirement); close gives the provider 750 ms after stdin EOF, then SIGKILLs the process group. An SSH client failure (exit 255) is `lost`, never a provider exit. The pinned constructor is the U15 Git upload one (the target's own trust/identity policy, no forwarding, no agent identity, no connector).
- **Server (TS).** Codec [`codec.providerLease.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.codec.providerLease.ts), bound-target client [`client.productionProvider.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.client.productionProvider.ts) (queued behind in-flight exchanges), the lease adapter [`sshOwnerProductionProviderLease.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionProviderLease.ts) (pipes, exit and confirmed cancel; status is polled only after both sockets end, so a running session costs no owner round trips) and the source [`sshOwnerProductionProviderSource.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionProviderSource.ts) (live registry owner per acquisition, `descriptorFor`, exact `descriptorServesTarget`, dev fixture first, never production when dev is selected). **Pi is composed:** [`RpcProcess.owner.production.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/Pi/RpcProcess.owner.production.ts) plus `selectedPiRpcOwner(executionTargetId)` in `Adapter.methods.startSession.ts`; under activation a remote Pi without an owner port is a typed "unavailable with the production SSH owner" refusal instead of the legacy fence. **Codex:** [`codexAppServerManager.process.remote.production.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/codex/codexAppServerManager.process.remote.production.ts) is the production `CodexSshOwner` (typed probe and lease start); under activation `startRemoteCodexAppServerProcessAsync` runs the typed version check through the production owner, then refuses with `REMOTE_CODEX_PRODUCTION_START_UNCOMPOSED` (see Open).
- **Bug found by the unit run, fixed.** The client half-closes its stderr socket at once; otherwise that socket never closed after the remote exit and the exit watcher waited forever.
- **Live evidence (run).** Initiator: macOS 27.2 arm64, system OpenSSH, debug `bigbud-ssh-owner --production` (target-free) built on this tree. Target: the OrbStack Debian 13 arm64 guest `bb-ssh-owner-linux-dev`, two disposable non-root `bbdev` sshd (ports 42931 and 42932) in private mount namespaces with a tmpfs home seeded with the stock dotfiles and a tmpfs `/var/tmp` (the guest disk is full); generated host and client keys, explicit known-hosts pin. Binaries (no installs): Pi 1.6.0 `pi-linux-arm64.tar.gz` (SHA-256 `5404547b…b468`, matching the release `SHA256SUMS`) and Codex 0.171.0 `codex-aarch64-unknown-linux-musl.tar.gz` (SHA-256 `bcf50b83…871c`), extracted to `/tmp/tprov` and reachable only through `~/.local/bin` symlinks on port 42931 (login PATH). [`RpcProcess.owner.production.native.test.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/provider/Layers/Pi/RpcProcess.owner.production.native.test.ts): (1) ready — two concurrent Pi sessions on one target through `resolveProductionPiRpcOwner` → `createPiRpcProcess`, each answering `get_state` with `success`, then confirmed stop; the Codex typed probe (`codex-cli 0.171.0`), an owner lease with the question flag answering JSON-RPC `initialize`, then confirmed cancel. 4/4 passes. (2) unavailable (port 42932, neither CLI) — Pi start and the Codex probe both fail with `providerExecutableUnavailable`, and a lease open without a successful probe is `operationDenied`. 3/3 passes. sshd logs: 6 command sessions per ready run and 2 per unavailable run, exactly as designed; no `pi`, `codex` or SSH session left on the guest and no owner left on the Mac after the passing runs. Two owners leaked earlier when vitest timed out a hung run (the fixture's `finally` never ran); they were killed by hand with their fixture roots. Environment findings from the live run, not product bugs: the stock guest home and `/var/tmp` are on the full disk (Pi `ENOSPC` creating `~/.pi/agent/sessions`; Codex's SQLite state migration "disk is full" because the fixed environment has no `TMPDIR`).
- **Checks.** See the plan's W2 T-prov status for the final command list and counts.
- **Open (decisions, not done).** (a) **Codex app-server consumption:** the Codex manager (`codexAppServerManager.*`) is built on `ChildProcess` (`killed`, `exitCode`, `on('exit')`, `killChildTree`), and the owner rule forbids a synthetic child; consuming `OwnedCodexAppServer` needs a process-handle refactor of the manager. Also the adapter always passes the local orchestration MCP `-c` overrides, which the owner path refuses by design. (b) **Pi resume:** Pi keeps session files under the account's `~/.pi/agent/sessions`, outside any workspace the owner authorizes, so a production resume is refused typed (`PiProductionResumeUnsupportedError`). (c) **Provider environment:** the fixed `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`/`LC_ALL=C` environment (OpenCode v2 rule) also applies to the agents' own tool commands; legacy ran them in the login environment. (d) **npm installs:** the ELF guard refuses `#!/usr/bin/env node` launchers (npm `pi`, npm `codex`); release binaries pass. (e) A custom `CODEX_HOME` (`homePath`) is refused typed. (f) An SSH child survives an owner crash (process group of its own); no retained-owner adoption for provider leases.

## U15 production owner Git receive (push) and scp/alias remotes (26 November 2026)

Built from the plan's "U15 design entry: production owner Git receive (push)". The activation flag is unchanged and no commit was produced.

1. **Trusted approval producer (server).** `GitCore.initiatingPush.approval.ts` runs only inside the user-initiated push (`git.runStackedAction`). Through the fenced executor (`--no-lazy-fetch`, no network) it reads the repository identity, the branch, `HEAD` (the source OID) and the tracking ref `refs/remotes/<remote>/<branch>` (the expected old OID). The existing guard `pushSelectedInitiatingBranch` then re-checks the scope and calls the bounded transport once. Two guard additions: an optional producer-resolved `endpoint` per destination (needed for scp and alias URLs), and a distinct `stale` push outcome with a typed message.
2. **Gate library (Rust).** `git_receive_gate.advertised.rs` adds `bind_advertised`. It reads a complete v0 advertisement:
   - bounds of 16 MiB and 65,520 bytes per packet;
   - exactly one destination entry;
   - placeholder, shallow and `.have` lines handled.

   A moved or absent ref returns `StaleRef`. Local Git gets a synthesized single-ref advertisement with narrowed capabilities. The existing fixture conductor is unchanged.

3. **Owner conductor (Rust).** `owner_git_receive.rs` reuses the upload accept, socket, admits and kill helpers (now shared). The pinned child runs the fixed guard `owner_git_receive/guard.sh`, which execs `git-receive-pack` with `receive.denyNonFastForwards=true` and `receive.denyDeletes=true` forced through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`. The conductor flow:
   1. Bind the full advertisement; `stale` if the live ref is not the expected old OID.
   2. For an equal approval, answer `unchanged`.
   3. Otherwise gate one exact command; any deviation is `refused`.
   4. Revalidate the lease, forward the command, and pump the pack (1 GiB cap).
   5. Forward the report to local Git only when it is confirmed (`accepted` or `rejected`).

   Anything after forwarding that is uncertain is `unconfirmed`. A scoped watchdog kills the process group and shuts down the socket on close or at the 120 s deadline. The lease lives in the target's lanes (`git_receive`, separate from upload) and is reaped on revoke. A shared `owner/lease_worker.rs` now backs both the upload and receive leases.

4. **Protocol.** Request 110–112 (`git_receive_open`, `git_receive_status`, `git_receive_close`) and Reply 66 (`git_receive`). New messages `GitReceiveOpen`, `GitReceiveLease`, `GitReceiveState`, `GitReceivePhase` and `GitReceiveReceipt` are in `v1.workspace_git.proto`. No new ErrorCode. Frame and grant goldens are in `v1.golden.gitReceive.ts` (spread from the upload golden module) and `verify-rust/tests/support/git_receive_vectors.rs`. The frozen product-verb list names the three verbs. Admission: production verb on macOS and Linux; status and close are lease-local control.
5. **TypeScript.** The codec is `sshOwnerPickerProof.codec.gitReceive.ts`. Both U15 lease kinds are routed through `codec.gitLease.ts`, so the near-limit `codec.ts` and `codec.outcome.ts` changed by 3 and 1 lines. The client is `client.productionGitReceive.ts`. `GitCore.ownerSocketLease.ts` is shared with upload, and `bridgeOwnerGitLease` was extracted from `openOwnerGitStream`.
   - The transport is `GitCore.selectedStream.receive.ts`: one destination only. It pushes `<source OID>:<ref>` with `--no-follow-tags --recurse-submodules=no --no-signed`, and the settled owner receipt decides the outcome.
   - Selected-mode push (`GitStatus.push.ts`) routes like fetch:
     - HTTP(S), file and local paths, and third-party SSH hosts, run as ordinary or U14-fenced `git push` (`GitCore.pushRoute.ts`, sharing `ordinaryGitTransport` with fetch).
     - Bigbud targets, or an unreadable target set, take the owner path.
     - Mixed or unsupported URLs are refused.
     - Publishing a new branch on a target is refused.

     Before this change, every initiating push under the owner selection was refused.

6. **scp-style and alias remotes (user-approved).** `GitCore.remoteEndpoint.ts` parses `ssh://` and `[user@]host:path` remotes and resolves the host with `resolveProfileHostAuth` (HostName with `%h` and `%%`, User, Port).
   - Routing (fetch and push) matches every spelling.
   - Serving (upload and receive) requires the dialled host, user (default: the local account) and port (default: 22) to equal the descriptor.
   - The resolver refuses whole configs containing `Match`, an `Include` outside `~/.ssh`, or `UseKeychain` (this development Mac's config is refused). In that case the literal spelling is kept, which is the previous rule. Recorded residual in the plan entry.
7. **Live run (run).** Initiator: this macOS host, debug `bigbud-ssh-owner --production` built on this tree. Target: OrbStack Debian 13 arm64 guest `bb-ssh-owner-linux-dev`, Git 2.47.3. The sshd was a disposable non-root `bbdev` instance on port 42977 under `/tmp/u15r` (tmpfs), with a generated host key and a Mac-generated client key. The bare repository was `/tmp/u15r/ws/repo.git`. [The native test](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/git/Layers/GitCore.selectedStream.receive.native.test.ts) uses the scp remote `bbu15r:/tmp/u15r/ws/repo.git`, an alias declared only in a private HOME's `~/.ssh/config` (HostName, User and Port). Each run:
   1. Fetched through the upload lease.
   2. Pushed one approved fast-forward through `GitStatus.pushCurrentBranch`; the bare `main` advanced to it.
   3. Moved the remote behind the client's back, then pushed again. The push was refused as stale, and the bare `main` stayed at the moved commit.

   Four passes in total; the last three were consecutive after a test fix. The bare history grew exactly 1 + 2 × 4 commits, and no refused commit landed. sshd logged 36 accepted sessions, matching 3 owner sessions plus 5 test-helper sessions per run. No `git-receive-pack` or `git-upload-pack` and no owner socket directory remained.

- **Checks.**
  - macOS owner crate: `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 71 + 557 (8 ignored) + 8 + 2. That includes 7 `bind_advertised` tests and 6 conductor tests against a real local `git receive-pack`: accept, stale, refused, forced non-fast-forward rejection, unchanged, and the fixed command.
  - `cargo fmt` clean on this lane's files.
  - Strict Clippy `--all-targets` on macOS and `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`: no finding in this lane's code. The only error at the time was in the T-term lane's `owner/terminal_inspect.rs`.
  - Protocol: `verify-rust` 3 + 10; TS goldens 11.
  - Structural checker: 25 tests; 0 violations on the 9 new or changed routing files. The two pre-existing `GitCore.selectedStream.ts` shim findings are present at HEAD.
  - Server `tsc` (8 GiB) 0 errors; explicit-path oxfmt and oxlint clean on 26 files.
  - Focused Vitest `src/git` plus the `src/ssh` codec, client and production files: 115 files passed (3 skipped: env-gated native tests), 661 tests.
- **Not run / open.**
  - Linux-native owner tests and a Linux-initiator live run: the guest no longer has a Rust toolchain and its root disk is full.
  - Branch creation on a target, and multi-URL push to a target, stay refused.
  - The refused-config residual (item 6) needs a product decision.

## W2 T-shell production ACP bridge and shared shell recipe (26 November 2026)

Follow-up to the T-shell production slice. The activation flag and every wire field are unchanged; no new protocol number was used. No commit was produced.

- **Production ACP bridge composed.** [`remoteWorkspaceAcpBridge.production.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-workspace-bridge/remoteWorkspaceAcpBridge.production.ts): under production activation, a direct `ssh:` remote workspace (not the dev fixture, not a remote-agent target) now gets the selected ACP bridge. The composition is in `prepareAcpRemoteWorkspaceSession`, plus `ownerSelected` in `createRemoteWorkspaceAcpBridge`. File reads and writes use the live owner's bound-target workspace verbs (T-files), resolved per call with `descriptorFor` and `descriptorServesTarget`. Their session-FS fence uses the owner epoch and the target generation. The approved `pwd` terminal uses the ACP exec lease, and approvals are bound to the last observed generation. A missing workspace root is refused by the session-FS binding; it is never defaulted and there is no legacy fallback. Unit: [`remoteWorkspaceAcpBridge.production.test.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-workspace-bridge/remoteWorkspaceAcpBridge.production.test.ts) checks selection, capabilities (`writeTextFile` and `terminal` true), an owner read, an owner write with the exact target, root and relative path, a terminal producing `${root}\n` and exit 0, and a refused non-`pwd` command that opens no lease.

(1) Cursor CLI `2026.11.22-e9042b2` (`cursor-agent --trust acp`, logged in): `initialize`, `session/new` and `session/prompt` completed through the production bridge. The account answered "Upgrade your plan to continue", so the model made no tool call and no file or terminal callback was exercised. (2) Devin CLI `3000.14.30821` (`devin acp`, logged in): the agent issued an agent-to-client request, and our client's RPC server crashed with `Cannot convert <uuid> to a BigInt`. `effect@4.0.0-beta.43` `RpcMessage.RequestId` does `BigInt(id)`, so string JSON-RPC ids from the agent are unsupported. The session then hung until the 240 s test timeout. That timeout left a `devin acp` and an owner process; both were identified by start time and removed, and the prompt is now bounded at 150 s inside the runtime scope so teardown always runs. This client bug is outside T-shell and is reported, not fixed.

The command runs in its own process group with the remote watchdog (session gone or deadline + 2 s ⇒ TERM, then KILL), so no exec path leaves remote processes. The proof tests were updated to the shared builder. Live: the production shell native test passed twice more with the shared recipe (owner SHA-256 `fc1e8089…4ceb8`).
- **Provider-shell fixed-tool rule (checked, not changed).** The rule is intended: the implementation plan's provider-shell entry says "Fixed file, search, listing, and patch actions use typed workspace verbs; do not forward the existing raw process envelope." The re-check found a larger production gap. Both in-tree bridges (the MCP bridge template and the Pi bridge template) send every tool as `execRemote('sh', ['-lc', script, …])`, with stdin for writes, edits and patches. The selected and production envelope refuses argv and stdin, so in production all of these bridge tools are refused, not only `cat`/`ls`: read, write, edit, `bash`, grep, ls and patch. Options are in the slice report; nothing was loosened.

Rust: `rustfmt --check` clean on the shell files; strict Clippy (macOS and Linux target) has no finding in them; shell unit tests 31 passed after the recipe change. Later whole-crate runs were blocked by the providers lane's in-flight `ProviderLeaseScope` fields. Guest sshd, scratch and keys removed; no `cursor-agent`, `devin acp`, owner or remote `sleep` left.

### W2 T-backend follow-up: owner leak, golden split, shared lease worker (26 November 2026)

- **Owner leak, root cause.** The production client already reaps a spawned owner whose session failed closed: two new tests inject a malformed reply, once on the fake owner (`malformed-reply` mode) and once on the real `target/debug/bigbud-ssh-owner` (a mocked frame decoder), and both find no owner left after `shutdown()`, including through the live fixtures' `Effect.promise(run).pipe(Effect.ensuring(shutdown))` shape. The leak is elsewhere. After its primary session ends, a production owner keeps serving its attach socket indefinitely (`startup_production.rs`: `serve_primary_session`, then `attachment.serve`). So when a run ends before its handle shuts down (an interrupted or timed-out test, a killed worker), the owner outlives the run. The fixture then deleted its state directory, leaving the owner unattachable and unreapable. The two leftover owners carried `--opencode-v2-discover`, so they came from a discovery-mode OpenCode live run, not from the T-backend test (explicit `--opencode-v2-service`).
- **Fix (test fixtures).** `makeProductionFixture().dispose()` (used by every production-client test and by `withProductionV2Owner`, which creates the `bigbud-oc-*` directories) now reaps every process started with `--state-dir <root>/` (SIGKILL, wait at most 3 s) before it removes the directory it created. `sshOwner.productionClient.failClosed.native.test.ts` proves it on the real owner: an interrupted run with a failed-closed session and no `shutdown()` leaves a live owner, and disposal leaves none and removes the root. Production state directories are durable profile state (trust store, retirement journal) and are never removed by the client.
- **Design decision.** Option (b) was chosen and built (plan §4 U18); see the next entry.
- **Golden split.** The production lease spreads (U15 Git upload/receive and T-backend) moved from `v1.golden.cases.ts` into `v1.golden.production.ts` (`productionLeaseWireCases`); every lane's cases are unchanged. `v1.golden.cases.ts` is 400 lines. TS goldens 11, `verify-rust` 3 + 10.
- **Shared lease worker.** `owner/lease_worker.rs` (the T-git worker) now takes any `Clone` outcome. The T-backend `owner/backend.worker.rs` is a thin wrapper (token, socket path) over it, so the Git upload/receive leases and the deployment-step and relay leases share one thread, cancellation, bounded retire and cancel-on-drop. Git callers are unchanged.

[`owner/terminal_inspect.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/terminal_inspect.rs) runs the fixed `ps -e -ww -o pid=,ppid=,pgid=,tpgid=,command=` as one mux session over the target's verified production master (5 s, 512 KiB, exit 0 required) and returns only the exact lease's shell (first) and its descendants, at most 512 processes, commands capped at 1 KiB, `truncated` when bounded. A lease without a witness PID is `outcome_unknown`. Production only; fixtures refuse. All terminal verbs now go through one owner dispatcher arm ([`owner/terminal_dispatch.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/terminal_dispatch.rs)).

A prepare may carry one such path (owner and TS both refuse anything else before the wire); at launch the wrapper re-checks it with `grep -qxF` against `/etc/shells` and `-f`/`-x`, otherwise prints a refusal marker that the owner maps to `terminalShellUnavailable`. Server: [`ShellProfiles.sshOwner.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/terminal/Layers/ShellProfiles.sshOwner.ts) lists and resolves profiles from the owner inventory; the native spawn sends only the listed path for the selected profile id.

[`sshOwnerProductionTerminal.lazy.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerProductionTerminal.lazy.ts) builds it from the live owner's artifact (runtime metadata: SHA-256 of the owner binary) and refuses `sshOwnerUnavailable` while no owner is open. ### U18 launcher-tied lifetime for non-surviving owners (26 November 2026)

- **Owner (Rust).** `startup_production.rs` adds `--launcher-lifetime`: `ProfileKind::survives_launcher` (`startup_profile.rs`) is false only for `desktop-dev`, and the flag is required for that kind and a `Conflict` refusal for every surviving kind or when repeated. With the flag, the owner binds the existing `owner_attachment::LauncherLifetime` (FD 3, `SOCK_STREAM`, `FD_CLOEXEC`) before any child is spawned and passes it to `attachment.serve`, so any activity or hang-up on FD 3 retires the owner. Unit test: `only_a_non_surviving_profile_is_tied_to_its_launcher`.
- **Server (TS).** `sshOwnerProfileLifetime.ts` is the single rule (`sshOwnerProfileSurvivesLauncher`, `sshOwnerBindingTiedToLauncher`). The production client's shutdown rule now uses it. The production argv adds the flag, and `startPickerProofOwner` opens the FD 3 socket (the existing supervised stdio slot) for the same profiles. Surviving profiles are unchanged; `sshOwner.productionLaunch.test.ts` (desktop-packaged) still sees the exact old argv, and `sshOwner.productionClient.spawn.test.ts` (desktop-dev) now expects `--launcher-lifetime`.
- **Evidence (macOS arm64, real `target/debug/bigbud-ssh-owner`).** `sshOwner.productionClient.launcherLifetime.native.test.ts` starts the owner from a separate Bun launcher process and kills that launcher with SIGKILL (no shutdown or cleanup runs). The desktop-dev owner retired in 121, 259 and 152 ms over three runs (bound 5 s). A desktop-packaged owner still outlived its launcher, and fixture disposal reaped it. In `sshOwner.productionClient.failClosed.native.test.ts`, a desktop-dev session that fails closed with no shutdown now retires through FD 3 on its own; the fake-owner fail-closed test still passes. Linux: strict Clippy for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` and the parse unit test; no Linux launcher-kill run (the guest home is out of space).

They are surviving-profile fakes that earlier fixtures detached and then deleted; the new fixture disposal reap prevents that class.

## U17 SSH config tolerance and U15 push follow-ups (26 November 2026)

User decision U17 (plan §4) applied, plus two U15 push follow-ups. No commit was produced.

1. **U17 owner (Rust).**
   - Ten harmless directives join the shared vocabulary: `UseKeychain`, `TCPKeepAlive`, `Compression`, `LogLevel`, `VisualHostKey`, `EscapeChar`, `IPQoS`, `ConnectionAttempts`, `FingerprintHash`, `NumberOfPasswordPrompts`. `config::is_inert` accepts and ignores them, together with `AddKeysToAgent`, `IdentitiesOnly` and user `Local`/`Remote`/`DynamicForward`. All are provably inert under the `ssh -F /dev/null` launch, which forces `IdentitiesOnly=yes`, `AddKeysToAgent=no` and `ClearAllForwardings=yes`.
   - `IgnoreUnknown` (`config/ignore_unknown.rs`) uses the first active value and ignores only keywords outside the OpenSSH 9.x keyword list.
   - `Match` evaluation is unchanged: the safe subset was already evaluated, and `exec` refuses only while it could apply.
   - Not changed: scope privacy (0700 `~/.ssh`), the synthetic `~/` and Includes outside the scope. See the plan's U17 entry; alias targets on a world-readable `~/.ssh` stay refused by the owner.
2. **U17 resolver (TS).**
   - Per-host evaluation moved into `sshConfigProfileAliases.eval.ts`, which made the main resolver smaller. It uses the safe `Match` subset with the same `exec` rule as the owner, decides unknown keywords per host (`IgnoreUnknown` with `sshConfigOpenSshKeywords.ts`), and keeps located refusals in source order.
   - Production resolution (`resolveProductionTargetDescriptor`, `GitCore.remoteEndpoint`) reads `~/` Includes outside `~/.ssh` that pass `assertSafeReadableFile`: account- or root-owned, writable by no other account in the whole directory chain, within 256 KiB.
   - Remote routing and serving now fail closed on a config that cannot be evaluated (owner path / refusal). The earlier "host as written" fallback is removed.
   - On this Mac (`~/.ssh` 0755, config 0644, top-level `Include ~/.orbstack/ssh/config`, `UseKeychain`, `LocalForward`), a value-free probe resolved all 18 declared hosts plus `github.com` with 0 refusals; before, every host was refused.
3. **Push rejection reason.** The receive transport keeps Git's ` ! [rejected]` status line (bounded, no URL or path). Being behind the remote becomes "the remote branch has commits you do not have (non-fast-forward). Pull, then push again." The guard returns "The selected Git push was rejected: <reason>" (`InitiatingGitPushRejection`); without a reason it keeps the generic message.
4. **First publish on a target (U15, no product decision needed).** An all-zero expected old OID is a first publish:
   - Gate library: `validate_creation` and an internal creation-aware prefix gate; the public `authorize_receive_prefix` stays strict.
   - `bind_advertised` requires the ref to be absent; present is `StaleRef`. Local Git sees the live tips as `.have` lines, with the capabilities on the first one, exactly as receive-pack writes them; `capabilities^{}` appears only when nothing else exists.
   - The owner admits only `<zero> <source> <ref>`.
   - Server: the producer emits `publish` with zeros; the guard requires no upstream and the same branch name, and reports `setUpstream: true`; the transport pushes `-u <remote> refs/heads/<b>:refs/heads/<b>`.
   - **Bug found live, fixed.** The first build put `.have` lines after `capabilities^{}`, which Git rejects; the scripted-client unit test had not caught it.
5. **Live run (run).** Same setup as the U15 receive section:
   - macOS debug production owner → OrbStack Debian 13 guest;
   - disposable non-root sshd on port 42977, bare repository `/tmp/u15r/ws/repo.git`;
   - scp alias `bbu15r` in a private HOME.

   The native test now does all remote reads and writes through the owner (no direct `ssh`). Each run:
   1. Fetch.
   2. Approved fast-forward: advanced.
   3. Another owner client pushes; our push is refused as stale.
   4. Fetch, then the diverged push is refused with the surfaced non-fast-forward reason.
   5. First publish of `feature-<run>`: created on the target, upstream recorded.

   Results:
   - **Passes:** 3 consecutive, with exact guest accounting — +3 refs, +6 `main` commits, +48 sshd sessions (16 owner sessions per run).
   - **Leftovers:** no `git-receive-pack`, `git-upload-pack` or owner socket directory.
   - **Earlier failures:** 3 runs failed on the advertisement bug above, and 3 more could not start the owner while the launcher-lifetime lane was mid-change.

- **Checks.**
  - `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner --lib --bins`: 72 + 568 (8 ignored). New: 2 creation gate tests, 1 owner first-publish test against real `git receive-pack`, 3 `real_shape_tests`, 1 `ignore_unknown` test. Two entries in the base reject list (`LocalForward`, bare `IgnoreUnknown`) were removed as U17 admits them.
  - rustfmt clean on this lane's files.
  - Strict Clippy `--all-targets --keep-going`, macOS and `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`: the only error is the T-files lane's `guarded_write_stage.rs:107` (unused items).
  - `verify-rust` 3 + 10; TS goldens 11; structural checker 25 tests, 0 new violations (3 at HEAD in `startupDiagnostic.ts`).
  - `tsc`: only another lane's `ws/http.threadTools.remoteWorkspaceTool.test.ts`.
  - oxfmt and oxlint clean on 21 files.
  - TS tests: `sshConfigProfileAliases.realShape.test.ts` (4), plus first publish, reason extraction and the diverged push in `GitCore.selectedStream.receive.test.ts`.
  - Focused Vitest `src/git` plus the `src/ssh` config, production-target, codec and client files: 118 files passed (4 skipped: env-gated native tests), 680 tests.
- **Other-lane test touched (decided semantics).** In `sshOwnerProductionTargets.userProfile.test.ts` (T-alias), the included `Match host *.corp exec true` became `Match exec true host *.corp`. Under U17, and as in the owner, a false earlier `host` criterion proves the block cannot apply; the test's intent (an included unevaluable `Match` refuses) is kept.
- **Open.** Closed below by the owner-scope decision: the alias scope policy, and the discovery `IgnoreUnknown` gap.
  - Multi-URL push to a target stays refused.
  - Linux-native owner tests: the guest has no Rust toolchain.

## W2 T-prov U19 and U20 (26 November 2026)

Follow-up to "W2 T-prov production Pi and Codex provider leases", implementing the decisions recorded in the plan as U19 (provider execution like the old path) and U20 (provider leases). The activation flag is unchanged; no commit, tag or release artifact was produced. Protocol numbers stay within the T-prov reservation: `ProviderLeaseScope` gains `session_file = 6` and `codex_home = 7` (additive, in `v1.provider_lease.proto`), with TS and Rust goldens `probe_provider_resume` and `probe_provider_codex_home` and their grant digests.

- **U20 (a) Codex sessions.** [`codexAppServerManager.processHandle.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/codex/codexAppServerManager.processHandle.ts) defines the narrow `CodexAppServerProcess` the manager uses (pipes, `killed`/`exitCode`/`signalCode`, `exit`/`error`/`close`); a local `ChildProcess` satisfies it structurally, and `OwnedCodexAppServerProcess` implements it for an owner lease with no PID (owner-confirmed exit, `SIGKILL` after its own close, `SIGHUP` for a lost transport). `killChildTree` closes a lease through its confirmed cancel. Under activation `startRemoteCodexAppServerProcessAsync` resolves the production selection and returns the lease process; `REMOTE_CODEX_PRODUCTION_START_UNCOMPOSED` is gone. The Codex adapter skips the local-only orchestration MCP `-c` settings and the expected MCP server for an owner-selected remote runtime (the dynamic orchestration tools stay).
- **U20 (b) Pi resume, (e) `CODEX_HOME`.** The launch script resolves both with `readlink -e` and admits a resume file only beneath the resolved `~/.pi/agent/sessions` (regular file) and a Codex home only as an existing directory beneath the resolved home, exporting the resolved `CODEX_HOME`; otherwise exit 126 → `operation_denied`. The owner arms opens per exact checked scope (kind, generation, cwd, resume file, Codex home; a bounded set of 16 per target), and the Pi port probes the exact resume scope before opening, so a refusal surfaces as the typed `PiProductionResumeRefusedError` / `CodexProductionHomeRefusedError` before any provider starts.
- **U20 (f) owner crash.** Remote: the shell lane's watchdog was extracted to the shared [`transport/session_guard.sh`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/session_guard.sh) (`setsid`, or `perl setpgrp`; a watchdog kills the group when the SSH session process is gone or an optional deadline passes); the T-shell recipe and provider starts both use it. Local: [`owner_provider_lease/local_guard.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_provider_lease/local_guard.rs) runs the SSH client under a `/bin/sh` group leader whose watcher reads a pipe whose only write end the owner holds (close-on-exec); owner death, including SIGKILL, closes it and the watcher kills the whole group. This is the same on macOS and Linux (`PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` was not used: it fires when the spawning thread exits). A client path that is not an absolute executable stays a spawn refusal (not a shell exit 127).
- **U19 login environment and npm launchers.** Discovery and the guard still run in the fixed environment, which now carries `USER`, `LOGNAME` and `LANG`; the provider is exec'd as `/bin/sh -lc 'cd -- "$1"; shift; exec "$@"'` (the old path's `sh -l`) with the opened descriptor and the owner's argv, and `LC_ALL` unset. The OpenCode v2 supervisor does the same and keeps its isolated XDG profile. An npm launcher (a single-link, owner- or root-owned, not group/other-writable `#!…node` file inside `node_modules` with a trusted directory chain) runs as `node ENTRY`, where node comes from the login `command -v node` or known locations and must pass the same ELF guard. OpenCode v2 discovery stays ELF-only.
- **Live evidence (run).** macOS 27.2 arm64 production owner (debug, this tree) → OrbStack Debian 13 arm64 guest, disposable non-root sshd per case in private mount namespaces with a tmpfs home and `/var/tmp`. (1) Release binaries (Pi 1.6.0 SHA-256 `5404547b…`, Codex 0.171.0 `bcf50b83…`), 3 rounds after a first pass: two concurrent Pi sessions and an owner Codex lease (`initialize`); Pi resume of a crafted v3 session file under `~/.pi/agent/sessions` (`get_state` reports that file and its id), with a symlink that escapes the sessions directory and a file outside it both refused; owner SIGKILL mid-Pi-lease, after which no `bigbud-provider-lease` process remained on the Mac within 5 s and no Pi or guard process remained on the guest within 10 s; and a full `CodexAppServerManager.startSession` (activation on, registry owner) reaching `ready` with a custom `CODEX_HOME` that Codex populated, plus a typed home refusal for `/etc`. (2) npm-installed `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent@1.6.0` and `@openai/codex@0.171.0` (installed on the Mac with `--os=linux --cpu=arm64`, because npm is OOM-killed in the guest) with only the launchers and node on the login PATH: all four cases 3/3. (3) Neither CLI: typed `providerExecutableUnavailable` 3/3. (4) OpenCode v2 2.1.4 (`7f32303f…`, the existing pin) through the login environment: discovery test 3/3; a sample of the live `serve --stdio` process showed the profile `PATH` (with `~/.local/bin`) and no `LC_ALL`. sshd command sessions: 101, 6, 76 and 8 on the four targets. No owner, guard, SSH client or provider remained on either host; the guest scratch, keys and downloads were removed. Setup findings (environment, fixed): the first OpenCode run was refused because the new disposable sshd lacked `AllowTcpForwarding local`.
- **Checks.** Owner crate on macOS: `rustfmt --check` clean on this slice's files; `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 72 + 570 (8 ignored) + 8 + 2 passed (includes the T-shell tests on the shared guard and `local_guard` tests that drop the owner end and assert the group dies); strict Clippy on macOS and `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` reports nothing in this slice's files (findings remain in other lanes' `transport/workspace/entry_ops.rs` and `owner/guarded_write_stage.rs`). Protocol goldens: TS 11, `verify-rust` 3 + 10. Server: `tsc --noEmit` (8 GiB) 0 errors; focused Vitest across `provider/Layers/Pi`, `provider/Layers/Codex`, `codex/` and the lease adapter: 79 files, 498 passed, 5 skipped; explicit-path oxfmt and oxlint clean; every changed file at most 400 lines. The guest `cargo test` still cannot run (4 GiB cgroup OOM while linking).

## W2 T-files production guarded save and session-FS entry verbs (26 November 2026)

Production-owner slice; the activation flag is unchanged and no commit was produced. Wire numbers, all from this lane's reservation: Request `workspace_entry_info = 122`, `workspace_rename = 123`, `workspace_append = 124`; Reply `workspace_entry_info = 73`, `workspace_entry_change = 74` (messages in `v1.workspace_write.proto`). Initiator: macOS 27.2.1 arm64, OpenSSH 10.5p1, debug `bigbud-ssh-owner --production`. Target: Debian 13 arm64 guest, disposable non-root `bbdev` sshd (OpenSSH 10.0p2) on `192.168.139.96:42974`, generated keys and pin, scratch `/tmp/bb-tguard` (tmpfs). The guest's `~/workspace-mode-proof*` and `/tmp/oc2-owner-proof` were not touched; no Linux-owner or port-22 run was attempted.

- **Per-target guarded-write lane (owner).** A production target's `Lanes` now carry a guarded-write lane ([`owner/guarded_write.production.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/guarded_write.production.rs)), created on first use, bound to that target's current generation (a generation change drops any draft) and keeping its never-evicted finalized-id set per target. The stage takes each save's own root under the production rule instead of the fixture's single root; the compiled guarded recipe keeps its loopback-only fence for fixtures and, in production, uses the target's own trust/identity policy and `root_authority`. Production admits the five guarded verbs; the fixture's admission-time storage check stays fixture-only because a production target is resolved after admission and dispatch re-checks that target's durable state.
- **Production journal (server).** [`SshGuardedWriteJournal.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Layers/SshGuardedWriteJournal.ts) is the dev journal's code, unchanged in semantics, parameterized by a fixed table; the dev journal keeps `dev_ssh_guarded_write_journal`. [`SshOwnerGuardedWriteJournal.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Layers/SshOwnerGuardedWriteJournal.ts) bootstraps `ssh_owner_guarded_write_journal` in the server's state database, admitted only under production activation without the dev fixture. Each row's scope is `production:<owner epoch>:<target generation>` plus the exact target, root, path, base and attempted SHA-256 and size; one autocommitted claim authorizes exactly one APPLY, receipts are recorded once, and a different owner or generation can never replay an intent. [`wsSshOwnerProduction.workspace.guarded.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerProduction.workspace.guarded.ts) composes it; `ws.ts` opens the journal and, if it cannot, guarded saves stay refused `not-dispatched`.
- **Session-FS entry verbs.** [`transport/workspace/entry_ops.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/workspace/entry_ops.rs): entry info (`python3 -I` lstat after the shared symlink-refusing walk; birth time falls back to ctime), rename (both paths walked; the destination must not exist; `mv -n` plus a post-check) and append (at most 48 KiB, staged beside the file, exact size check, one append). Missing acknowledgements are `outcome_unknown`. [`remoteWorkspaceSessionFsBridge.ownerEntries.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-workspace-bridge/remoteWorkspaceSessionFsBridge.ownerEntries.ts) maps `exists`/`stat` to entry info, `readdir`/`readdirWithTypes` to the existing directory list (a truncated listing is refused), `rm` to entry info plus the existing delete (with `recursive`/`force` semantics and `ENOTEMPTY`/`ENOENT`), `rename` to the new verb (a file-over-file rename deletes the destination first, two typed steps rather than one atomic rename) and `appendFile` to ordered 48 KiB appends (a missing file is created through the existing write). Production session-FS now binds the full bound-target client per session root.
- **Live evidence (run, 3/3 each).** (1) [Guarded save](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/wsSshOwnerProduction.workspace.guarded.native.test.ts): the preview advertised `edit.mode = guarded`; a save with the read hash returned `saved` and the attempted SHA-256; the same operation id replayed the journal receipt without a second dispatch; a stale-hash save was a terminal `conflict`; the guest file held `saved`. The first run surfaced the admission-time `storageUnavailable` bug above, fixed before these runs. (2) [Session-FS](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-workspace-bridge/remoteWorkspaceSessionFsBridge.production.native.test.ts): mkdir, write, read, append, append-creates, exists, stat (size and a real mtime), readdir, readdirWithTypes at the root, rename, rename over an existing file, `rm` non-empty without `recursive` → `ENOTEMPTY`, `rm` file, forced `rm` of a missing file, stat → `ENOENT`, recursive `rm`; paths outside the root refused. (3) The production Files test still passes. 196 sshd command sessions over the nine runs; the sibling `outside` directory stayed empty and no staging file remained.

## W2 T-prov U19 follow-up: captured login environment and OpenCode npm launchers (26 November 2026)

Refines U19 (plan §4). The activation flag and the wire are unchanged; no commit, tag or release artifact was produced.

- **Login environment, captured apart from profile output.** The earlier `sh -lc 'exec "$@"'` exec let anything a profile printed reach the provider's stdout (its RPC stream). Now the shared [`owner_agent_proxy/provider_discovery.sh`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner_agent_proxy/provider_discovery.sh) `capture_login_env` runs `/bin/sh -lc 'exec /usr/bin/env -0 >&3'` with the login shell's stdout and stderr discarded and only fd 3 read, bounded at 5 s (`timeout -s KILL`) and 64 KiB; it drops `LC_ALL`, `_` and an empty `LANG`, and fails closed (exit 126) on a timeout, an overflow or no `PATH`. The provider is then started by a non-login `/usr/bin/env -i -- <captured entries> /proc/self/fd/9 <owner argv>` (Pi and Codex under the shared session guard). The OpenCode v2 launcher execs its supervisor the same way, and the supervisor passes that environment (minus `LC_ALL`) to OpenCode with its isolated XDG profile overrides on top.
- **One discovery for all three providers.** `provider_discovery.sh` now holds discovery (an explicit configured path only; otherwise login `command -v`, provider extras such as `~/.opencode/bin/opencode`, and known locations), the npm launcher rule and the environment capture. `owner_opencode_v2/discover.sh` shrinks to its `missing` receipt and extra candidate, and the Pi/Codex `launch.sh` keeps only its resume/home checks and argv. OpenCode v2 therefore accepts npm launchers too: the supervisor gets the entry as an extra argument and runs `/proc/self/fd/9 ENTRY serve …` (fd 9 is the guarded node).
- **OpenCode 2.1.4 on npm.** `@opencode/cli@2.1.4` has no node launcher: its postinstall copies the platform ELF over `bin/opencode.exe`, so a real npm install takes the ELF path through `node_modules` (its SHA-256 matched the pin `7f32303f…`). The node-launcher path was exercised with a test-only fixture package (`bigbud-opencode-launcher-fixture`, a `#!/usr/bin/env node` script that spawns the pinned binary).
- **Live evidence (run).** macOS production owner (debug, this tree) → Debian 13 arm64 guest, disposable non-root sshd with a tmpfs home whose `~/.profile` ends with `echo BIGBUD-PROFILE-JUNK-STDOUT` and the same on stderr (prompt-free; `sh -lc` showed both lines). Target 42951: npm-installed Pi 1.6.0 and Codex 0.171.0 (real `#!…node` launchers, installed on the Mac with `--os=linux --cpu=arm64`) and npm `@opencode/cli@2.1.4`; target 42952: the launcher fixture only. 3/3 rounds each of: two concurrent Pi sessions plus an owner Codex `initialize`; Pi resume with escape refusals; owner SIGKILL leaving nothing on either host; a full Codex manager session with a custom `CODEX_HOME`; and OpenCode v2 discovery (ready, 401/200 at 2.1.4, close, restart) on both targets. The strict probe check is itself evidence: a junk line in the probe's stdout would not be a recognized version and no lease could open. A live sample of the launcher-path OpenCode showed `/proc/self/fd/9 …/bigbud-opencode-launcher-fixture/bin/opencode.js serve --stdio …` with the child `opencode.exe`, the profile `PATH` (with `~/.local/bin`), no `LC_ALL` and the isolated `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`. sshd command sessions: 82 and 8. No owner, guard, SSH client or provider remained; the guest scratch, keys and downloads were removed.
- **Checks.** Owner crate on macOS: `rustfmt --check` clean on this slice's files; `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 72 + 574 (9 ignored) + 9 + 2 passed (the OpenCode script-shape test now checks the shared discovery, `select_binary opencode` and `capture_login_env`); strict Clippy on macOS and `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` reports nothing in this slice's files (the remaining finding is another lane's `config/source.rs:62`). Protocol goldens: TS 11, `verify-rust` 3 + 11. Server `tsc --noEmit` (8 GiB): 0 errors. The guest `cargo test` still cannot run (4 GiB cgroup OOM).
- **Open.** A profile that blocks (for example a prompt) now fails the start with `operation_denied` after 5 s instead of hanging. The Linux-only launch-script unit test was not run on Linux.

## W2 T-files follow-up: migration 187 and atomic replace rename (26 November 2026)

No commit; the activation flag is unchanged. Target for the live check: Debian 13 arm64 guest, disposable non-root `bbdev` sshd on `192.168.139.96:42975`, removed afterwards.

- **Migration 187 `SshOwnerGuardedWriteJournal` (Build B-side table).** Adds `ssh_owner_guarded_write_journal` through the three-step migration rule (file in `persistence/Migrations/`, import and `migrationEntries` row in `Migrations.ts`). **187 is above the W4 Build A schema ceiling (184/185):** Build A must not contain it, and a Build A binary reading a database that ran 187 is refused by the existing newer-schema guard. The DDL is one shared function (`createSshGuardedWriteJournalTable`) used by the migration and by the journal's own `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` bootstrap, so either order yields one identical table; the bootstrap stays because the dev journal (no migration) and temporary test databases use it. Test: [`187_SshOwnerGuardedWriteJournal.test.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/persistence/Migrations/187_SshOwnerGuardedWriteJournal.test.ts) — absent at 186, exact columns at 187, bootstrap is a no-op over the migrated table, one claim and one receipt.
- **Atomic replace rename.** `WorkspaceRenameRequest.replace` (additive field 4). Without it the destination must still not exist. With it, an existing regular-file destination is replaced by one `os.replace` (rename(2), which fails rather than copying across filesystems) inside the same two symlink-refusing walks; a directory or symlink destination is refused. Session-FS `rename` now issues this single owner call; the earlier delete-then-rename path is gone. Golden `workspace_rename_replace` added on both sides.
- **Evidence.** Rust: `replace_swaps_a_regular_file_atomically_and_refuses_a_directory` checks the destination ends up with the source's own inode. Live: the plain rename onto an existing `old` answered `alreadyExists`; `replace` answered `done`; the guest showed `old` with the former `new` inode (422593) and content `new`; renaming onto the `escape` symlink was refused `workspacePathDenied` and `outside` stayed empty. The production session-FS and guarded-save native tests then passed 3/3 again.
- **Checks.** `cargo fmt --check` clean; strict Clippy macOS and `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` clean; `cargo test -p bigbud-ssh-owner` 72 + 575 (9 ignored) + 9 + 2 passed. `verify-rust` 3 + 11, TS goldens 11. Server: 48 focused files, 279 passed, 5 skipped; `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors; explicit-path `oxfmt`/`oxlint` clean; every changed file at most 400 lines.

## U17 owner scope follows OpenSSH's checks (26 November 2026)

User decision ("like the old path", recorded under U17 in the plan). No commit was produced.

1. **Production scope (Rust).** `config/source.policy.rs` (new) defines it:
   - `<home>/.ssh/config` is rooted at the real home: `~/` is the home, relative operands start at `.ssh`, and a symlinked home is followed (canonical mapping bound).
   - Directories, configs, Includes and known-hosts files: owned by the account or root, not group- or world-writable; ancestors likewise, or root-owned sticky.
   - Keys: owned by the account and readable by no one else.
   - Default `~/.ssh/known_hosts`.
   - The system config is admitted under the same rule.

   `Scope::open_with(path, openssh)` replaces `Scope::open`; the fixture policy is unchanged. To stay under 400 lines, `source.rs` was split (`source.token.rs` holds binding and revalidation) and `aliases.rs` was split (`aliases.entries.rs`).

2. **Discovery `IgnoreUnknown`.** Discovery keeps the source line of an unknown keyword and skips it exactly when admission does.
3. **Target set (TS).** `sshTargetEndpointsOf` resolves alias targets with the config-only resolver and returns an unknown set (fail closed) when that config cannot be evaluated. The live run first exposed the gap: an alias target known only by name let `bbu15r`, pointing at the same host, route to the fence (the dial failed on DNS).
4. **Value-free probe through the owner's own functions** (`config::aliases::list`, which backs `list_aliases`, and `config::resolve_with_facts`, which `resolve_for_startup` uses), over this Mac's real `~/.ssh/config` and `/etc/ssh/ssh_config`. Ignored test `probe_real_home_config` with `BIGBUD_U17_PROBE_HOME=$HOME`; counts and reasons only.
   - 19 aliases (18 declared plus one from the OrbStack Include) and 15 admitted. Before, 0 were admitted: `~/.ssh` is 0755.
   - Refused: 1 `ProxyCommand` (the OrbStack host; a route change, correct), and 3 hosts with no `IdentityFile` (fail closed, as the TS resolver's `no-identity`).
5. **Live run (run).** OrbStack Debian 13 guest, disposable non-root sshd on port 42977, macOS debug production owner. The native receive test now runs twice per invocation:
   - **Structured target:** as in the earlier run.
   - **Alias target:** the production fixture's user-profile layout (symlinked home, `Include config.d/*.conf`, `IdentityFile ~/.ssh/keys/work_ed25519`, owner-admitted under the new scope), with the scp remote spelled with that alias.

   Each variant covers fetch, approved push, stale refusal, surfaced non-fast-forward and first publish. Three consecutive passes of both variants. Guest accounting reconciles with every attempt: 9 refs, 17 `main` commits and 129 sessions, with no `git-receive-pack`, `git-upload-pack` or socket directory left.

- **Checks.**
  - `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner`: 72 + 574 passed (9 ignored), 1 failed. New: 3 `openssh_scope_tests` (real-shaped home and refusals: a readable key, group-writable config, Include directory or home, writable trust file, symlinked Include, missing default trust file; the fixture scope still refuses the same layout) plus the ignored probe.
  - The failure is the T-term lane's `owner::terminal::tests::a_stale_generation_cannot_cancel_or_attach_a_reserved_terminal`, which fails intermittently at its fixture directory creation (`terminal.tests.rs:32`; the name is pid plus nanoseconds). It passed in 1 of 3 reruns and touches no config scope.
  - rustfmt check clean. Strict Clippy `--all-targets --keep-going` clean on macOS and `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`.
  - `verify-rust` 3 + 11; TS goldens 11.
  - Server `tsc` 0 errors; oxfmt and oxlint clean.
  - Structural checker 0 violations on the changed routing and test files.
  - Focused Vitest (`src/git` plus the `src/ssh` config, target, codec and client files): 118 files, 682 tests passed (4 files skipped: env-gated native tests).
- **Open.**
  - Default identities: closed below.
  - Linux-native owner tests: the guest has no Rust toolchain.

## W2 T-shell U21 ACP shell grant, typed glob and a completed Devin task (26 November 2026)

Implements decision U21 (finish-line plan §4). The activation flag is unchanged; no commit was produced.

- **Wire (T-shell range).** `RunAcpShell run_acp_shell = 95` (lease, workspace root, cwd, ACP session id, tool call id, command text, timeout) and `SHELL_ROLE_ACP_SHELL = 4` in [v1.shell.proto](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/v1.shell.proto); status and close reuse `shell_status`/`shell_close` with the new role, and the reply is `shell = 56`. Goldens: the `run_acp_shell` frame and grant digest on both sides; the verbs list. Cross-role: the TS grants test adds `run_acp_shell` to the pairwise-distinct production exec set. The Rust test `acp_shell_grant_is_not_substitutable_with_acp_pwd_g2_or_pty_grants` covers the `pwd` recipe, provider and thread shells, role-specific status, read and PTY grants, and the picker/control classes.
- **Owner (Rust).** `acp_shell_launch` in `transport/shell.lease.rs` validates the session and tool-call identities, the command and the workspace scope. It runs through the shared shell recipe and session guard with the provider-shell bounds. The dispatch arm in `owner/shell_lease.rs` uses the `AcpShell` role. Production admission adds the verb; it is interactive-class in the grant budget. Unit test: an ACP shell lease never answers the fixed ACP role.
- **Approval flow (TS).** `AcpOwnerCommand.operation` is `print-working-directory` or `shell-command`, with the exact `command` text. [`remoteWorkspaceAcpTerminal.owner.command.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/remote-workspace-bridge/remoteWorkspaceAcpTerminal.owner.command.ts) builds that text (args POSIX-quoted; at most 64 KiB; no NUL). The producer issues a single-use receipt per command; consumption requires the same operation and text, and an environment is always refused. `pwd` is unchanged. The production owner gains `runAcpShell` (120 s ceiling); an owner without it refuses every non-`pwd` command, as before.
- **Typed glob.** [`http.threadTools.remoteWorkspaceGlob.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ws/http.threadTools.remoteWorkspaceGlob.ts) matches ripgrep `-g` style patterns (`**`, `*`, `?`, `[…]`, `{a,b}`; base-name matching without `/`) over one owner workspace index of the root (`indexProofWorkspace`, already admitted in production). The legacy path keeps `rg --files -g`. The MCP bridge `glob` now uses it, so only `bash` keeps the `sh -lc` envelope.
- **Harness bugs found and fixed (test only).** The live test's recording wrapper used `Effect.tap(sync, () => handler)`, which answers `undefined`. It also stringified a void reply with `.slice`, which killed the release response. Those, not the product, caused the earlier "file read failed / terminal failed" conclusions from Devin. The wrapper now uses `andThen` and void-safe logging.
- **Live evidence (run).** A macOS production owner with a disposable non-root sshd in the Debian guest (port 42943). (1) Typed tools including glob passed twice. (2) Real Devin `3000.14.30821` ACP sessions passed 3 out of 3 runs. In each, `fs/read_text_file` returned the token through the owner; `terminal/create ls` ran as an approved U21 ACP shell lease, `wait_for_exit` gave exit 0, and `terminal/output` gave the real remote listing (`acp-input.txt`, `notes.md`); `terminal/release` completed; `fs/write_text_file` wrote `acp-output.txt` through the owner; and the agent replied with the token. The test checks the reply and the remote file content (`<token>-ok`). The guest sshd, scratch and keys were removed, and no agent, owner or remote process was left.
- **Checks.** Rust: shell tests 32 passed; `rustfmt --check` clean on the shell files; strict Clippy (macOS and Linux target) has no finding in this slice's files. Protocol: `verify-rust` 3 + 11; TS goldens 11. Server: 69 focused files, 303 passed and 10 live-gated skipped; `tsc --noEmit` clean; explicit-path `oxfmt`/`oxlint` clean; changed files at most 400 lines (`codec.ts` 393).

## U23 host-key algorithms and U22 on Windows (26 November 2026)

User decision U23 (plan §4). No commit was produced. The plan's §4 table was also fixed: the U22 row had landed in §6 Risks and now follows U21, with U23 after it.

- **Built.**
  - Multi-type host keys: `transport/host_key.rs`, `host_key/wire.rs`.
  - Multi-type pins: `trust_store/pin.rs`.
  - Pin-aware inspection and approval: `transport/trust.rs`.
  - Pinned `HostKeyAlgorithms`: `transport/command.rs`.
  - Config: `config/host_verification.rs`.
  - Simple-store limits raised to a 4 KiB record and 64 KiB in total.
  - The jump first-use path and the tests were adapted to the observed key set.
  - U22's user-auth list moved into cross-platform macros (`ssh_algorithms.rs`). Unix uses the full list; `windows_transport/inbox.rs` uses it without the `sk-*` forms.
- **Tests.**
  - Real throwaway public keys (ECDSA P-256/384/521, RSA 2048/1024) with fingerprints from `ssh-keygen -l`.
  - Wrong-type, DSA, truncated and trailing-byte refusals.
  - Multi-type keyscan ordering; pin set and algorithm ordering; inspection rules (only an unpinned type changed, a pinned type among several verified, first use challenges the preferred key, RSA launch argument).
  - Two trust-store cap cases rescaled to the new limits.
- **Live run (run).** OrbStack Debian 13 guest, disposable non-root sshd on port 42977 whose only host key was:
  1. **ECDSA P-256, empty `known_hosts`.** The owner challenged `ecdsa-sha2-nistp256` with the same SHA-256 fingerprint `ssh-keygen -l` printed. It approved and wrote an ECDSA pin, re-inspection was verified, and connect succeeded. The full receive suite (structured and alias targets) then passed 2/2 over the ECDSA pin.
  2. **RSA-3072 only, ECDSA pin kept.** `inspectTrust` returned `hostKeyChanged` and the pin was unchanged.
  3. **RSA, fresh `known_hosts`.** The challenge was `ssh-rsa` with the matching fingerprint, followed by approve, verified and connect. The receive suite passed 2/2 over the RSA pin.
  4. **The same RSA server restricted to `HostKeyAlgorithms ssh-rsa`, SHA-1 only.** Inspection still verified (same key), but a dialling operation failed as `transportLost`. sshd (DEBUG) logged our client's offer `rsa-sha2-512,rsa-sha2-256` and "no matching host key type", with no authentication.

  Guest cleaned up; no `git-receive-pack` or `git-upload-pack` was left.

- **Checks.**
  - `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner`: 72 + 583 (9 ignored) + 9 + 2 passed.
  - rustfmt check clean. Strict Clippy `--all-targets --keep-going` clean on macOS and `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`.
  - `--target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`: `cargo check --all-targets` passes. Clippy reports only two dead-code items outside this lane: `owner_grants.budget.rs:93 for_startup` and `owner_storage.rs:23 unhydrated`.
  - `verify-rust` 3 + 11. Server `tsc` 0 errors.
  - Focused Vitest (`src/git` plus the `src/ssh` config, target, codec, client, host-trust and dev-picker files): 125 files, 729 tests passed (3 files skipped: env-gated native tests).
  - oxfmt, oxlint and the structural checker (0 violations) on the new `sshOwnerProduction.hostKeyTypes.native.test.ts`.

## Linux cross-build lane and the blocked Linux runs (26 November 2026)

No wire number was used and no commit was produced. The activation flag is unchanged.

- **Guest capacity (user-approved).** In `bb-ssh-owner-linux-dev`, `/tmp/oc2-owner-proof` (1.4 GB of earlier OpenCode scratch) was deleted; nothing else was touched (`~/workspace-mode-proof*` kept). Afterwards: root btrfs 3.6–3.9 GB free (its quota still refuses writes to `/etc`), tmpfs `/tmp` 475 MB used of 13 GB, memory 12.8 GB available of 16 GB.
- **Cross-build toolchain (Mac).** `brew install zig` (Zig 0.17.1, `/opt/homebrew/bin/zig`), `cargo install --locked cargo-zigbuild@0.24.1` (`~/.cargo/bin/cargo-zigbuild`), and `rustup target add aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` (`x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` was already present) under the pinned 1.95.0 toolchain. `cargo zigbuild --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner --target aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu --tests --bins` builds in about 46 s; the test executables are streamed into a guest tmpfs directory and run there. This replaces in-guest `rustc` builds, which hit the cgroup OOM.
- **Linux-native owner suite (run).** It is now green on Debian 13 arm64: lib 72; binary 592 passed (6 ignored); `guarded_write_stage` 9; `windows_password_helper` 2. The helper test reads the Mac-absolute `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*` path baked in at compile time, so it runs inside a `bwrap` private root that binds the helper at that path (the guest file system is not changed). The first run found two defects:
  - **Product bug (fixed).** Debian `/bin/sh` is dash, and dash points an async list's fd 0 at `/dev/null` _before_ an explicit `<&0` applies. So the local provider guard (`owner_provider_lease/local_guard.rs`) starved its SSH client of stdin on a Linux initiator. The same pattern sat in the shared remote `transport/session_guard.sh`. It is harmless today, because provider leases run it under bash and the shell recipe passes `</dev/null`, but it is wrong for any `sh` caller. Both now save stdin on a spare descriptor first (`exec 4<&0` / `exec 9<&0`, closed again in the parent). Regressions: the existing `local_guard` stdio test (failed on Linux, passes now), plus a new `the_session_guard_keeps_the_callers_stdin_under_posix_sh` in `transport/shell.tests.rs`.
  - **Test race (fixed).** `owner_git_receive::tests::a_command_for_another_ref_is_refused_before_forwarding` failed only under whole-suite load. The refused command closes the socket before the pack is read, so the scripted client's pack write can race to `EPIPE`. The client now discards its write result explicitly; the accepted cases are still proven by their report-status assertions.
- **Linux-owner PTY live run (run).** `production_master_is_reused_and_the_login_pty_round_trips` and `production_agent_proxy_reuses_the_verified_master_and_confirms_exit` used the cross-built owner tests on the guest against a disposable non-root `bbdev` sshd on `127.0.0.1:42988`, with generated host and client keys and an explicit pin. Result: 3/3 (2 passed each round). These tests now accept the Linux fixture parent `/tmp` (macOS keeps `/private/tmp`).
- **Live port 22 (run).** A root `unshare -m -n` namespace held a loopback-only network, tmpfs `/run`, `/var/log` and a fresh `/sys`, and a disposable account `bbp22x` (uid 60022). The account existed only as bind-mounted copies of `passwd`/`group`/`shadow` inside that namespace, so the guest's `/etc` and real sshd were never touched. A disposable root sshd listened on `127.0.0.1:22` with its own config and keys. The same two production tests ran as `bbdev` through `setpriv` with port 22 and the bare-host pin form. Result: 3/3, with sshd logging `Accepted publickey for bbp22x … port 22`. The test admits port 22 only when `/sys/class/net` lists `lo` alone (a private network namespace); every other port must still be at least 1024.
- **Full T-backend deployment (run).** `deployment.ssh.owner.production.native.test.ts` ran with `BIGBUD_TEST_TBACKEND_DEPLOY=1` from the macOS production owner (debug, this tree) against a root sshd on `192.168.139.96:42990`. That sshd ran in a private mount namespace with a namespace-only account `bbtbe` (uid 60023) whose home was a fresh 2 GiB tmpfs. Relay node: the official `node-v25.2.1-linux-arm64.tar.xz` (SHA-256 `75f910b5…e0f6`, matching `SHASUMS256.txt`). Both tests passed 3/3 on a fresh `~/.bigbud`: deploy took 3.7–4.1 s and the 4 × 512 KiB relay 266–342 ms. sshd logged 132 accepted sessions. The step test assumes no prior completion record: a repeat without clearing `~/.bigbud` fails its `probeHost` regex on the `completed=` line, which is a test-ordering property, not a product fault. The sshd, account home, fixture and Mac keys were removed afterwards.
- **Checks.** `cargo fmt -p bigbud-ssh-owner -- --check` clean. Strict Clippy `--all-targets -D warnings` was clean on macOS, `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` and `--target aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu`. macOS `cargo test --locked -p bigbud-ssh-owner`: 72 + 584 (9 ignored) + 9 + 2 passed. Linux (cross-built, guest tmpfs): 72 + 592 (6 ignored) + 9 + 2 passed. Every changed file is at most 400 lines.
- **Cleanup.** No fixture sshd, owner, guard or account process remains on either host. Guest `/tmp/bb-xowner-*`, fixture roots and homes were removed. Mac scratch (`/tmp/bb-xrun-scripts`, `/tmp/bb-tbackend-dl`, keys) was removed.
- **T-peer (U9/U10).** Blocked here on where production pairing authority lives; decided as U24 (option B) and built the same day, see the next section.

## U24 production peer enrollment and relay (26 November 2026)

W2 T-peer U9/U10 under the U24 decision (option B: the owner holds the pairing authority). Activation flag unchanged; no commit. Wire numbers used: Request 130–136, Reply 76–79, ErrorCode 59–62; Request 137–139 and Reply 80 stay free.

- **Owner (Rust).** [`owner/peer_store.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/peer_store.rs) holds the one-time challenges (300 s, at most 8) and the pin store under the owner's private profile state. [`owner/backend.peer.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/owner/backend.peer.rs) runs the relay channels (owner-wide, at most 8, reaped on revoke and on owner retirement through `revoke_all_network_leases`). [`transport/peer_production.rs`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/crates/bigbud-ssh-owner/src/bin/ssh_owner/transport/peer_production.rs) reuses the dev pieces: the coordinator key provisioning (now a free `ensure_coordinator`), the fixed keyscan (`scan_endpoint`) and the pinned peer SSH base (`peer_ssh_base`, shared with the dev `pinned_peer_ssh`), with errors mapped to the U24 codes. The verbs are production-only and owner-wide: there is no target binding (`requires_target`) and no target storage (`needs_storage`, moved to `owner/dispatch.scope.rs` to keep `dispatch.rs` under 400 lines). A peer route dials the owner-resolved address with the address as its known-hosts label; the key is the trust anchor.
- **Grants.** Each verb is its own operand digest. Ordinary trust and peer grants never substitute in either direction: Rust `peer_grants_and_ordinary_trust_grants_are_not_substitutable` (owner grants), `verify-rust` `production_peer_grants_never_substitute_for_ordinary_trust` (reference model; dispatcher class only) and the TS grants test `production peer grants are distinct from trust, proof peer verbs and each other`.
- **Goldens.** New module [`v1.golden.peerProduction.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/protocol/ssh-owner/v1.golden.peerProduction.ts) (spread from `v1.golden.production.ts`; `v1.golden.cases.ts` untouched) and Rust `support/peer_production_vectors.rs` / `peer_production_cases.rs`: 8 requests, 5 replies and the 4 error frames, plus 8 grant digests. The verbs test lists the seven production names (they were already the frozen names of the proof peer verbs, plus `approve_peer_pin`, `revoke_peer_pin`, `peer_relay_status`, `peer_relay_close`) and now bounds fields to `≥ 10`, `≠ 127`, `< 19000`.
- **Server (TS).** Codec [`sshOwnerPickerProof.codec.peerProduction.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.codec.peerProduction.ts) (routed through the backend lease codec); client [`sshOwnerPickerProof.client.peerProduction.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/ssh/sshOwnerPickerProof.client.peerProduction.ts) exposed as `handle.peer`. Owner-wide requests wait for the in-flight exchange through `sshOwnerPickerProof.client.session.ownerWide.ts`, which OpenCode lease polls now share (one helper instead of two loops). [`peerCoordinator.owner.production.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/peer-orchestration/peerCoordinator.owner.production.ts) is composed in `peerCoordinator.layer.ts` under `SSH_OWNER_PRODUCTION_ACTIVATION` when no dev owner session exists. `peerPairing.ts` save approves the owner pin (only the scanned challenge, only for exactly the confirmed key) before writing the pairing; delete revokes it. A relay refused for a pin mismatch or a coordinator change becomes the existing typed setup outcome (`host-identity-mismatch`, `authentication-refused`); a missing pin is `authorization-refused`. No TypeScript `ssh-keygen` or `ssh-keyscan` launch exists (rows 49 and 50 stay deleted, no allowlist entry).
- **Live (run).** Coordinator: the macOS production owner (debug, this tree) started through the production client fixture. Worker: OrbStack Debian 13 arm64 `bb-ssh-owner-linux-dev`, **rootful**. A root sshd on `192.168.139.96:42995` ran in a private mount namespace with its own config and host key. Worker account `bbpeer` (uid 60025) existed only in that namespace (bind-mounted `passwd`/`group`/`shadow` copies) with a tmpfs home. Official Node 25.2.1 linux-arm64 (SHA-256-checked) and the relay helper were root-owned under `/tmp/bb-peer-fixture/bin`. The test appended the `restrict,command=…` line for the owner's coordinator key through an operator authorize script. The owner's root-UID image guard was unchanged. Two tests, 3/3:
  - [`peerCoordinator.production.peerEnrollment.test.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/peer-orchestration/peerCoordinator.production.peerEnrollment.test.ts): an idempotent key, a presented key matching its fingerprint, approval returning the exact host/port/user/fingerprints, a reused challenge giving `peerPinUnknown`, revoke, a second revoke giving `peerPinUnknown`, and a relay after revoke giving `peerPinUnknown`. Also 3 unit cases.
  - [`peerCoordinator.production.peerRelay.linux.test.ts`](/Users/youpele/DevWorld/bigbud/apps/server/src/peer-orchestration/peerCoordinator.production.peerRelay.linux.test.ts): scan, approve, then a 130 KiB half-closed relay through `makeOwnedPeerRelaySshChannels`. The worker's forced command saw exactly `[worker, grant, coordinator authority, coordinator fingerprint]` and echoed every byte. The outcome was `closed`. After revoke the same pairing is `authorization-refused`, and a confirmed key other than the scanned one is not approved.
  - The sshd logged 3 accepted publickey logins (one per relay run). No owner, sshd, Node or worker process remained; the fixture, account home and Mac scratch were removed.
